Program, texts and objectives: Status conferred by the degree.
The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering aims to prepare students for the profession of Civil Engineer. To this end, the course aims to train individuals with technical and scientific skills in the field of construction and civil infrastructure and in the field of territorial management, which fully meets the needs of the public and private professional world.
Due to the range of skills acquired, the civil engineer can find various job opportunities. Depending on the credits obtained and the different training paths followed (master's degree, master's degree, doctorate), they can work as a consulting engineer, design engineer, safety engineer, construction manager, testing engineer, or as a manager of construction companies, among many other professional fields.
The places of work where the civil engineer can offer their professional skills are:
design and structural engineering firms, construction companies, companies for monitoring and controlling structures, laboratories and research centers, and public and private bodies operating in the field of management of civil infrastructure and protection of the territory.
The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering aims to prepare students for the profession of Civil Engineer. To this end, the course aims to train individuals with technical and scientific skills in the field of construction and civil infrastructure and in the field of territorial management, which fully meets the needs of the public and private professional world.
- professional studios and design companies for works, plants and infrastructure;
- construction and maintenance companies for civil works, plants and civil infrastructure;
- universities, research institutions and higher education schools;
- public offices for the design, planning, management and control of urban and territorial systems;
- companies, organizations, consortia and agencies for the management and control of systems of works and services in the civil field;
- service companies for the study of the urban and territorial impact of infrastructure;
- companies, organizations and service companies, public offices, organizations, consortia and agencies for the survey and control of the territory.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering will conclude with a design, development or research activity, also carried out at companies, research institutions or external public administration bodies.
The final exam consists of writing a degree thesis, also written in English, relating to an original work carried out by the student, of an applied, design or experimental nature, developed under the guidance of a thesis supervisor designated by the Degree Course among its effective members, at the student's request, in which the candidate describes the activities carried out for the execution of the assigned work and its discussion (with the aid of a multimedia presentation) to a commission of University Professors, formed as described in the Didactic Regulations of the Course. The graduate student must demonstrate mastery of the topics covered, the ability to work independently, an aptitude for synthesis and communication skills.
Required knowledge for access.
To be admitted to the Master's Degree Course in Civil Engineering, the following are required:
1) possession of a three-year degree in the L-7 or L-23 classes (DM 270/04) and 04 or 08 (DM 509/99) which guarantees direct access to the Master's Degree Course;
or:
2) possession of a three-year degree or university diploma obtained throughout the national territory or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable and a minimum number of credits equal to 42 among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors attributable to basic s.d. sectors: MAT/03 GEOMETRY, MAT/05 MATHEMATICAL ANALYSIS, MAT/07 MATHEMATICAL PHYSICS, FIS/01 EXPERIMENTAL PHYSICS, CHIM/07 FUNDAMENTAL CHEMISTRY OF TECHNOLOGIES and a minimum number of 54 credits among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors, attributable to characterizing s.d. sectors: ICAR/01 HYDRAULICS, ICAR/04 ROADS, RAILWAYS AND AIRPORTS, ICAR/06 TOPOGRAPHY AND CARTOGRAPHY, ICAR/07 GEOTECHNICS, ICAR/08 STRUCTURAL ENGINEERING, ICAR/09 STRUCTURAL DESIGN, ING-IND/11 TECHNICAL PHYSICS, ICAR/10 TECHNICAL ARCHITECTURE, ICAR/20 URBAN PLANNING AND PLANNING. In the case of a number of credits lower than 42 but still at least equal to 36, also acquired in other basic s.d. sectors included among those indicated by the DM relating to class 08 (DM 509/99) or class L-7 (DM 270/04), admission is subject to the evaluation of the competent C.C.L. Similarly, in the case of a number of credits lower than 54 but still at least equal to 48, also acquired in other characterizing s.d. sectors included among those of class 08 (DM 509/99) or class L-7 (DM 270/04), admission is subject to the evaluation of the competent C.C.L.
The verification of the student's preparation (as per art. 6, paragraph 2 of DM 270/04) is carried out before enrollment with the methods indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the course of study.
In order to be admitted to the Course, a level of knowledge of the English language equal to B1 or equivalent is required.
Specific training objectives.
Training objectives: The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering aims to train a conscious and critical professional, equipped with the necessary technical and scientific knowledge to design, build and manage complex structures, in accordance with the most innovative methodologies of civil engineering, and also to play a leading role in the field of territorial protection. A second objective is to provide training aimed at continuing studies in master's and doctoral programs. The training course includes laboratory activities and seminars and tutorials, as well as external activities such as training internships at companies and laboratories, and study stays at other Italian and European universities, also within the framework of international agreements.
Educational path: The didactic path, understood as the natural continuation of the three-year path and therefore following a solid basic physical and mathematical preparation, provides for the acquisition of knowledge and skills of a professional and operational type in all the characterizing disciplines of Civil Engineering, and in particular for:
i) the design, execution, management and control of civil construction works, hydraulic works, infrastructure, geotechnical works;
ii) the planning, design and management of interventions for the protection and development of the territory, and in particular for the planning of transport systems and for the management and protection of water resources, as well as the survey in the territorial and urban area;
iii) the evaluation, planning, design in the civil field for the purpose of safeguarding against seismic and hydro-geological risks.
The course of study provides for four curricula, associated with different professional figures and employment opportunities:
1. Water and Environment
2. Transport Infrastructure
3. Structures and Seismic Protection
4. Sustainable Construction
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in CIVIL ENGINEERING Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding: Master's graduates in Civil Engineering will acquire a broad and solid knowledge of the techniques and criteria for the design, construction and management of civil and building works, with particular reference to structures, transport and hydraulic infrastructure, and building renovation; they will also acquire in-depth knowledge and understanding of plans and programs aimed at the development and protection of the natural and built environment.
These aspects will be addressed in particular through the disciplines of the characterizing SSDs, which will allow the student to confront engineering problems that involve a large-scale understanding of the interventions and, consequently, allow them to acquire the basic elements for a correct design and management approach. The master's graduate also acquires complementary and interdisciplinary knowledge, through the teachings in the SSDs identified as related, of the design and development of urban planning, the restoration and management of the architectural heritage.
These objectives will be achieved through a targeted and precise frontal teaching activity, the carrying out of exercises, the drafting of design elaborations and, finally, the development of the thesis. The knowledge and understanding skills acquired will be verified through in-progress tests and profit exams. The thesis work, on the other hand, will represent an exclusive moment, during which the student, through a continuous relationship with the thesis supervisor, will have the opportunity to synthesize the knowledge acquired and to deepen, with original ideas, his knowledge on specific topics of the training path.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding: The master's graduate is able to use the knowledge acquired through the teachings of the characterizing SSDs to successfully carry out the tasks of design, construction and management of complex engineering works in compliance with the technical and operational requirements, also refining their skills through the teachings of the related SSDs, with a view to completing their cultural baggage, in function of the chosen curricular path, with elements of seismic geotechnics, management of water resources, stability of slopes or architectural and building restoration.
This will be concretized through a strong presence, within the individual courses, of design exercises, in which the design sensitivity and flexibility of the solutions will be acquired also through the use of the most modern simulation and calculation software. The ability to apply the cultural baggage acquired will also be expressed through the use of the didactic laboratories. A continuous comparison with the teacher through joint revisions of the design choices made during the exercises will allow the students to acquire mastery of the proposed technical solutions and their feasibility. The verification of the acquired skills is carried out through in-progress tests, exercises (including those in the laboratory or involving the use of IT tools), periodic revisions of the projects, through discussion with the teacher; in profit exams, through written and oral tests and the presentation of the project and finally through the discussion of the degree thesis.
III - Autonomy of judgment: The master's graduate in Civil Engineering must be able to respond to the needs of the design of civil and building works according to an integrated approach, through which to evaluate the effectiveness of alternative hypotheses, based on the use of theoretical and experimental analysis models aimed at optimizing the choices, also from the point of view of both the costs and the benefits that can be obtained. The possession of adequate autonomy of judgment is therefore a fundamental requirement. The achievement of adequate critical skills is pursued through the drafting of elaborations to be carried out individually and/or in groups, laboratory activities, interdisciplinary seminars, aimed at evaluating the various design solutions in relation to the technical-functional, economic, social and environmental aspects, followed by relative verifications. The laboratory and internship activities are designed to stimulate the student's autonomy of judgment. This ability is verified in profit exams, in the evaluation of the elaborations relating to the experimental activities and in the evaluation of the final thesis, both by the supervisor during the development of the thesis and by the Thesis Committee during the presentation of the thesis.
IV - Communication skills: Civil engineering master's graduates will be able to carry out their professional activity also by working in teams within interdisciplinary contexts in both national and international contexts, always communicating with appropriate technical language. Written and oral communication skills are particularly stimulated in the drafting of design exercises which will also be set up as group activities and, in general, in the training activities that also involve the preparation of reports and written documents and the presentation, also with multimedia systems, of the same. In particular, all design activities will be accompanied by reports aimed at communicating and justifying the choices. The drafting of the reports, in addition to the graphic elaborations, will therefore represent a moment of synthesis in which to highlight the technical aspects and the motivations that have led to the final solution. The discussion in written form of the project will therefore be the tool through which the students will be stimulated to compare and, above all, to develop communication skills. Participation in internships and training internships provided for by the didactic regulations is also an important tool for the development of communication and relational skills. Finally, the drafting of the degree thesis may be a further opportunity to develop communication skills, especially when, for its development, external collaborations are used with companies or other national or foreign universities, or bodies that are part of the Advisory Board. The communication skills acquired will be evaluated through periodic checks of the projects and the written and oral profit exams as well as during the public discussion of the thesis which summarizes the work carried out.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN V - Ability to learn: All the didactic activities of the Course contribute to achieving adequate learning skills and methodologies by the graduate, which can allow him to operate effectively in the context of the specific problems of his field of competence in relation also to new technologies and the needs of the economic and productive system. This objective is achieved through the implementation of seminar activities and periodic exercises in the individual teachings. The ability to learn is also improved through a continuous updating of the multidisciplinary knowledge developed during the course of study, by consulting bibliographic material and databases available on the network. The verification of the learning skills takes place during the profit exams of the individual teachings. The development of the degree thesis will represent a further step for the achievement of these skills, as the student will have to be able to face new issues autonomously. The verification of the learning skills will be carried out both through the in-progress tests and the profit exams and during the interviews with the teacher during the period of preparation of the degree work.
Skills related to the function.
The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering aims to prepare students for the profession of Civil Engineer. To this end, the course aims to train individuals with technical and scientific skills in the field of construction and civil infrastructure and in the field of territorial management, which fully meets the needs of the public and private professional world.
The graduate in the Master's Degree in Civil Engineering has the skills to:
- design in seismic areas using innovative construction techniques to mitigate seismic risk;
- address complex geotechnical problems, such as the design of foundations and support works, the analysis of slope stability and the implementation of consolidation techniques for the protection of the territory;
- design and manage transport infrastructure (roads, railways, airports, subways, etc.);
- design and manage hydraulic works (aqueducts, sewers, etc.) and works for the protection and safeguarding of the territory (works for regulation and defense against water);
- evaluate the problems related to the territory, also with regard to the interaction with civil structures and, if necessary, intervene on it by designing works for safeguarding.
The Master's Degree in Civil Engineering aims to prepare students for the profession of Civil Engineer. To this end, the course aims to train individuals with technical and scientific skills in the field of construction and civil infrastructure and in the field of territorial management, which fully meets the needs of the public and private professional world.
The graduate has the adequate technical and scientific knowledge to carry out functions of:
- designer;
- construction manager;
- planner of interventions aimed at the development and protection of the territory;
- manager of territorial infrastructures such as roads, railways, airports, subways, aqueducts, sewers, works for regulation and defense against water, etc.;
- expert evaluator of the environmental compatibility of civil works.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2020.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian, English.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulations: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-72 - Class of Master's Degrees in Maritime and Navigation Sciences and Technologies.
Inter-university Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: ENGINEERING.
Website: https://scienze-e-logistica-trasporto-marittimo-aereo.cdl.unime.it/
Admission Requirements: Optional qualifications (choose from the following):
[L2+TSS] First-level degree, Upper Secondary School Diploma.
[L1+TSS] Degree, Upper Secondary School Diploma.
[TS] Foreign qualification, Upper Secondary School Diploma.
[TS+TSS] Foreign qualification, Upper Secondary School Diploma.
[LS+TSS] Upper Secondary School Diploma, Specialist Degree.
Student Mobility Manager: ADELE MARINO, VINCENZO CRUPI, PASQUALE CASTAGNO.
President: ADELE MARINO.
Coordinator: ADELE MARINO.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
MARITIME NAVIGATION SPECIALIST WITH MULTIDISCIPLINARY TRAINING. Master's degree graduates in Science and Logistics of Maritime and Air Transport can access various maritime professions, including those in the offshore and recreational boating sectors.
They can also participate in public competitions to find employment in public bodies (port system authorities, ministries, coast guard, European and international bodies) and research institutions, and can also work in shipping companies, transport, shipping and logistics companies.
Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance tests for training courses for secondary school teachers.
Furthermore, the Master's degree graduate acquires the skills that will allow them, if interested, to successfully complete their professional training within subsequent specific training courses to acquire the skills for Category A and Category B hydrographers.
AIR NAVIGATION SPECIALIST WITH MULTIDISCIPLINARY TRAINING. Master's degree graduates in Science and Logistics of Maritime and Air Transport can carry out activities related to the safety and security needs of airports.
They can also participate in public competitions to find employment in public bodies (ministries, EASA, ENAC, ENAV, European and international bodies) and research institutions, and can also work in airport management companies, handling companies, airlines, transport and logistics companies.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree consists of a public discussion of the thesis, relating to an original work carried out by the student under the guidance of a supervisor. The thesis should preferably be of an applied, design or experimental nature, from which the Commission can assess the cultural and scientific maturity as well as the quality of the work carried out.
Knowledge required for admission.
Admission to the Master's Degree Course in "Science and Logistics of Maritime and Air Transport" is open to students who hold a Bachelor's or three-year university degree obtained throughout the national territory or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
Holding a three-year degree in class L-28 (D.M. 270/04) obtained throughout the national territory guarantees direct access to the Master's Degree course.
Students who hold a three-year degree belonging to a class other than the one indicated, a three-year university diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, may be admitted provided they meet the following minimum curricular requirements:
45 CFU, including:
(a) 18 CFU in the field of mathematics and physics in the following SSDs of the basic activities: MAT/01-09, FIS/01-06
with the requirement that at least 9 CFU belong to the SSDs MAT/05 and MAT/07;
(b) 12 CFU in the field of engineering in the following SSDs of the characterizing activities: ING-IND/01, ING-IND/02, ING-IND/22, ICAR/05, ICAR/06;
(c) 9 CFU in the field of legal disciplines in the following SSDs: IUS/04, IUS/06, IUS/13, IUS/14;
(d) 6 CFU in the field of geological and geophysical disciplines in the following SSDs of the characterizing activities: GEO/02, GEO/03, GEO/04, GEO/10, GEO/11, GEO/12.
The verification of the student's preparation (as per Art. 6, paragraph 2 of D.M. 270/04) is carried out before enrollment, using the methods indicated in the didactic regulations of the study course. For the purposes of admission to the Course, a B1 level of knowledge of the English language is required.
Specific training objectives.
The aim is to provide graduates with solid theoretical and practical training, characterized by a multidisciplinary approach that will enable them to address and solve problems related to the processes and management of activities connected to logistics and the maritime and air transport sector.
The didactic structure is designed to enable students to acquire knowledge in the field of navigation, which will be gradually enriched by providing tools and opportunities for in-depth study, in order to acquire technical-specialistic and transversal skills, such as linguistic skills.
The study course, structured over two years, provides for a common path in the first year and two orientations that students can choose in the second year.
In the first year, the characterizing training activities are provided for the acquisition of knowledge and skills in the fields of navigation, logistics, remote sensing and oceanography, and the training activities present in the field of related disciplines for the acquisition of knowledge and skills on astronomy.
In the second year, the course develops through the provision of training activities in the characterizing disciplines for the acquisition of knowledge and skills required for the professions of offshore and recreational boating, and continues in the following two orientations:
a) naval, b) aeronautical.
In orientation a) "naval", themes related to hydrography and maritime navigation safety are explored in depth.
Particular emphasis is given to the skills for the training of a new professional figure: the expert hydrographer who acquires, processes and represents hydrographic data, through observations, sampling and monitoring of tides, currents and seabed morphology. The themes related to hydrography are developed in the training activities of the course and also in collaboration with experts from the Hydrographic Institute of Genoa through seminars, conferences, study visits.
Hydraulic systems for navigation, such as navigation locks, are also analyzed, which require the use of appropriate methods and technologies to support the design, in order to limit the risk of system inoperability.
The themes related to navigation safety are developed in the training activities of the course and also in collaboration with experts from the Navy and the Coast Guard through seminars, conferences, study visits.
In orientation b) "aeronautical", themes related to the world of air navigation and aeronautical construction will be explored in depth.
In particular, the legal disciplines develop the themes relating to: aircraft use contracts, commercial agreements between air operators, contractual relationships between carrier and passenger and in particular the protection that can be guaranteed to the latter, airport management and airport services, administrative organization of air navigation, safety and security of navigation.
Students are also encouraged to work in groups and acquire the knowledge and skills necessary to easily integrate into or, if already integrated, operate with greater preparation in the areas of work involved.
The activities of stages and internships are particularly important for the acquisition of these skills.
Dublin descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding. Knowledge and understanding are acquired both through personal study, also supported by targeted tutoring activities, and through the student's participation in: lectures, exercises, laboratory activities, seminar series, internships and traineeships at bodies or companies.
The knowledge and understanding, which enables the graduate to address and deepen independently the future technical-scientific developments of their sector, is acquired during the course of study through training activities in multidisciplinary areas, which include geological-geophysical, engineering and legal disciplines.
All Master's degree graduates acquire technical knowledge and understanding in the fields of navigation, logistics, remote sensing, oceanography and astronomy. They also acquire knowledge and skills required for the professions of offshore and recreational boating. Furthermore, depending on the orientation chosen by the student, the following are acquired:
- in the naval orientation, the skills in the sectors of hydrography and maritime navigation safety.
- in the aeronautical sector, the skills in sectors related to aeronautical construction and legal aspects of air navigation.
The achievement of learning outcomes is verified mainly through in-progress tests, laboratory tests, oral and written exams, oral presentations during the course and at the final exam. The learning checks are also aimed at evaluating the student's mastery of the scientific method of investigation and their ability to apply the knowledge and skills acquired for the analysis and interpretation of experimental data.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. The Master's degree graduate in "Science and Logistics of Maritime and Air Transport" is able to apply their knowledge to:
- identify, understand and solve real and practical problems arising from various scientific and technological areas of the logistics and navigation sectors, applying scientific methods of investigation in a critical and autonomous manner.
- develop scientific and technological innovation in the sectors of logistics and navigation.
- assume responsibility for the logistics and safety of navigation, ports and airports.
- manage personnel, coordinate work groups, operate independently and integrate quickly into work environments.
The achievement and verification of the ability to apply knowledge and understanding are developed mainly with the didactic tools (exercises, laboratory activities, classroom simulations, preparation of design works carried out mainly independently) and through collaborations with companies and research laboratories during the periods of internships and traineeships. The ability to apply the knowledge acquired by the student and the ability to present a topic of the course with appropriate technical language are further verified during the final thesis exam.
III - Autonomy of judgment. The possession of a high degree of autonomy of judgment is a fundamental requirement for the graduate in the Course, as they must be able to respond to the needs of maritime and air transport, evaluating all the technical, scientific and legal implications. The achievement of adequate critical skills is pursued through the preparation of individual and/or group works, laboratory activities, simulated tests on specific equipment, on-board experiences and interdisciplinary seminars, followed by related checks. The laboratory activities are carried out within the geological-geophysical and engineering disciplines and during the thesis period. The laboratory and internship activities are designed to stimulate the student's autonomy of judgment. This ability is verified during profit exams, in the evaluation of the elaborations relating to the experimental activities and in the evaluation of the final thesis, both by the supervisor during the preparation of the thesis and by the Examining Board during the presentation of the thesis.
IV - Communication skills. The objective pursued during the course of study is that the graduate is able to communicate with appropriate technical language, also in English, and is able to interact with experts in their field, even at an international level. Knowledge of the English language is provided through the training activities relating to further linguistic knowledge, which are provided at the beginning of the training course. The communication skill in technical language in English is also acquired through a training activity of the geological-geophysical disciplines, taught in English. The graduate acquires an adequate technical language both in the scientific and legal fields. Written and oral communication skills are particularly stimulated on the occasion of seminars, exercises and, in general, in the training activities that also involve the preparation of written reports and documents and the presentation, also with multimedia systems, of the same.
Participation in internships and training periods provided for by the didactic regulations is an important tool for the development of communication and relational skills. The acquisition of technical language is also facilitated by the interaction with external experts to the University both during the seminars and during the carrying out of the internship and training activities. The support activity of the CUST (University Center for Transport Studies of the University of Messina) is important for the organization of seminars and internships. Furthermore, through these activities, the student is motivated to develop the ability to work in a team, to coordinate various types of activities in the field of maritime and air transport, to transmit and disseminate technical information, highlighting problems and proposing solutions. The communication skills achieved by the student are also verified during the preparation of the degree thesis and during the public discussion of the thesis which summarizes the work carried out.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN, ENGLISH. V - Learning ability. All didactic activities contribute to achieving adequate learning ability on the part of the graduate. This objective is achieved through the implementation of seminar activities and periodic exercises in the individual courses. The ability to learn is also improved through continuous updating of the multidisciplinary knowledge developed during the course of study, through the consultation of bibliographic material and databases available on the network. The verification of learning abilities takes place during the profit exams of the individual courses and during the activities of internships and the preparation of the final thesis.
Skills related to the function.
MARITIME NAVIGATION SPECIALIST WITH MULTIDISCIPLINARY TRAINING. Master's degree graduates acquire skills in the fields of logistics, navigation sciences, naval engineering, hydrography, oceanography and navigation law. The study path provides transversal skills, which also take into account the legal aspects, in the field of logistics, goods management, shipping and import-export, sectors of activity in constant growth, in which an increasing demand is observed in the world of work.
In particular, they acquire the following skills:
- knowledge of the procedures for optimizing the transport of goods;
- knowledge of the legal and organizational models implemented by the main companies operating in the logistics and integrated logistics sector;
- knowledge of the bodies and procedures for a transport and logistics activity within the framework of international trade;
- mastery of the scientific method of investigation;
- management of personnel and coordination of work groups;
- advanced knowledge of software and experimental techniques for practical applications in the areas of interest;
- knowledge of sector regulations;
- mastery of technical terminology in English;
- preparation for undertaking subsequent specific third-level training courses (second-level master's degrees, doctoral research programs);
- preparation for participating in public or private selection procedures in the sectors of navigation and logistics.
In the study path, emphasis is given to the skills for the training of a new professional figure: the expert hydrographer who carries out measurements and descriptions of the physics of the marine environment in order to use them for various purposes, starting from the safety of navigation to the economy of the sea, the environment, research.
AIR NAVIGATION SPECIALIST WITH MULTIDISCIPLINARY TRAINING. Master's degree graduates acquire skills in the fields of logistics, navigation sciences, air navigation safety, oceanography and navigation law. The study path provides transversal skills, which also take into account the legal aspects, in the field of logistics, goods management, shipping and import-export, sectors of activity in constant growth, in which an increasing demand is observed in the world of work.
In particular, they acquire the following skills:
- knowledge of the procedures for optimizing the transport of goods;
- knowledge of the legal and organizational models implemented by the main companies operating in the logistics and integrated logistics sector;
- knowledge of the bodies and procedures for a transport and logistics activity within the framework of international trade;
- mastery of the scientific method of investigation;
- management of personnel and coordination of work groups;
- advanced knowledge of software and experimental techniques for practical applications in the areas of interest;
- knowledge of sector regulations;
- mastery of technical terminology in English;
- preparation for undertaking subsequent specific third-level training courses (second-level master's degrees, doctoral research programs);
- preparation for participating in public or private selection procedures in the sectors of navigation and logistics.
Function in a work context.
MARITIME NAVIGATION SPECIALIST WITH MULTIDISCIPLINARY TRAINING. The Master's degree graduate is able to operate independently and integrate quickly into work environments, assuming responsibilities for the logistics and safety of navigation in ports. They are able to plan, monitor and optimize activities related to logistics processes and goods management.
The graduate is able to carry out management and coordination tasks in various work contexts, performing various functions including:
- the acquisition, processing and representation of hydrographic data;
- the management and coordination of port terminals;
- the management and organization of the handling of goods and passengers on land;
- the carrying out of expert opinions in the context of legal proceedings;
- the carrying out of consultancy activities for public or private bodies;
- the carrying out of teaching and/or research activities in public and private institutions.
AIR NAVIGATION SPECIALIST WITH MULTIDISCIPLINARY TRAINING. The Master's degree graduate is able to operate independently and integrate quickly into work environments, assuming responsibilities for the logistics and safety of navigation in airports. They are able to plan, monitor and optimize activities related to logistics processes and goods management.
The graduate is able to carry out management and coordination tasks in various work contexts, performing various functions including:
- the management of air navigation flows in its various phases;
- the management and organization of the handling of goods and passengers on land;
- the carrying out of expert opinions in the context of legal proceedings;
- the carrying out of consultancy activities for public or private bodies;
- the carrying out of teaching and/or research activities in public and private institutions.

Department: ENGINEERING.
Website: https://lm-ingegneria-gestionale.cdl.unime.it/it.
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [TSS+L2].
Bachelor's Degree: [L1+TSS].
Degree: [TS+TSS].
Foreign qualification: [LS+TSS].
Specialist Degree.
Roles: President: MARIA FRANCESCA MILAZZO. Coordinator: MARIA FRANCESCA MILAZZO.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Management Engineer. The main employment and professional opportunities for graduates in Management Engineering are within small, medium and large companies in the manufacturing, automotive, energy, petrochemical and industrial sectors, and in the service sector, where qualified professionals are needed in the field of digitalization and sustainability.
The reference organizations are:
- manufacturing companies
- food industries
- mechanical and electromechanical industries
- transport industries
- processing industries
- companies and organizations for the production and conversion of energy
- petrochemical industries
- public and private services
- public administration
- research institutions
- design firms
- consulting companies
- freelance activities (after obtaining state exam certification).
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree consists of a public discussion of the thesis, relating to an original work carried out by the student. The thesis should preferably be of an applied, design or experimental nature, from which the Commission can evaluate the cultural and scientific maturity as well as the quality of the work carried out.
Knowledge required for admission.
Admission to the Master's Degree Course in Management Engineering is open to students who hold a bachelor's degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
Holding a three-year bachelor's degree in the L-9 - Industrial Engineering (ex D.M. 270/04) - or L-10 - Industrial Engineering (ex D.M. 509/99) - obtained throughout the national territory guarantees direct access to the Course of Studies.
Students who hold a three-year bachelor's degree belonging to a class other than those listed, a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, may be admitted provided they meet the following curricular requirements:
a) a minimum number of CFU equal to 18 among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors:
MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/07, SECS-S/01, SECS-S/02, FIS/01, FIS/03, CHIM/07, INF/01, ING-INF/05.
b) a minimum of 18 CFU among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors:
ING-IND/16, ING-IND/17, ING-IND/35, ING-INF/04, SECS-P/07, SECS-P/08, MAT/09, of which at least 12 must be in the characterizing sectors of the Master's Degree Class LM-31 (ING-IND/16, ING-IND/17, ING-IND/35, ING/INF/04).
The knowledge required for admission is identified, described and publicized in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
The verification of the student's preparation (as per Article 6, paragraph 2 of D.M. 270/04) is carried out, before enrollment, according to the methods indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course (CdSM) in Management Engineering (LM-31 class) aims to train Management Engineers capable of managing and optimizing the various areas/functions of public and private companies, through the application of technical-managerial approaches, capable of finding solutions to interdisciplinary problems related to the organization and management of processes and projects and of adopting innovative systems, effectively responding to current market needs and its rapid changes, in particular those related to digitalization and sustainability.
The specific training objectives of the CdSM, consistent with the training objectives that characterize the LM-31 degree class, contribute to acquiring knowledge and skills summarized in the following three areas of learning:
- area of management of production systems and projects: theoretical-scientific aspects of the management and optimization of processes and production systems including project management techniques and industrial risk analysis to identify, address and solve complex and interdisciplinary problems also concerning the broader concept of supply chain in the field of production/delivery of goods and services.
- area of strategy, organization and business management: theoretical-managerial aspects necessary for the design of organizational models, for understanding and managing innovation and technological change of product and process and for the financial management of the company.
- area of management of digital systems: technical-scientific aspects related to the development, design, management and control of technological and information systems of production/service companies including techniques for the automation of processes.
The training course is structured in a first year with courses mainly characterizing and a second year with courses for the most part divided into two curricula, called "Advanced Services" and "Sustainable Industry". In the first year, courses are offered to create a solid base of knowledge and skills in the three areas of learning. In particular, content is provided on organizational analysis and design, on models for optimizing complex systems, on sustainability management, on process automation, on project management, on innovation management, on smart cities, mobility and logistics and on industrial risk. In the second year, courses are offered to complete the knowledge and skills in the three areas of learning relating to both the service sector and the industrial sector. In particular, content is provided on production, logistics and supply chain management, on digital services for companies and public administration, on the application of business and finance models, on advanced information systems, on sustainable product development systems, on plant reliability and on technologies for managing environmental impacts.
In all disciplines, particular importance is given to the practical application of the knowledge acquired through appropriate techniques and tools. The courses include in-depth case studies and exercises, also through the use of computer tools and laboratory activities. The project activities carried out contribute to the development of the expected skills for the Management Engineer as well as to the improvement of soft skills (ability to work in a group, problem finding, problem solving, flexibility and relational skills).
Internships and orientation internships at public and private organizations, promoted by the Steering Committee, and the final exam complete the training, allowing the development of adequate critical and autonomous skills, written and oral communication skills also in a language of the European Union other than Italian (level B2) and the acquisition of cognitive tools suitable for the continuous updating of one's knowledge, awareness of one's professional responsibilities.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding. The Master's Degree Course (CdSM) in Management Engineering aims to acquire knowledge and understanding in three areas of learning ("Management of production systems and projects", "Strategy, organization and business management" and "Management of digital systems") attributable to the degree class of Management Engineering. In particular, the graduate acquires:
- theoretical-scientific knowledge and understanding of the management and optimization of processes and production systems, the management of logistics and the supply chain, project management and industrial risk analysis;
- theoretical-managerial knowledge and understanding of the design of organizational models, the management of innovation and technological change and the financial management of the company;
- technical-scientific knowledge and understanding of the development, design, management and control of information systems and techniques for the automation of processes.
The knowledge and understanding are acquired through a multidisciplinary approach, which will allow the graduate to face and deepen independently future developments in the field of Management Engineering.
The knowledge and understanding are acquired through participation in lectures, exercises, laboratory activities, seminars, personal and guided study, also through targeted tutoring activities and internship activities. The achievement of learning outcomes is verified mainly through oral and/or written exams and during the final exam.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. The graduate in Management Engineering will be able to apply the knowledge and understanding to the prediction, identification, analysis, evaluation, management and resolution of complex problems typical of Management Engineering using multidisciplinary analysis methods and techniques with a design approach oriented to digitalization and sustainability. The achievement of the ability to apply knowledge and understanding, and the verification of these skills, is developed mainly with experimental teaching tools, such as exercises, laboratory tests, classroom simulations, discussion of case studies, preparation, especially in an autonomous way, of design elaborations and internship activities.
The ability to apply the knowledge and understanding acquired by the student is also verified during the development of the elaboration and its discussion during the final exam.
III - Autonomy of judgment. The graduate in Management Engineering develops an ability to analyze complex problems that stems from the mental flexibility in integrating different aspects (technical, technological, managerial, etc.). The achievement of adequate critical skills is pursued through exercises in the classroom and in the laboratory, guided discussion (also in groups) aimed at stimulating the integration of acquired knowledge, also with specific bibliographic research or with the development of design activities, and to perfect the ability to critically compare different possible solutions. Personal elaborations and testimonies from the world of business and professions will offer the student further opportunities to develop their decision-making and judgment skills autonomously.
The verification of the acquisition of autonomy of judgment takes place during the profit exams, through discussion and critical re-elaboration of the different aspects of the disciplines, during the internship activities and during the preparation of the final elaboration.
IV - Communication skills. The graduate in Management Engineering is able to interact with experts in their own or other engineering fields and in general with other professional figures through the use of appropriate technical-scientific language. At the same time, he/she must be able to make non-specialist interlocutors understand the problems and application solutions proposed. He/she must also master an adequate level of knowledge of the English language both in understanding the sources and in communicating his/her ideas, also in writing. Written and oral communication skills are also perfected through participation in seminars, exercises, projects and, in general, in those training activities that involve the preparation of written reports and documents and their presentation also with multimedia systems. Participation in internships and study stays abroad are also useful tools for the development of the individual student's communication skills in a language of the European Union other than Italian (level B2).
The verification of the communication skills acquired by the students takes place mainly during the profit exams, both in written and oral form, in which the students will have the opportunity to express their ability to express concepts and application solutions. The final exam offers the student a further opportunity for in-depth study and verification of the skills of analysis, elaboration and communication of the work carried out.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN. V - Learning ability. The graduate in Management Engineering acquires during the studies an ability to learn continuously which will be of fundamental importance in the work and professional activity, as well as in the continuation of studies with advanced training courses (Doctorate and Master's). All the didactic activities contribute, to varying degrees, to achieving the objectives set in terms of learning ability, both through individual study and through group work in which each student has the opportunity to compare their learning skills with those of their colleagues.
The student's learning ability is verified at different times, that is, during the lessons, during the in-progress checks, or during the exercises, laboratory activities or design activities, through the presentation of information found and elaborated independently, or during the profit exams also by proposing specific questions that highlight the student's ability to use the knowledge acquired to solve complex problems or during the evaluation of the activity related to the final exam.
Competencies related to the function.
Management Engineer. In order to carry out the functions described above, the graduate in Management Engineering possesses the specific knowledge, skills and competencies listed below:
- methodologies and general criteria for the planning, design, control and management of production systems and projects.
- methodologies and criteria for the management of company and supply chain logistics.
- methods and criteria for the management of industrial production.
- criteria relating to organizational, managerial and design aspects in the engineering field.
- design and implementation of innovations.
- methods of financing projects.
- methods and technologies of digital systems for the processing of information, planning, management and control.
- models and methods for optimization.
- methods and techniques for the analysis and evaluation of technological resources for production.
- life cycle analysis of products.
- business models.
- smart city models.
- information processing and management systems.
- advanced and sustainable processes for the transformation of products.
- methodologies for the protection, performance and control, risk analysis, environmental impact and cost evaluation in industrial plants.
- methods for the definition and implementation of sustainable industrial processes.
- methods for the analysis of the reliability of materials.
The Master's degree graduate in Management Engineering also has the ability to learn and update continuously, the ability to collaborate and cooperate in a group, the ability to integrate the acquired knowledge, the ability to manage small or large work groups with a good level of autonomy and responsibility.
Function in a work context.
Management Engineer. The Master's degree graduate in Management Engineering represents a highly qualified and responsible figure in small/medium/large companies, both public and private, as well as in the service sector, or in freelance activities. He/she will be able to manage and optimize the various areas/functions, through the application of technical-managerial approaches, capable of finding solutions to interdisciplinary problems related to the organization and management of processes and projects and of adopting innovative systems, effectively responding to current market needs and its rapid changes, in particular those related to digitalization and sustainability. In this context, this figure will be able to carry out various functions such as:
- Project management.
- Production management.
- Safety and reliability management.
- Supply chain and logistics management.
- Business analysis and design.
- Digitalization management.
- Management of advanced and sustainable production systems.
- Development of business models.
- Research and development.

Specialist Degree
Roles: NICOLA DONATO (President), NICOLA DONATO (Coordinator)
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Biomedical Engineer. The acquired skills allow the biomedical engineer to operate in sectors ranging from services to design, to consulting, functional to the production, certification and use of medical devices, equipment and instruments for the healthcare sector, diagnostic equipment, surgical instruments, prostheses, biomedical implants, medical imaging systems, as well as the research and development sector, contributing to the implementation of new technologies, medical devices and therapies for the treatment of neurological disorders or to understanding the functioning of the human brain. The acquired skills also allow the biomedical engineer to find opportunities in private practice (after registration with the register of engineers), or in higher education programs (doctoral or master's degrees). The reference organizations fall within the public and private sectors, in the healthcare, industrial, service and research fields, such as:
- industries that produce and/or supply systems, devices, equipment for diagnosis, treatment and rehabilitation;
- specialized service companies in the management of equipment and medical plants in public and private healthcare facilities, in the world of sport, in other structures of the national health service;
- public or private hospitals;
- private and research institutions;
- private practice (after obtaining the necessary qualifications and registration with the register of engineers);
The skills and application areas can be summarized with the following profiles:
- Design engineer for diagnostic systems;
- Design engineer for assistive and rehabilitation devices;
- Neurorehabilitation engineer;
- Product specialist and certification engineer.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree consists of a public discussion of the thesis, relating to an original work carried out by the student. The thesis, under the guidance of a supervisor, should preferably be of an applied, design or experimental nature, from which the Commission can evaluate the cultural and scientific maturity as well as the quality of the work carried out.
Knowledge required for admission.
Admission to the Master's Degree Course in Bioengineering (Class LM-21) is open to students who hold a degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
Possession of a three-year degree in the classes L-8 - Information Engineering (ex D.M. 270/04) - or 09 - Information Engineering (ex D.M. 509/99) and L-9 - Industrial Engineering (ex D.M. 270/04) - or 10 - Industrial Engineering (ex D.M. 509/99) - obtained throughout the national territory guarantees direct access to the Course of Studies.
Students who hold a three-year degree belonging to a class other than those listed, a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, may be admitted provided they meet the following curricular requirements:
- A minimum number of credits equal to 18 among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: MAT/02, MAT/05, FIS/01, FIS/03, CHIM/07, INF/01, ING-INF/05, BIO/16, BIO/09.
- A minimum of 18 credits among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: ING-INF/06, ING-IND/34, ING-INF/07, ING-IND/12, ING-IND/22, ING-INF/04, ING-INF/01, ING-INF/03, ING-IND/31, of which at least 6 credits must be in the sectors characterizing the "Bioengineering" area of the Master's Degree Class LM-21 (ING-INF/06, ING-IND/34) or those indicated as such on the basis of D.M. 1649/2023 (ING-INF/07, ING-IND/12).
The knowledge required for admission is identified, described and publicized in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course. The verification of the student's preparation (as per art. 6, paragraph 2 of D.M. 270/04) is carried out, before enrollment, according to the methods indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course (CdSM) in Bioengineering (class LM-21) aims to train biomedical engineers, with solid methodological foundations and with high professional qualifications, capable of designing and developing products and systems in the area of Bioengineering in general, and with particular reference to solutions and technologies aimed at supporting the complex challenges related to the human brain and the nervous system. In this way, students will develop an interdisciplinary cultural training, closely linked on the one hand to the sectors of Information Engineering and Industrial Engineering, and on the other to the medical-biological and legal-regulatory sectors, which constitute the natural field of application and employment, represented by public and private actors and stakeholders. The specific training objectives of the CdSM, consistent with the training objectives that qualify the degree class LM-21, are related to the acquisition of the methodological and technological tools to identify, formulate and solve problems of biomedical engineering, even of high complexity, according to a systemic vision and an integrated and interdisciplinary approach relating to diagnostics, data management and interpretation, prevention, active aging, therapy and rehabilitation, design, experimentation, application, integration and management of biomedical technologies and devices and their interactions with biological systems, certification and marketing of medical devices and related regulatory sources, integrated enabling technologies (digital, sensor, robotic, etc.).
The specific training objectives of the CdSM are grouped into the following four areas of learning:
- Area 1 - "Methods and Technologies of Biomedical Engineering": acquire knowledge and develop skills on the aspects that characterize biomedical engineering, in the areas of signal processing, data analysis, instrumentation for diagnosis, for the therapy of pathologies and for functional rehabilitation, instruments for interfacing with the nervous system.
- Area 2 - "Physico-Medical and Legal-Regulatory": acquire knowledge about the operation in operating conditions, safety, certification and the legal-regulatory aspects of biomedical instrumentation. Develop skills in the design, operational functioning and compliance with international standards of biomedical instrumentation. Acquire knowledge and develop skills related to the functioning of the nervous system, its pathological alterations, and the technologies for monitoring and intervention, including medical imaging diagnostics.
- Area 3 - "Information Systems and Technologies": acquire knowledge and develop skills for the design of hardware/software interfaces for measurement, digitization, modeling and control, communication and data management for biomedical applications.
- Area 4 - "Biomechanical Systems and Technologies": acquire knowledge and develop skills on the operational functioning and the instruments for the measurement of mechanical quantities aimed at the characterization, design and development of biomechanical systems.
The characterizing teachings provided, framed in the SSDs of the disciplinary area "bioengineering" of the degree class LM-21, fall within the Area of learning 1, fundamental for acquiring knowledge and developing skills on the aspects that characterize biomedical engineering. The other characterizing teachings belong to the disciplinary area "biomedical disciplines", and, within the flexibility and interdisciplinarity of the CdSM, to the SSDs ING-INF/07 Electrical and Electronic Measurements and/or ING-IND/12 Mechanical and Thermal Measurements (identified as characterizing the CdSM in the disciplinary area "bioengineering") and are divided into the Learning Areas 2, 3 and 4. In particular, the SSDs of ING-INF/07 Electrical and Electronic Measurements and ING-IND/12 Mechanical and Thermal Measurements are identified as characterizing because they are considered essential for acquiring knowledge and developing skills on the advanced design and use of sensors and electrical and mechanical measurement systems for humans and their well-being, on the operation in operating conditions, on safety, on the certification and on the regulatory aspects of the instrumentation. In this way, in synergy with the SSDs already belonging to the areas that characterize the class, the cultural objectives of the class LM-21 are satisfied, declined among the training objectives of the CdSM and among the skills of the professional figure in output concerning sensor technologies, the classification of medical devices, the procedures for certification and marketing of medical devices, and the related regulatory sources.
The level of in-depth treatment of the topics covered during the training course therefore characterizes the Master's Degree graduate for a high technical-cultural preparation in the various fields of biomedical engineering, and gives him the ability to deal with complex problems, especially with an interdisciplinary approach, specifically aimed at innovation.
The CdSM lasts two years, and from the first year it is structured in a common path and two subsequent curricular paths, one related to neurobioengineering themes, and the other to themes of technologies and systems for human movement. Both the activities of the common path and those of the curricular paths are evenly distributed in both years of the CdSM. The common path is based on the themes of the aforementioned learning areas 1 and 2, the curricular paths, on themes falling within the learning areas 3 and 4.
In all disciplines, particular importance is given to the practical application of the knowledge acquired through adequate techniques and tools. The courses include in-depth case studies and exercises, also through the use of computer tools and laboratory activities.
The acquisition of knowledge and the development of technical-scientific skills, combined with medical-legal skills, will be extremely useful for identifying, formulating and solving, even in an innovative and interdisciplinary way, complex engineering problems and for interacting effectively with healthcare professionals, within the scope of their respective skills.
Project activities carried out in teams contribute to the development of the skills expected for the Biomedical Engineer as well as to the improvement of soft skills (ability to work in a group, problem-finding, problem-solving, flexibility and relational skills). Training and orientation internships with public and private organizations, promoted by the Steering Committee, and the final exam complete the training, also allowing the development of adequate critical and autonomous skills, written and oral communication skills also in a language other than Italian (level B2) and the acquisition of cognitive tools suitable for the continuous updating of one's knowledge and awareness of one's professional responsibilities.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding skills The Master's Degree Course (CdSM) in Bioengineering aims to train graduates with knowledge and understanding skills in four areas of learning ("Methods and Technologies of Biomedical Engineering", "Physico-Medical and Legal-Regulatory", "Information Systems and Technologies", "Biomechanical Systems and Technologies"). In particular, the graduate acquires knowledge and understanding skills:
- on the aspects that characterize the engineering solutions of biomedicine, signal processing, data analysis, instrumentation for diagnosis, for the therapy of pathologies and for functional rehabilitation, instruments for interfacing with the nervous system;
- on the advanced design, operation in operating conditions, safety, compliance with international standards and certification of biomedical instrumentation;
- on the legal aspects that regulate the public and private healthcare system;
- on the functioning of the nervous system, its pathological alterations, and the technologies for monitoring and intervention, including medical imaging diagnostics;
- on the design of hardware/software interfaces for measurement, digitization, modeling and control, communication and data management for biomedical applications;
- on the operational functioning and the instruments for the measurement of mechanical quantities aimed at the characterization, design and development of biomechanical systems.
The knowledge and understanding skills are acquired with a multidisciplinary approach, which will allow graduates to independently address and deepen future developments in the field of Bioengineering. These skills are achieved through participation in lectures, exercises, seminars, personal and guided study, also through targeted tutoring activities and internships. The achievement of the learning outcomes is verified mainly through oral and written exams, laboratory tests and during the final exam.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding The graduate in Bioengineering will be able to apply the knowledge and understanding to predict, identify, analyze, evaluate, manage and solve complex problems typical of Bioengineering using multidisciplinary analysis methods with a design approach oriented to the aspects relating to diagnostics, data management and interpretation, prevention, active aging, therapy and rehabilitation, design, experimentation, application, integration and management of biomedical technologies and devices and their interactions with biological systems, certification and marketing of medical devices and related regulatory sources, integrated enabling technologies (digital, sensor, robotic, etc.).
The achievement of the skills to apply knowledge and understanding, and the verification of these skills, is developed essentially with the experimental teaching tools, such as exercises, assisted laboratory activities, classroom simulations, discussion of cases, the preparation, independently or in groups, of design elaborations and internship activities. The ability to apply the knowledge acquired by the student is also verified during the development of the master's thesis and its discussion during the final exam.
III - Autonomy of judgment The Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering develops the ability to critically evaluate and solve complex engineering problems in an innovative and interdisciplinary way, interacting effectively with healthcare professionals, within the scope of their respective skills. The graduate will be able to evaluate the impact of the solutions adopted in the technical field (invasiveness, safety, compliance with current regulations, etc.) and reflect on the social and ethical responsibilities related to their skills.
The achievement of adequate critical skills is pursued through exercises in the classroom and in the laboratory, guided group discussions aimed at stimulating the integration of the knowledge acquired also with specific bibliographic research and improving the ability to critically compare different possible solutions. Personal elaborations and testimonies from the world of business and professions will offer the student further opportunities to develop their decision-making and judgment skills autonomously. The acquisition of autonomy of judgment is verified during the profit exams through discussion and critical re-elaboration of the various aspects of the discipline, during the internship activities and during the preparation of the final thesis.
IV - Communication skills The Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering develops the ability to collaborate, cooperate and manage small or large work groups with autonomy and responsibility, to write technical reports and reports, to promote and operate in a continuous "bioengineer-healthcare professional" interaction environment and to interact with expert specialists and non-specialists, with appropriate technical language.
He/she also knows how to fluently use, in written and oral form, at least one foreign language other than Italian, with reference also to the disciplinary lexicons. Written and oral communication skills are also improved through participation in seminars, exercises and, in general, in those training activities that involve the preparation of written reports and documents and their presentation also with multimedia systems. Participation in internships and study stays abroad are also particularly useful tools for developing the communication skills of the individual student in a language other than Italian (level B2). The verification of the communication skills acquired by the students takes place mainly during the profit exams, both in written and oral form, in which the students will have the opportunity to express their ability to express concepts and application solutions. The final exam offers the student a further opportunity for in-depth study and verification of the ability to analyze, elaborate and communicate the work carried out.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment: ITALIAN
V - Learning ability The Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering has the ability to learn and update continuously, also in a group, in a sector, the biomedical one, which is constantly evolving; he/she is therefore able to identify, select and consult technical and scientific literature on databases, institutional sources and specialist sites.
All the didactic activities contribute, to varying degrees, to achieving the objectives set in terms of learning ability, both through individual study and through group work in which each student has the opportunity to compare their learning skills with those of their colleagues. There are several moments of verification of the student's learning skills during the lessons and exercises, during the in-progress tests, the laboratory activities and the preparation of design elaborations, through the presentation of autonomously obtained data, or during the evaluation of the activity related to the final exam.
Competencies related to the function.
Biomedical Engineer In order to carry out the functions described above, the Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering possesses a set of knowledge, skills and competencies summarized below:
- in the field of the main techniques for the measurement, processing, treatment, communication and management of biomedical data and signals aimed at the development of medical instrumentation for diagnosis/therapy/rehabilitation both in general and in particular for neurological pathologies;
- on the instruments and systems for interfacing with the nervous system;
- in the context of the reliability, quality and certification of biomedical devices;
- on the legal aspects that regulate the public and private healthcare system;
- in the field of robotics and biomechanics for the development of innovative technological systems in the biomedical field;
- to use fluently, in written and oral form, at least one foreign language other than Italian, with reference also to the disciplinary lexicons.
The Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering also has the ability to learn and update continuously, the ability to collaborate and cooperate in a group, the ability to integrate the acquired knowledge, the ability to manage small or large interdisciplinary work groups with a good level of autonomy and responsibility.
The plurality of skills of the graduate in Bioengineering is such as to allow a fruitful dialogue between the various actors in the biomedical sector, positioning himself as a multifaceted professional figure capable of facing the complex problems of the biomedical sector, in which, in addition to the knowledge and methodologies of engineering, the ability to face the typical problems of the clinical healthcare field is also required. The specific skills acquired will therefore allow the Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering to apply the advanced techniques of bioengineering, adapting easily to the continuous evolution of technologies and to the changing needs of the production and healthcare sectors.
Function in a work context.
Biomedical Engineer The figure of the Master's Degree graduate in Bioengineering coming from the LM-21 Master's Degree Course in Bioengineering masters a set of technologies and methods common to the application sectors of Biomedical Engineering, and is able to operate in public or private organizations, research, industrial and healthcare assistance for planning, advanced design, development, installation, testing, management, control and maintenance of equipment and systems dedicated to specific therapeutic treatments or equipment and systems for diagnosis and acquisition, storage and processing of data of various kinds. He/she may also be involved in verification and evaluation of the technological appropriateness of projects and products, as well as training and assistance in the use of medical devices and diagnostics, interacting effectively with healthcare professionals, within the scope of their respective skills. In addition to these, there are specific functions on the design and management of systems for the analysis of the functioning of physiological systems and of man-device interfaces to support new treatments for various pathologies, with particular reference to the nervous system and neurological pathologies, and on the development of technologies aimed at improving the quality of life of people affected by various disabilities with particular attention to sensory and/or motor deficits.

[1081R] BIOENGINEERING Master's Degree Course NEUROBIOENGINEERING CURRICULUM

-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina

Roles:
President: MONICA MILASI.
