Program, texts and objectives: Status conferred by the degree.
Quantitative economics expert. The skills acquired during the studies will allow graduates to apply for jobs in the public administration (central and local), statistical agencies, private and public research centers, consulting and professional services companies.
Quantitative finance expert. The skills acquired during the studies will allow graduates to apply for jobs in companies, banks, financial companies and SGR, international financial institutions and central banks.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam consists of the preparation, under the supervision of a lecturer, of an original written paper on topics related to one of the courses taken by the student during his/her master's degree program.
To pass the final exam, the student must demonstrate that he/she has acquired, in addition to an advanced knowledge of the chosen topic, also an autonomous ability to learn, understand and research; the ability to correctly use the literature (including international literature); the ability to manage, analyze and present data; the ability to expose and support scientific arguments in a logical and coherent way, demonstrating maturity in the analysis, synthesis and organization of the topics covered.
Knowledge required for admission.
To be admitted to the Master's Degree in Quantitative Methods for Economics and Finance, the following curricular requirements must be met:
a) possession of a three-year degree in the classes L-18, L-33 or L-41.
b') knowledge of the English language at least at the B1 level for students who choose the Quantitative Methods for Economics curriculum, taught in Italian.
b'') knowledge of the English language at least at the B2 level for students who choose the Quantitative Methods for Finance curriculum, taught in English.
For applications from candidates with degrees other than those mentioned above, reference will be made to the acquisition in the previous curriculum of CFU in the following four disciplinary areas: business, economics, law, mathematics and statistics.
In any case, pursuant to current regulations, enrollment in master's degree courses is not allowed for graduates to whom a training debt is recognized on the access qualification. The graduate to whom the CdS recognizes a training debt will not be able to enroll in the master's degree course but must acquire the relevant credits before enrollment by enrolling in individual courses.
The verification of the adequacy of the student's personal preparation in possession of the curricular requirements, as well as that of the knowledge of the English language, is mandatory and is carried out, after pre-registration, by a special Commission appointed within the CdS Council.
The regulations of the CdS may provide for the elements considered in the verification of personal preparation, including the achievement of a certain score in the achievement of the first-level degree and the possession of English language certifications issued by bodies recognized by the Ministry of Education.
The operational methods for verifying personal preparation will be defined within the CdS's didactic regulations in accordance with what is provided for in the University's didactic regulations.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree course in Quantitative Methods for Economics and Finance (MQEF), class LM-56 (Economics), in line with the training objectives of the class, aims to train experts in quantitative economics and quantitative finance.
The student of the MQEF course will acquire advanced knowledge in quantitative methodologies, which he/she will be able to apply to economics and finance, within the scope of economic analysis and financial choices of companies and public administrations.
Attention is paid to the tools necessary for the correct measurement, understanding and management of sustainability dimensions, considering the related risks and opportunities, both at the level of a single institution and at the economic/financial system level.
The theoretical skills acquired will be supplemented by the deepening of digital skills which are an integral part of the data analysis processes underlying strategic public and corporate decisions. In fact, an important enrichment for the student's training concerns the use of statistical software most used in the world of research and companies and of economic databases, both at the macro and micro level.
The training course, structured in two academic years, includes courses in the economic field (24-36 CFU), mathematical and statistical (24-36 CFU) which provide a solid basis for the analysis of the economic system, to be applied to decisions and evaluations of choices in economics and finance, accompanied by a selection of business disciplines relating to the financial system and the companies operating in it (12-24 CFU), as well as courses in the legal field relating to the regulation of the economic system (6-12 CFU). The characterizing courses will be supplemented by related and integrative courses (12-24 CFU) with the aim of acquiring further specific skills in the applied, quantitative and digital fields.
More specifically, the training course will be organized as follows in the different training areas:
-Economic area, which includes courses aimed at framing and evaluating economic and regulatory policies and the macroeconomic context in which economic and financial analysts operate, with a long-term perspective and particular attention to sustainability aspects (chosen from the SECS-P/01, SECS-P/02, SECS-P/03, SECS-P/05, SECS-P/06, SECS-P/12 sectors);
-Mathematical-statistical area, which includes courses dedicated to the use of statistical models, especially in the economic and financial field, but also in the evaluation and demographic dynamics (chosen from the MAT/06, SECS-S/01, SECS-S/03, SECS-S/04, SECS-S/05, SECS-S/06 sectors);
-Business area, which includes courses relevant to the financial management of companies, especially in the field of financial intermediaries (chosen from the SECS-P/07, SECS-P/08, SECS-P/11 sectors).
-Legal area, where the fundamental contribution of analysis of the regulatory context and regulation of the economic system is inserted, and especially of the markets, with an international focus, with one or more courses framed in the IUS/04, IUS/05, IUS/10, IUS/13 sectors.
Dublin descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding skills The student of the MQEF course will acquire advanced knowledge in quantitative methodologies which he/she will be able to apply to economics and finance, within the scope of economic analysis and financial choices of companies and public administrations.
The courses in the economic field will allow the student to acquire advanced knowledge for the analysis of the economic system, while those in the statistical-mathematical field will provide skills for the decisions and evaluations of choices in economics and finance. With the courses in the business field, the student will acquire knowledge and develop understanding skills relating to the financial system and the companies operating in it; finally, the courses in the legal field will allow the student to understand the regulation of the economic system.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding The theoretical and practical knowledge acquired and the analysis of case studies will allow the student to:
- analyze and interpret the dynamics of the economic system
- apply the principles of economic theory to real case studies
- analyze the effects of the decisions of economic operators
- evaluate the impact of the direct and indirect effects generated by the activities of a company or a public administration
- use the quantitative tools necessary to carry out empirical economic analyses in a rigorous manner
- integrate economic and quantitative knowledge in order to define scenarios and prospects in the financial field
- support and evaluate decision-making processes by using mathematical, statistical and econometric modeling
- design tools, including innovative ones, for economic and statistical analyses both descriptive and predictive of complex phenomena
- develop critical skills in the selection of sources and data, evaluating their quality These skills will be acquired through the study of practical cases and exercises with practical-operational skills relating to the collection and processing of data relevant to economic and financial analysis in its various application aspects, also in support of judgments that include reflection on socio-economic phenomena. To this end, the graduate must demonstrate at the end of the training course that he/she is able to relate and discuss clearly on case studies with a theoretical-practical content, adopting an adequate technical language which, if necessary, requires mastery of the English language.
The verification of the acquisition of the ability to apply knowledge will take place through practical examination tests, such as statistical-econometric analysis conducted on specific datasets and, with the discussion of the final exam, during which the graduate must also demonstrate the acquired ability to analyze and solve concrete problems.
III - Autonomy of judgment The MQEF master's degree graduates will acquire advanced skills and competencies relating to:
- Autonomous skills for critical analysis of economic and financial processes.
- Ability to select the most appropriate statistical sources for the study of economic and financial problems.
- Ability to critically reflect on the functioning of economic-social and financial systems.
- Ability to evaluate the consequences of the economic and financial activities of companies and public administration.
IV - Communication skills At the end of the course of study, the MQEF master's degree graduates will be able to:
- Communicate accurately information, ideas, problems and solutions in written and oral form.
- Interact in the development of group projects.
- Use in an advanced way the language of economic and quantitative disciplines.
- Write and present informative or technical reports, also in English.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN, ENGLISH V - Learning skills The MQEF master's degree course will provide the student with the advanced and specialized skills useful for:
- Analyzing autonomously data useful for economic and financial analysis.
- Understanding specialist texts in English.
- Using autonomously the theoretical notions for the solution of empirical problems.
- Working flexibly thanks to interdisciplinary knowledge.
- Consulting databases and retrieving bibliographic documentation on topics of interest.
Skills related to the function.
Quantitative economics expert The graduate will acquire knowledge, skills and competencies relating to:
- advanced aspects of economic theory
- macroeconomic context in which economic analysts operate
- themes of financial management of companies
- fundamental concepts of risk management
- advanced statistical-mathematical tools for economic analysis
- main theoretical models to support economic decisions
- techniques for managing statistical datasets
- problems of estimation and forecasting in the economic and demographic field
- models for the evaluation of economic policies
- fundamentals of the regulations governing the economic system
- sustainability aspects of the economic system Quantitative finance expert The graduate will acquire knowledge, skills and competencies relating to:
- basics of economic theory
- macroeconomic context in which financial analysts operate
- themes of financial management of companies
- fundamental concepts of risk management
- advanced statistical-mathematical tools for financial analysis and for the evaluation of policies and services
- techniques for managing financial datasets
- main models for the evaluation of risks
- fundamentals of the regulations governing the economic and financial system Function in the work context.
Quantitative economics expert The theoretical and practical knowledge acquired and the analysis of case studies will allow the graduates of the Master's Degree course in Quantitative Methods for Economics and Finance, Quantitative Methods for Economics curriculum, to carry out management functions in various work contexts, public and private, both national and international.
In particular, the master's graduate will be able to carry out the functions of quantitative economist, public official in the economic field, consultant and expert in the economic field.
The main tasks will be:
- carry out quantitative analyses in the field of economics;
- operate effectively in the management of the economic-quantitative aspects of public administrations and companies, also in supranational contexts.
- analyze and interpret the dynamics of the economic system
- relate the observed dynamics to the theoretical models studied
- analyze the effects of the decisions of economic operators
- apply the principles of economic theory to real case studies
- evaluate the impact of the direct and indirect effects generated by the activities of a company or a public administration Quantitative finance expert The theoretical and practical knowledge acquired and the analysis of case studies will allow the graduates of the Master's Degree course in Quantitative Methods for Economics and Finance, Quantitative Methods for Finance curriculum, to carry out management functions in various work contexts, public and private, both national and international.
In particular, the master's graduate will be able to carry out the functions of financial analyst, portfolio manager, risk manager, financial consultant, financial broker, and more generally expert in the economic-financial field.
The main tasks will be:
- use quantitative tools to carry out empirical economic analyses
- integrate economic and quantitative knowledge in order to define scenarios and prospects in the financial field
- support and evaluate decision-making processes by using mathematical, statistical and econometric modeling
- design tools, including innovative ones, for economic and statistical analyses both descriptive and predictive of complex phenomena
- select the sources and data to be used in the analyses, evaluating their quality.

[1298] BUSINESS CONSULTING AND MANAGEMENT Master's Degree Inter-university English Italian

-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [1298] BUSINESS CONSULTING AND MANAGEMENT
TYPE: Master's Degree
CURRICULUM: Inter-University
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS

Program: 2018
Status: Open

Language: Italian, English
Type: Master's Degree
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004
Degree Class: LM-77 - Master's Degree in Business and Economics

Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]

Bachelor's Degree [L1+TSS]
Degree [TS+TSS]

Student Mobility Manager: ELVIRA TIZIANA LA ROCCA

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
HIGHLY QUALIFIED BUSINESS CONSULTANT: Holder of professional studies, responsible positions in business consulting companies.
BUSINESS FUNCTION MANAGER: Private, public and non-profit companies, of any size and sector.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists in preparing a written dissertation, developed by the candidate under the supervision of a lecturer of the Course, and its subsequent public discussion. The dissertation must demonstrate critical skills and contain elements of originality.
The overall final assessment also takes into account the individual curriculum and the assessments obtained by the student and, in cases of integration with the internship activity, the assessment of the same.
Knowledge required for access:
REQUIRED KNOWLEDGE FOR ACCESS: In addition to holding a three-year or four-year degree or a three-year university diploma, or another degree obtained abroad recognized as suitable, the student must possess the curricular requirements for admission to the course and adequate personal preparation.
Curricular requirements: The minimum curricular requirements are defined in the didactic regulations of the course of studies in terms of the number of CFU obtained in specific scientific-disciplinary sectors (or equivalent areas if the degree was obtained abroad).
In particular, for the minimum curricular requirements, at least 104 credits must have been obtained in the four disciplinary areas:
- business area (SECS-P/07; SECS-P/08; SECS-P/09; SECS-P/10; SECS-P/11; SECS-P/13; AGR/01) 34 CFU;
- economic area (SECS-P/01; SECS-P/02; SECS-P/03; SECS-P/06; SECS-P/12; MGGR/01; MGGR/02) 24 CFU;
- legal area (IUS/01; IUS/04; IUS/05; IUS/06; IUS/07; IUS/09; IUS/10; IUS/12; IUS/14) 24 CFU;
- mathematical-statistical area (SECS-S/01; SECS-S/03; SECS-S/04; SECS-S/05; SECS-S/06; MAT/05; MAT/06; INF/01; INF/05) 22 CFU.
The Committee will verify the possession of the requirements for equivalent areas, in the event that the three-year or four-year degree or the three-year university diploma was obtained abroad.
Enrollment with outstanding credits is not allowed. If the student does not have the minimum number of credits required for the different disciplinary areas, they must obtain them before enrolling, by enrolling in individual courses, at this or another University (individual courses can only be at the undergraduate level) and pass the relevant exam before enrolling in the Master's degree course.
Personal preparation: The didactic regulations of the course of studies also define that adequate personal preparation of the student is necessary for access to the Master's degree course, in order to ensure that the student possesses, independently of the formal verification in terms of credits, the skills and knowledge necessary to successfully follow the Master's degree courses.
METHODS FOR VERIFYING THE POSSESSION OF SUCH KNOWLEDGE: For the purposes of verifying the possession of the minimum curricular requirements, in addition to the CFU obtained in the three-year or four-year degree or university diploma, or another degree obtained abroad recognized as suitable, the CFU obtained in previous Master's degrees, four-year degrees and university master's degrees, resulting from adequate certification, can also be used. Personal preparation is verified through the assessment of the required skills and knowledge, through analysis of the student's curriculum, possibly supplemented by an interview by the Teaching Committee of the Department. In any case, the personal preparation of the graduate who has the necessary curricular requirements is declared positive whenever the graduate has obtained a degree mark, of the first level or of the pre-509/99 system, not lower than 95/110 (or equivalent grade if the degree was obtained abroad). If the student does not have the minimum degree mark, they must undergo verification of personal preparation through an oral interview.
The knowledge of the English language (at a level equal to at least B1) and good IT skills, certified by passing university exams or aptitude tests, or by certifications recognized at European or international level (e.g. ECDL, Trinity College London, University of Cambridge Local Examination Syndicate, etc.) are also required.
Specific training objectives: The Master's degree in "Business Consulting and Management" aims to train highly qualified professionals, with a Master's level competence in the field of accounting professions and business consulting as well as in the administration of companies. On the front of business administration, the graduates of the "Business Consulting and Management" Master's degree will be able to support companies and public administrations, both externally and internally, in the activity of financial planning, budgeting and accounting auditing, economic-financial control of management.
The specific training objectives of the course are functional to the acquisition of skills that allow the graduate to provide advanced consulting in the field of:
- design of company information systems;
- company accounting;
- economic-financial evaluations in extraordinary operations;
- legal control of accounts on the accounting and financial statements of private and public companies; - control of company performance;
- economic, financial and managerial consulting to organizations, companies and individuals, on both national and international scales, also for the management of corporate crises;
- economic-financial analyses to support transfer pricing choices;
- compliance checks (which also include verification of quality control procedures);
- planning, organization and management of internal auditing processes.
The professional figures trained will be able to manage the main company functions, in the various reference contexts, both through full mastery of analysis techniques and knowledge for supporting decision-making processes, and by coordinating and organizing operational units and, finally, in external functions to the organization as business consultants and professionals authorized to practice the economic-commercial profession.
The Master's Degree Course in "Business Consulting and Management" mainly welcomes students from degrees with an economic-business orientation, as well as students from degrees with an economic-political and economic-legal orientation. The Master's Degree in "Business Consulting and Management", therefore, is aimed at those who intend to complete their university studies, choosing a second-level course capable of ensuring the acquisition of specific skills in the business field, with a high qualification profile both at a consulting and professional level (laying a solid cultural foundation for the exercise of the profession of Chartered Accountant), and at a managerial level.
The training course is organized in such a way that students deepen at a Master's level the specialized topics of an economic-business nature (company valuations and extraordinary operations; financial control; accounting and auditing principles; business plan and company creation; social and environmental responsibility of companies), legal, statistical-quantitative and economic, with particular attention on the latter front to the themes of public economics.
Interdisciplinary laboratories and an adequate internship in companies are planned. The thesis, which constitutes the final moment of the training course, is an important moment of in-depth study, also theoretical-conceptual, on the topics studied in the Course.
In addition, the Master's degree course in "Business Consulting and Management" has a curriculum in which some courses are taught in English, organized to respond to the growing demand for graduates who are able to take on operational and managerial roles in companies that also operate in international contexts.
Dublin descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding: Master's graduates in Business Consulting and Management possess high and articulated skills in the business and management field, combined with knowledge of the specific areas of the legal, economic and statistical-mathematical field that appear particularly connected to the expected employment opportunities.
In particular, they:
- have the ability to interpret the overall process of value creation in companies of various types and purposes, thanks to in-depth knowledge of analysis models and business management techniques in the various functional areas of specialization;
- have in-depth knowledge in the field of business administration;
- have in-depth knowledge of information systems and their relationship with the organization and administration of the company;
- have in-depth knowledge of the preparation of business plans and the conduct of simulation analyses;
- know the issues and methodologies relating to the economic-quantitative determinations carried out in extraordinary operations;
- deepen the macroeconomic issues related to the analysis of public policies;
- acquire knowledge and develop understanding of legal regulations;
- acquire knowledge and develop understanding of the specific areas of the statistical-mathematical field.
At the end of the course of study, the graduates will also have acquired advanced knowledge and skills in business studies, demonstrating an ability to understand suitable for developing and/or applying original ideas even within a research context relating to business and management studies.
The methods used for the transfer and acquisition of knowledge and skills are: lectures and participatory lessons, seminars, testimonials and discussion of case studies.
Verification of learning outcomes, knowledge and skills is carried out through: tests, questionnaires, exercises to be carried out, individual or group reports and final exam tests expressed in thirtieths.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding: The Master's graduate in Business Consulting and Management knows how to apply the analytical and conceptual tools of business disciplines, with integration of economic, mathematical-statistical and legal concepts and tools.
In particular, they:
- know how to prepare the financial statements by applying national and international accounting principles;
- know how to carry out the audit of the financial statements;
- are able to contribute to the design of company information systems, even advanced ones;
- know how to estimate the value of companies;
- know how to prepare business plans and conduct simulation analyses;
- know how to carry out the economic-quantitative determinations in extraordinary operations;
- know how to provide global financial consulting to the company and the entrepreneur (family business);
- know how to provide economic-financial consulting on extraordinary company operations (mergers, spin-offs, transfers, etc.);
- know how to develop economic-financial analyses to support transfer pricing;
- know how to develop interdisciplinary analyses of the main problems of business management in the various sectors;
- know how to formulate decision-making alternatives, integrating economic-business, economic-general, legal and quantitative knowledge;
- know how to design the structure and operating systems of organizations, proposing different alternatives and highlighting their benefits;
- know how to coordinate the activity of organizational systems, even complex ones, integrating into the processes of resource management;
- know how to design and coordinate management systems in the various areas of functional specializations, mastering the knowledge of management models and techniques;
- know how to address business management problems with an integrated and interdisciplinary approach;
- know how to select, use and interpret sources of various types, in order to offer a critical and original reading of complex business phenomena;
- know how to carry out advanced economic and financial calculations;
- are able to grasp the impact of public policies on the conditions of existence of companies, evaluating their relevance in the business decision-making process;
- are able to identify and apply the regulations that govern business activity;
- are able to autonomously apply the analytical and conceptual tools of the mathematical-statistical disciplines for company valuations.
The methods used to promote the application of knowledge and skills are: study and working groups, practical exercises and internship activities.
Verification of results is carried out through: individual or group reports, development of projects and, finally, the preparation of the thesis.
III - Autonomy of judgment: Master's graduates in "Business Consulting and Management" are able to:
- solve recurring professional problems in the context of business life and in the consulting activities provided for by the regulations of the accounting profession and the performance of managerial/operational roles;
- select and evaluate decision-making alternatives to propose correct management choices, integrating economic-business, economic-general, legal and quantitative knowledge;
- integrate economic-business and managerial knowledge with each other and in an interdisciplinary perspective;
- integrate into complex and articulated decision-making processes, working in teams and groups, contributing to the overall critical reflection and contributing to the formation of shared and conscious decisions;
- take full autonomy and full responsibility for the decisions of the professional figure (managerial) trained.
The graduate therefore:
- will have excellent analytical skills and awareness of their own abilities;
- will have the ability to plan their own work and that of others;
- will be able to coordinate work groups, report on their work activity, motivate other people;
- will have acquired a considerable level of problem-solving and decision-making skills applied to very complex business situations in which objectives, constraints, levers and scarce information are provided;
- will know how to use appropriate techniques and methods of economic evaluation, also based on statistical or accounting models.
The objectives are pursued through the inclusion, in the various training activities (classroom and laboratory teaching activities, internship, preparation for the final test), of case studies and moments of critical reflection on problems, simulated or taken from reality, with respect to which the students identify and evaluate alternatives. The methods used to develop autonomy of judgment are: guided discussions in the classroom; individual problem solving; incidents; group work; role-playing; simulations of real business situations. Autonomy of judgment is also developed on the occasion of internship activities or the carrying out of the laboratories provided for in the training course.
The main teaching tools used will be: case studies, work tracks, simulations of organizational structures, individual and group reports.
Verification of the acquisition of autonomy of judgment takes place through the evaluation of the student's participation in the exercises and the analysis of the cases carried out in the classroom, as well as the results of the individual or group work on the cases proposed by the teacher, as well as through the judgment of the tutor of the host structure in the case of internship activities and participation in professional laboratories. Significant is also the evaluation of the final test, through which it will be possible to appreciate the ability to analyze and critically argue the hypotheses of research undertaken and to evaluate the overall maturity of the student and the ability to make autonomous judgments.
IV - Communication skills: The Master's graduate in "Business Consulting and Management" is able to:
- justify choices and communicate the results of the analyses with appropriate language, at the right level of detail and with the most appropriate technological methods;
- interact with both expert and non-expert interlocutors;
- work individually and integrate profitably into work groups, even in an international context;
- communicate, with competence and high linguistic propriety, information and evaluations relevant to the subjects of their competence;
- express themselves effectively in writing and orally.
The development and verification of communication skills are entrusted to various training activities and are assessed during oral presentations or the carrying out of laboratory activities or on the occasion of activities of coordination and participation in working groups, in the seminars on advanced topics, in the verification of the understanding of the texts. These activities are also added to those of coordination, participation and discussion within study groups.
Finally, the final test (which involves the discussion, before a commission, of a paper produced by the student on a thematic area of their own interest in the study path) also offers a further opportunity for in-depth study and verification of the skills of analysis, elaboration and communication of the work carried out. Any participation in internships, training courses and study stays abroad can also be a useful tool for the development of communication skills.
Official language(s) of teaching and assessment: ITALIAN, ENGLISH
V - Learning ability: The Master's graduate in "Business Consulting and Management":
- knows how to keep up to date on the evolution of the issues that affect the professional world, consulting and business management;
- has knowledge useful for the development of the profession towards issues related to quality control;
- has the ability to select and access information sources of various kinds (publications, databases, information available on the web, regulatory sources, practices);
- knows how to follow the evolution of knowledge, techniques, regulatory sources and their interpretations and consequently pursue their constant updating.
The student also possesses advanced study methodologies and learning skills that enable him to continue his studies at a high level in Master's degrees or in a PhD program, or to carry out autonomous in-depth studies of the problems addressed in the Master's degree course.
The student's learning ability is developed and verified through individual study, bibliographic research and updating activities, the study of relevant cases, the correction of papers. Space is also reserved for individual and group projects. Finally, the preparation of the thesis encourages autonomy in research as well as collaboration with teachers.
The achievement of the training objectives is verified within the final evaluation of the various training activities and the final test.
Skills related to the function: HIGHLY QUALIFIED BUSINESS CONSULTANT: The Master's graduate in Business Consulting and Management is able to address and solve the most complex problems of accounting, administration and taxation, integrated management control and financial control, as well as those related to the extraordinary phases of the life of companies. In particular, the Master's graduate in Business Consulting and Management: - is able to support private and public companies in the implementation of extraordinary company operations, also addressing the valuation aspects related to these operations;
- knows the international accounting principles for the preparation of financial statements, and can therefore provide consulting services on financial statements to all companies that apply the IAS/IFRS principles (large companies, banks, insurance companies); - knows the principles, techniques and tools of financial control and can therefore address and solve the problems of valuation and management of financial risks;
- is able to prepare business plans for new entrepreneurial initiatives and for companies that are already established; - together with the other skills of an accounting nature, he also knows the principles of auditing and can therefore operate effectively in the field of legal audit of accounts; - knows the principles and techniques for preparing the social balance sheet and can provide his professional services also in this consulting field; - can support companies in the crisis phase (bankruptcy trustee, expert approver of restructuring plans, etc.).
BUSINESS FUNCTION MANAGER: As already mentioned, the Master's graduate in Business Consulting and Management is able to address and solve the most complex problems of decision-making, administration, integrated management control and financial control. Therefore, he can also hold, even in large companies operating in the various sectors, the following roles: - administrative director; - head of the financial function; - head of the planning and control function; - head of the risk controlling function; - head of the internal control systems; - general manager, operational manager, management analyst, business consultant.
Function in a work context: HIGHLY QUALIFIED BUSINESS CONSULTANT: The Master's Degree Course in Business Consulting and Management trains professional profiles with highly qualified skills for the exercise of the profession of Chartered Accountant and Certified Accountant (with access to the A class of the register, subject to passing the state exam, according to current regulations) and business consultant. It is therefore aimed at students who intend to develop higher-level economic-business skills in the field of accounting professions and business consulting. With the degree course, in fact, the skills necessary for the exercise of these activities, acquired by students in the three-year courses of business and economics, are integrated and enhanced, as analytically indicated in the following section.
BUSINESS FUNCTION MANAGER: The specialized skills acquired in the areas of management, accounting and control allow the graduates of the course to be able to manage the most complex phases and operations of the life of companies, operating in the main functions of companies in various sectors, both public and private, therefore as a component of the personnel organization with responsibility in the various reference contexts, both through full mastery of analysis techniques and knowledge for supporting decision-making processes and by coordinating and organizing operational units.
