UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [1475] SOCIAL WORK, SOCIAL POLICIES AND SOCIOLOGICAL STUDIES AND SOCIAL RESEARCH
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10064

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Organization: 2013.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulations: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-87 - Master's Degree in Social Work and Social Policies.
Interclass: LM-88 - Master's Degree in Sociology and Social Research.
Inter-University Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: COGNITIVE, PSYCHOLOGICAL, EDUCATIONAL AND CULTURAL SCIENCES.
Website: https://servizio-sociale-politiche-studi-ricerca.cdl.unime.it
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Optional qualifications (choose from the following):
[L1+TSS]
Bachelor's Degree.
[L2+TSS]
First-Level Bachelor's Degree.
[TS+TSS]
Foreign Qualification.
[DU+TSS]
University Diploma.
[LS+TSS]
Specialist Degree.
Roles: Student mobility manager: ANNA PITRONE.
President: ANTONIA CAVA.
Coordinator: ANTONIA CAVA.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
The degree provides access to Specialization Courses, Research Doctorates and Second-Level University Master's Degrees.
Professional status conferred by the degree.
The LM course offers various professional opportunities in public administrations, local authorities and public and private service structures.
In particular, sociologists trained in LM-88 will be able to hold positions as officials or managers in public, private and social private organizations. Research, consulting and training roles. They will also be able to carry out specialist consulting functions in social research, as organization analysts, as public policy analysts, as experts in human resource management. They will hold managerial roles in structures dedicated to the study or implementation of social policies (services for families, children, the elderly, drug addicts, the mentally ill, etc.); in the field of training, guidance and information.
On the other hand, specialist social workers trained in LM-87 will find employment with functions of design or coordination in public and private structures that manage social services; in structures that provide services to people (Local Health Authorities, Ministry of Labor, Health, Social Policies, Equal Opportunities, Justice, Interior).
Graduates of the LM-87 class can, after passing the state exam, access the register of specialist social workers (section A).
All graduates of the course can continue their studies in second-level master's degrees with a sociological and psychological or social work focus, as well as in research doctorate courses activated in the specific area.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam will consist of a discussion of a written paper, with requirements of originality, on a topic of relevance for the graduate's training project, of which it is an integral part. The paper will be agreed upon with one or more teachers and must present the results of a research work developed independently, albeit under the supervision of a teacher of the course. The treatment must demonstrate knowledge of the main and most up-to-date reference literature as well as the methodological tools required by research in the field. The exam will be a significant moment in the graduate's training and will play a fundamental role in completing their learning path. In this perspective, it can also be developed in connection with the planned internship and training activities. With the final exam, the graduate must demonstrate that they have developed adequate critical and analytical skills and that they know how to combine the knowledge acquired with the specific requirements of a concrete research, the result of a constructive analysis of complex and even heterogeneous data.
Knowledge required for admission.
For all students, admission is conditional on the possession of curricular requirements defined in the didactic regulations of the study course. Regardless of the curricular requirements, a verification of personal preparation is required for all students, with methods defined by the didactic regulations. There are no outstanding educational debts, i.e. additional educational obligations, at the time of admission.
Specific training objectives.
Graduates in the courses of the LM-87 class must:
• possess in-depth knowledge of the theories of social work and the ability to use and experiment with advanced and innovative methodologies of social work;
• possess in-depth knowledge of sociological, social work, anthropological, economic-statistical, ethical-philosophical, legal, political, pedagogical, psychological and historical disciplines;
• possess a good knowledge of disciplines related to those of social work, also in relation to specific areas of application;
• possess in-depth methodological skills in social research, relating to the collection and processing of data and the understanding of the functioning of complex societies, also in specific areas of application;
• possess skills to decode complex needs of people, families, groups and the territory, for the formulation of social diagnoses, for psychosocial counseling, for mediation interventions in the family, child, social and criminal fields, for the management and organization of resources both in the public and private social sectors;
• possess skills to design integrated systems of local welfare and activate and manage, at national and international level, programs of information, awareness, empowerment, concertation and social protection of groups and communities, to protect their social rights;
• possess skills in design, planning, organization and managerial management in the field of policies, social and socio-health services, public and private social; analysis and evaluation of the quality of services and performances provided;
• possess adequate skills for communication and information management;
• possess qualifying experiences in relation to specific areas of intervention and training objectives related to the class; - be able to use at least one language of the European Union in addition to Italian, also with reference to disciplinary lexicons.
Graduates in the courses of the LM-88 class must:
• possess advanced knowledge of sociological disciplines and a high capacity for analysis and interpretation of social phenomena;
• possess advanced knowledge of basic disciplines in the area of social sciences and economics, statistics, philosophy, history, law and political science;
• possess advanced knowledge of disciplines related to sociology in relation to a specific area of application;
• possess advanced methodological skills relating to the measurement, collection and processing of data relevant to social research, and more generally to the analysis of the functioning of complex societies in general and in particular in a specific area of application;
• possess advanced knowledge of the theories and methods for the comparative analysis of societies;
• be able to carry out advanced analyses of the social and cultural effects of globalization processes;
• be able to operate in social research structures, or even in learning, development and dissemination of sociological knowledge in a national and international context, with a high degree of autonomy and responsibility;
• be able to use fluently at least one language of the European Union in addition to Italian, also with reference to disciplinary lexicons;
• possess adequate skills and tools for communication.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in SOCIAL WORK, SOCIAL POLICIES AND SOCIOLOGICAL STUDIES AND SOCIAL RESEARCH. Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding skills. Graduates at the end of the training course will have acquired:
- an advanced capacity for analysis and interpretation of social phenomena;
- an in-depth knowledge of the economic, historical and political framework in relation to specific areas of applied sociological analysis, of reading the needs of the territory and of designing social interventions;
- a high knowledge of the methods and techniques of social research and social work;
- an adequate methodological, organizational and legal knowledge relating to the system of socio-health services;
Knowledge and understanding skills of the indicated topics will be acquired through:
- participation in lectures, exercises, seminars
- personal study, guided and autonomous, within the scope of the training activities. The verification of the achievement of the expected results takes place mainly through oral and/or written examination methods, drafting and discussion of specific reports.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. Graduates will be able to:
- design, carry out and coordinate research in sociology and social intervention.
- apply theoretical knowledge to the analysis of social functioning;
- apply advanced methodological skills relating to the collection and processing of data relevant to social research to concrete cases;
- operate in interdisciplinary contexts, participating in complex design and working groups;
- conduct comparative analyses of different social contexts;
- understand social changes by identifying emerging problems and designing social policy interventions.
- analyze data and information in numerical or textual form.
- operate in evaluation and design in the fields of social sciences and social services.
The skills to apply the knowledge acquired at a theoretical level will be developed, in particular, through the internship and professional training activities at research and operational structures in the political and social sectors.
The verification of the acquired skills will take place, mainly, through the periodic evaluation of papers prepared by the students and the internship activities.
III - Autonomy of judgment. Graduates will be able to:
- read and analyze social problems with full autonomy of judgment, through the application of the sociological research method and through the empirical verification of their initial hypotheses;
- design and coordinate socio-health services in the area, coordinate social diagnosis projects with reference to the main areas of discomfort: family, child, social;
- critically examine methods and techniques from an epistemological point of view, critically orienting themselves in the construction of interpretative models of social phenomena and autonomously planning individual or team research;
- evaluate the applicability of the theoretical knowledge acquired in the specific social contexts under analysis, in order to carry out any integrations to support informed social policies.
Autonomy of judgment is developed through participation in seminar activities, through the preparation of written papers, participation in internship and training activities and the activity assigned by the teacher for the preparation of the final exam.
The verification of the acquisition of autonomy of judgment takes place through the evaluation of the individual student's study plan and the evaluation of the degree of autonomy and ability to work, even in a group, during the seminar activities, the internship and the preparation of the final exam. The achievement of autonomy of judgment skills can be ascertained, in particular, through the final thesis work.
IV - Communication skills. Graduates will have the ability to:
- communicate and interact with the various stakeholders in the scientific community and their work environment;
- dialogue with other experts, also in an academic context, to evaluate and interpret data and build projects.
- communicate effectively and clearly with any stakeholder of an institutional nature or belonging to civil society or operating in the third sector, also being able to network.
- master communication techniques, including IT, to make the results of their research accessible and to communicate their content.
The refinement of written and oral communication skills will take place, in addition to the seminar activities and the training activities that provide for active student participation, also through a language course, with the provision of specific laboratories, finding recurring verifications in the seminar activities, in the internship activities, in the presentation of the final exam.
Official language(s) of teaching and assessment.
ITALIAN.
V - Learning ability. Graduates will be able to:
- design and manage services for people, in local authorities or in the third sector, also with management responsibilities and with the functions of transferring their knowledge on innovation to collaborators of various kinds.
- independently manage their own updating and develop their skills both in an academic and professional context;
- integrate, with coordination functions, into qualified working groups in social research or social policies, also in international contexts.
Learning skills will also be verified with the contribution of language and computer laboratories and the use of new teaching and communication technologies, of which the graduate will become aware as a student, also acquiring the ability to use them during the two-year period.
Personal skills and the degree of learning ability will be specifically verified for each course also in the examination activities provided for at the end of the individual curricular activities, as well as by evaluating participation in seminar activities and the preparation of reports related to internship and training activities.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.

Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications:

[LS+TSS]
Specialist Degree
Roles: ROSANNA DI PAOLA (President), ROSANNA DI PAOLA (Coordinator)
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Veterinary Biotechnologist. Graduates with a Master's degree in Veterinary Biotechnology will find employment opportunities in the following professional fields:
- scientific research and technological development in public and private university institutions;
- public institutions (Istituto Superiore di Sanità, CNR, National Environmental Agency, Experimental Zooprophylactic Institutes, research institutions);
- pharmaceutical, diagnostic and biotechnological industries;
- public and private institutions dedicated to preclinical activities in the medical and veterinary fields;
- technical-scientific information;
- quality control management activities;
- clinical monitoring activities;
- regulatory, management and creation of biotechnological companies in the public and private sectors.
Characteristics of the final exam.
To be admitted to the final exam for the award of the Master's Degree in Veterinary Biotechnology, the student must have obtained all the credits provided for in the Programmed Teaching Plan, with the exception of those assigned to the final exam, and must be up to date with the payment of fees and university contributions.
The final exam for the award of the Master's Degree consists of a public discussion of the thesis, relating to an original work carried out by the student. The thesis should preferably be of an applied, design or experimental nature, from which the Commission can evaluate the cultural and scientific maturity as well as the quality of the work carried out. The student and the Supervisor may make use of the collaboration of a Co-supervisor in the preparation of the thesis. The thesis may be written in English, in whole or in part.
The determination of the degree score and the methods of presentation and evaluation of the thesis work are specified in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course.
Knowledge required for access.
To access the Master's Degree Course in Veterinary Biotechnology, applicants must hold a First-Level Degree in the following classes, as well as in the corresponding classes relating to D.M. 509/99, L-2, L-13, L-25, L-26, L-27, L-32, L-38, or hold a qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. Graduates of other First-Level classes who have obtained at least 40 CFU in the following SSDs, the possession of which is considered essential in order to attend the Course, may also be admitted: AGR/17, AGR/18, AGR/19, AGR/20, BIO/10, BIO/11, BIO/13, VET/07, VET/05, VET/06, VET/01, VET/02, VET/03, VET/10.
Subject to the possession of the specific curricular requirements, the verification of the student's preparation (as per Article 6, paragraph 2 of D.M. 270/04) is carried out, before enrollment, by a special Commission appointed within the Course Council. The adequacy of the personal preparation of the candidates will be verified by the Course Coordinator and by a Commission of teachers from the Course Council, specifically appointed. The adequacy of the initial preparation will be considered satisfactory for Candidates who have obtained a First-Level Degree with a score of not less than 90/110.
Specific methods of verifying the student's preparation will be indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course.
Specific learning objectives.
The specific objectives of the Study Course are:
- to possess in-depth knowledge of the biochemical and genetic aspects of cells and cell culture techniques;
- to possess solid knowledge of the structure, functions and analysis of biological macromolecules and cellular processes;
- to possess good knowledge of the morphology and functions of animal organisms;
- to know and be able to use the main methodologies that characterize cellular biotechnologies, also for the purpose of designing and producing biopharmaceuticals, diagnostics and vaccines for health purposes;
- to know and be able to use the methodologies in the cellular and molecular field of biotechnologies also for animal reproduction in clinical and experimental settings;
- to possess skills for the analysis of biopharmaceuticals, diagnostics and vaccines in the veterinary field with regard to biochemical, biological and toxicological aspects;
- to know the fundamental aspects of the operational processes that follow the industrial design of biotechnological products (including for gene therapy and cell therapy), and the formulation of biopharmaceuticals;
- to know and be able to use specific techniques and technologies for the design of innovative drugs;
- to know the fundamentals of pathological processes of interest to animals, with reference to their cellular and molecular pathogenic mechanisms;
- to know the congenital or acquired pathological conditions in which it is possible to intervene with a biotechnological approach;
- to acquire the ability to intervene to optimize animal production and reproductive efficiency;
- to be able to organize development activities within pharmaceutical and biotechnological companies with particular attention to bioethical aspects.
The training course that characterizes the Master's Degree Course in Veterinary Biotechnology aims to build professional figures with multidisciplinary skills in the biotechnological field. The course aims to train professionals who are able to understand the phenomena that underlie biotechnological processes, intervening ad hoc through the application of new methodological principles.
The first year of the course includes specialist compulsory courses common to all students, which allow them to acquire basic and advanced knowledge in the field of veterinary biotechnology, relating to the morpho-functional and animal biomolecular learning area, which allow them to achieve in-depth theoretical and practical training on biotechnological methodologies in the cellular and molecular field, which represent the essential cultural baggage underlying the professional activity required by the market. This must be added to the common training course relating to the legal, managerial and bioethical learning area, which aims to acquire essential knowledge on bioethical regulations and the ethical, environmental, social and economic impact associated with biotechnological research in the veterinary field; the acquisition of knowledge on patent and technology transfer activities in the field of veterinary biotechnology; the achievement of knowledge on managerial, management and marketing aspects related to research and development activities in the biotechnological sector.
In the second year of the course, students can choose between two training curricula, which offer the possibility of further deepening their preparation in specific sectors of veterinary biotechnology:
-Curriculum: Regenerative and Reproductive Biotechnologies, which falls within the biotechnological learning area in the animal production and reproductive field. The graduate will acquire knowledge in the biotechnological learning area in the animal production and reproductive field with a specific knowledge base on the structure and function of gametes, on the mechanisms that govern their interaction and that guide the process of fertilization and embryonic development. The biotechnological area applied in the animal production and reproductive field is characterized by the achievement of knowledge relating to biotechnological methodologies for animal reproduction in clinical and experimental settings, for the optimization of animal production and reproductive efficiency, as well as for the knowledge of reproductive biotechnological methodologies for the creation of experimental animal models, including transgenic ones. The graduate will have a practical application in the animal reproductive field, both clinical and experimental; he will acquire specific skills on gametes, embryos or tissues of the reproductive system and on the biotechnologies of reproduction through direct and advanced laboratory experience. Specifically, he will be able to: - operate with animal embryos and gametes; - intervene to optimize animal production and reproductive efficiency; - apply reproductive biotechnological methodologies for the development of reproductive experimental models and the creation of transgenic animals. The expected learning outcomes for the biotechnological area in the animal production and reproductive field are achieved through the following courses: -VET/01- Morphogenetic processes and developmental anomalies; -VET/02- Biotechnological applications to physiological systems; -VET/10- Biotechnologies applied to reproduction; -AGR/18- Biotechnologies applied to animal nutrition; -VET/10- Reproductive methodologies; -VET/07- Pharmacovigilance and pharmacosurveillance of veterinary drugs
-Curriculum: Biotechnology and Translational Medicine, which falls within the biotechnological learning area with diagnostic and therapeutic purposes in the translational field. The graduate will acquire diagnostic and therapeutic knowledge in the translational field. Knowledge of pathological processes of interest to animals, with reference to their cellular and molecular pathogenic mechanisms, zoonoses and the tools for understanding the role of laboratory animals as models of pathologies; knowledge of microbiological biotechnologies, animal nutrition and functional foods; knowledge of biotechnologies applied to the genetic improvement of animals and the enhancement of biodiversity; knowledge of methodologies aimed at choosing experimental models for research in the medical and surgical field, with reference to in-depth knowledge in the legislative field, on the protection and welfare of animals; knowledge of cellular biotechnological methodologies for the design and production of diagnostic aids, biopharmaceuticals and vaccines in the field of animal health, knowledge of biomaterials for the design of innovative therapeutic aids. Finally, knowledge of the operational processes for the industrial design of biotechnological products for gene therapy and cell therapy. The graduate will have the ability to: - use the knowledge relating to the structure and function of organs and systems to address the study of animal pathology; - manage a research project, planning the application of experimental methods, assuming project and structural responsibility; - develop, in vitro, in vivo and ex vivo, spontaneous, induced and genetically modified experimental models in the biomedical field for scientific, technological and animal reproduction research; - use the main methodologies of cellular biotechnologies for the design and production of diagnostic aids, biopharmaceuticals and vaccines in the field of animal health; - use diagnostic techniques in veterinary medicine, conservation and respect for animal biodiversity, also through targeted technologies for genetic selection and protection of human health through the control of the use of drugs and the spread of animal diseases of zoonotic character; The expected learning outcomes for the biotechnological area with diagnostic and therapeutic purposes are achieved through the following courses: -VET/01- Anatomical-histological methodologies of interest to veterinary and biomedical science; -VET/02- Welfare of animals of medical and veterinary interest and biomedical science; -VET/03- General veterinary pathology and molecular oncogenesis; -VET/06- Biotechnologies applied to the diagnosis of parasitic diseases of medical and veterinary relevance; -VET/05- Biotechnologies applied to the prevention and control of zoonoses; -VET/08- Clinical methodologies and animal legislation; -VET/09- Application of biomaterials and experimental surgery.
The didactic activities of the Course include theoretical courses with lectures and seminars and a practical activity carried out in special laboratories, which will have a significant weight as it is inherent in the philosophy of the course to provide the graduate with scientific and technical training that puts him in a position to enter the world of work. The completion of the student's cultural and professional training will also be enriched by direct participation in practical-applicative activities carried out in the field of Veterinary Biotechnologies aimed at preparing for the final exam.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding. The graduate must possess solid scientific foundations useful for understanding complex biological systems, with advanced knowledge relating to genetic/biochemical/molecular parameters in biological samples for the study of indicators of the alterations that are at the basis of veterinary pathologies, their use in the field of prevention and diagnosis. The graduates will also present specific skills to translate the understanding of biotechnological processes and the knowledge of complex biological systems into specific veterinary biomedical applications. For this reason, sectors characterizing the figure of the biotechnologist (BIO) and specifically of veterinary (VET and AGR) are involved. The results will be verified through profit exams (written and/or oral and practical tests, with the methods provided for in the University Didactic Regulations, through a joint overall evaluation by the teachers responsible for the courses or the coordinated modules; the acquisition of the credits established for each training activity is subject to passing the related tests and gives rise to votes in thirtieths, according to the University and Department regulations. Each course gives rise to a single profit exam.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding At the end of the training course, the graduate in Veterinary Biotechnology will have the ability to:
- apply the main methodologies of cellular biotechnologies, based on the knowledge acquired relating to cell structure and function, genetics and molecular biology. The results will be achieved with the following training activities: BIO/09 The cell and endocrine functionality, BIO/11 Molecular biology, BIO/12 Biomolecular methodologies, VET/02 Biotechnological applications to physiological systems;
- use the knowledge relating to the structure and function of organs and systems to address the study of animal pathology. The results will be achieved with the following training activities: VET/01 Methodologies of anatomical-histological interest to veterinary and biomedical science, VET/02 Biotechnological applications to physiological systems, VET/03 General veterinary pathology and molecular oncogenesis, VET/08 Clinical methodologies and animal legislation, VET/09 Application of biomaterials and experimental surgery;
- apply the legal, managerial and marketing knowledge related to research and technology transfer activities in the industrial field, through the development of research and patent projects. The results will be achieved with the following training activities: SECS-P/07 Business economics, VET/02 Welfare of animals of medical and veterinary interest and biomedical science, VET/08 Clinical methodologies and animal legislation;
- develop, in vitro, in vivo and ex vivo, spontaneous, induced and genetically modified experimental models in the biomedical field for scientific, technological and animal reproduction research. The results will be achieved with the following training activities: VET/07 Experimental models of drug and toxicology, VET/10 Biotechnologies applied to reproduction, VET/10 Reproductive methodologies, VET/02 Welfare of animals of medical and veterinary interest and biomedical science, VET/03 General veterinary pathology and molecular oncogenesis, VET/08 Clinical methodologies and animal legislation, VET/09 Application of biomaterials and experimental surgery;
- apply reproductive biotechnological methodologies for the development of reproductive experimental models and the creation of transgenic animals, operating with animal embryos and gametes to optimize animal production and reproductive efficiency. The results will be achieved with the following training activities: AGR/18 Biotechnologies applied to animal nutrition, BIO/12 Biomolecular methodologies, VET/01 Morphogenetic processes and developmental anomalies, VET/10 Biotechnologies applied to reproduction, VET/10 Reproductive methodologies; VET/02 Welfare of animals of medical and veterinary interest, VET/07 Experimental models of drug and toxicology;
- use the main methodologies of cellular biotechnologies for the design and production of diagnostic aids, biopharmaceuticals and vaccines in the field of animal health. The results will be achieved with the following training activities: BIO/10 Biochemistry and applied biochemistry, BIO/09 The cell and endocrine functionality, BIO/11 Molecular biology, BIO/19 Microbiology, VET/07 Experimental models of drug and toxicology, VET/10 Biotechnologies applied to reproduction, VET/05 Biotechnologies applied to the prevention and control of zoonoses, VET/06 Biotechnologies applied to the diagnosis of parasitic diseases of medical and veterinary relevance; VET/08 Clinical methodologies and animal legislation;
- use diagnostic techniques in veterinary medicine, conservation and respect for animal biodiversity, also through targeted technologies for genetic selection and protection of human health through the control of the use of drugs and the spread of animal diseases of zoonotic character. The results will be achieved with the following training activities:
AGR/17 Biotechnologies applied to the genetic improvement and biodiversity of livestock, VET/05 Biotechnologies applied to the prevention and control of zoonoses, VET/06 Biotechnologies applied to the diagnosis of parasitic diseases of medical and veterinary relevance; VET/07 Pharmacovigilance and pharmacosurveillance of veterinary drugs, VET/08 Clinical methodologies and animal legislation;
Methods and tools for achieving and verifying this descriptor:
- participation in practical exercise activities;
- participation in guided educational visits;
- carrying out internships and practical training;
- carrying out in-progress tests, verification tests not subject to final evaluation;
- carrying out oral and written examination tests.
III - Autonomy of judgment. Graduates with a Master's degree in Veterinary Biotechnology will acquire autonomy of judgment such as to allow the development of critical skills essential for the design and conduct of studies and research in the specific sectors of veterinary biotechnology, but also in related research sectors. The ability to analyze must also be directed to solving operational structure management problems. Graduates will also develop the ability to independently assess the ethical-social impact of scientific innovations resulting from biotechnological research not only in the animal field.
The methods and tools for achieving and verifying this descriptor are represented by the preparation of technical reports and presentations on IT supports relating to practical and internship activities in biotechnological companies, diagnostic and biotechnological laboratories and pharmaceutical industries.
IV - Communication skills. Graduates in Veterinary Biotechnology will be able to communicate effectively and without ambiguity of interpretation the innovative knowledge acquired and their implications in a context of collaboration with interlocutors with similar specialist training but also with interlocutors with different or less specialist training. Graduates will develop the ability to present in an appropriate and clear manner all the progress and the latest application innovations in the areas of interest of veterinary biotechnology, with particular reference to the industrial application of new diagnostic and therapeutic tools in the veterinary field, in translational medicine and in the field of animal reproduction. The training that graduates receive during their training course, also thanks to the attendance of the planned seminars and workshops, will allow them to direct their communication skills also towards interlocutors with different scientific training.
The methods and tools for achieving and verifying this descriptor are represented by the realization of group activities in which the presentation of specific topics is required, through the preparation of written and oral tests, through the preparation of presentations with IT tools. These skills can also be verified by evaluating them during oral and written examination tests and in the preparation of written papers, always within the examination tests.
Official language(s) of teaching and assessment.
ITALIAN
V - Learning ability. Graduates with a Master's degree will develop the ability to deepen their knowledge and skills in such a way as to allow the setting up and autonomous execution of studies and research relevant to the most specific sectors of veterinary biotechnology but also related research sectors not specifically related to the animal world. In particular, graduates will learn to consult databases and online library systems to carry out research activities and to use IT systems for continuous professional updating during the internship and thesis preparation period. Overall, the development of this ability, as well as the previous ones, will allow the graduate to organize and manage the activity of research groups made up of operators with similar or related scientific training.
The methods and tools with which the expected results are verified are:
1) courses that provide for a direct application of the acquired skills through exercise activities;
2) specific training activities relating to the acquisition of language skills;
3) preparation and drafting of the final paper;
4) other training activities including internships and practical training at companies, public or private bodies, and training and guidance internships.
Competencies related to the function.
Veterinary Biotechnologist. The skills of the graduate in Veterinary Biotechnology relate to the carrying out of:
- design and technological research activities for the selection and management of animal species;
- activities for the control of genetic variability and animal production in relation to the preservation of biodiversity; - design and development of biotechnologies for clinical and therapeutic purposes in a multidisciplinary context;
- pharmaceutical technical-scientific information.
Function in a working context.
Veterinary Biotechnologist. The professional figure of veterinary biotechnologist will have highly qualified skills in the field of reproductive biotechnologies and biotechnological methodologies applied to translational medicine and animal experimentation.
The functions performed by the graduate in a working context are as follows:
- therapeutic, with particular regard to the development and experimentation of innovative products with potential therapeutic applications to be applied to animal pathology and the use of animal models for biomedical and cell therapy studies;
- design and production, in relation to patents in the health field;
- diagnostic, through the management of molecular analysis technologies and cellular biotechnologies applied to the health sector;
- animal selection and reproduction in relation to the preservation of animal biodiversity and endangered species.

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [2460] PEDAGOGICAL SCIENCES
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10009

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2011.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulations: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-85 - Master's Degree in Pedagogical Sciences.
Inter-university Course: No.
Admission: Open Admission.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: COGNITIVE, PSYCHOLOGICAL, PEDAGOGICAL AND CULTURAL STUDIES.
Website: https://scienze-pedagogiche.cdl.unime.it
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L1+TSS]
Bachelor's Degree.
[L2+TSS]
First-Level Bachelor's Degree.
[TS+TSS]
Foreign Qualification.
[LS+TSS]
Specialist Degree.
Roles: Student mobility manager: PAOLO GIUSPOLI.
President: ANNAMARIA CURATOLA.
Coordinator: ANNAMARIA CURATOLA.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
This degree provides access to Specialization Courses, Research Doctorates and Second-Level University Master's Degrees.
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Graduates in this degree course will be able to carry out pedagogical consultancy, management and coordination activities in public and private, school and non-school structures, with regard to all dimensions of educational problems (service, project and cultural programs directly or indirectly aimed at training, or also support activities for access to and dissemination of knowledge and culture); evaluation and monitoring of educational and training interventions of various levels; support for information campaigns in the aforementioned fields.
Specifically, graduates in the specialist degree courses of this class will be able to carry out activities of:
- research, action research, design, coordination, consultancy, evaluation, documentation in the field of services, training systems, centers and research structures, public and private bodies and organizations connected to education, training, social forms of prevention, enhancement of human resources and the relationship with the world of work and enterprise;
- design, intervention, research and evaluation in the field of educational and training services of various kinds connected to the aspects of discomfort, marginality and integration between cultures in the territory;
- promotion, design, coordination, evaluation of cultural and intercultural projects connected to educational and training themes and problems with reference to the various age groups, launched by local authorities, cultural institutions, foundations, associations.
Graduates may consider teaching in schools as a career, once they have completed the process of obtaining teaching qualifications and passed the competitions provided for by current regulations.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam consists of the preparation and discussion, before a Commission appointed according to the Faculty's didactic regulations, of a dissertation in which the student demonstrates that they have acquired a rigorous research method in a specific area of educational sciences (and with a specific declination: theoretical, historical or empirical) and that they have developed adequate argumentative skills.
This exam will take place in a public session before a commission of teachers, who will express the overall evaluation in hundredths.
The final degree grade will be determined by the average of the grades obtained in the individual profit exams, the quality of the credits acquired and the discussion of the final paper produced by the student.
The activities related to the preparation of the final exam for the achievement of the degree will be carried out by the student under the supervision of a teacher.
The paper may also be written in a foreign language previously studied.
Knowledge required for admission.
Enrolment in the Master's Degree Course LM 85 "Pedagogical Sciences" presupposes that the student possesses the following requirements:
1. a three-year degree in class L-19 ("Educational Sciences and Training") obtained at the University of Messina or at another university that provides an equivalent qualification.
2. any other first-level degree that allows access with no more than 30 outstanding credits (which must be completed within the first year of the Master's degree course), as defined by current regulations (students with another Master's degree or equivalent with at least 20, but no more than 40, transferable credits will be admitted to the second year of the course);
3. aptitude for study and learning of knowledge in the field of pedagogical, psychological, philosophical and sociological disciplines;
4. good knowledge of the methodology (quantitative and qualitative) of research in the socio-psycho-pedagogical field and its application;
5. verbal, communication and relational skills.
In any case, a verification of the personal preparation required for admission is foreseen, with methods that will be appropriately defined in the didactic regulations of the course of study.
Specific training objectives.
Graduates in the Master's Degree courses of this class, upon completion and refinement of the training acquired in the three-year degree courses of class 18, must possess:
• solid and in-depth theoretical and practical skills and knowledge in the sciences of pedagogy and education and in those disciplines that, such as philosophy, history, psychology and sociology, on the one hand contribute to defining the entire conceptual framework and, on the other, promote its application in the different educational and training contexts;
• adequate mastery of the methodology of educational research of a theoretical, historical, empirical and experimental nature, in formal, non-formal and informal training environments;
• in-depth knowledge of the various aspects of educational design (analysis of needs, definition of general and specific objectives, evaluation of human, instrumental and structural resources, programming, intervention methodologies, verification and evaluation) and the methods and techniques relating to the monitoring and evaluation of the outcomes and social impact of projects and programs;
• good mastery of the main IT tools and telematics communication in the specific areas of competence;
• fluent possession, in writing and orally, of at least one language of the European Union, in addition to Italian, with reference also to disciplinary lexicons.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in PEDAGOGICAL SCIENCES.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding skills.
At the end of the course, the student must demonstrate that they have consolidated the theoretical knowledge specifically relating to the sciences of education (pedagogy, philosophy, psychology, sociology, etc.) already acquired in the first university cycle. This must guarantee the student:
- the ability to analyze, understand and effectively manage the different training contexts and the problems connected to them;
- adequate skills and abilities to solve problems in operational and/or research contexts that are broader, more complex or, in general, unusual but still connected to the specific sector of study/educational/training intervention.
The knowledge must concern in particular:
- the epistemological structure of the individual disciplines that make up the sciences of education;
- the interdisciplinary relationships that connect them;
- the didactics relating to the same disciplines.
The level of competence acquired is evaluated during the course through oral interviews and/or written tests and/or papers on specific disciplinary topics.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding.
The knowledge and skills acquired in the field of educational sciences must allow the possession of specific skills, transversal knowledge, work and research methods, teaching strategies, etc. necessary for the graduate to manage teaching/learning activities but also educational interventions in the services. The graduate in "Pedagogical Sciences" must be able to design and implement personalized training courses, to devise and conduct work-school alternation training courses, to design and apply appropriate training models for training projects and the recipients of the interventions.
The specific skills acquired must support the implementation, management and evaluation of training interventions in formal contexts.
The acquisition of these skills will be verified through laboratory and internship activities.
III - Autonomy of judgment.
Graduates must demonstrate the ability to integrate the knowledge and skills acquired with the concrete conditions they will have to face in operational contexts. The graduate must therefore be able to read and interpret autonomously the context in which he/she operates in order to adequately respond to the demand expressed by the social and cultural reality and to formulate appropriate and targeted operational intervention hypotheses.
The possession of "making judgment" will be ascertained during the periodic checks both through a constant reference to the state of the art of the discipline and through the solution of emerging problems through "new and original" answers.
IV - Communication skills.
The graduate in "Pedagogical Sciences" must possess communication, relational and organizational skills to design, implement and manage educational interventions both in formal and non-formal contexts.
He/she must also demonstrate the ability to manage relationships with the various institutions in the area in relation to the demand (manifest and/or latent) of the users.
His/her communication skills must be supported by:
- adequate knowledge of at least one language of the EU;
- skills in the use of the main IT tools for teaching/educational purposes.
This skill will be assessed through panels, forums, working groups and communication methods that use multimedia languages (excel, power point, concept maps, etc.).
Official language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN.
V - Learning skills.
The graduate must be able to acquire and interpret new information coming from different contexts, local, national and international, in order to respond to the needs expressed by the social reality.
He/she must also demonstrate the ability to translate new methods and techniques relating to the design, monitoring and evaluation of projects and programs into operational terms.
The graduate in "Pedagogical Sciences" must therefore demonstrate that he/she has acquired, at the end of his/her studies, the autonomy necessary to undertake advanced research paths (doctorate, second-level master's degrees, refresher courses).
The verification of "learning skills" will be demonstrated by the preparation of the specialist degree thesis in order to verify the possession of the issues relating to scientific research, applying and interpreting in an original way the most advanced theoretical and practical resources available.

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [2470] TOURISM AND ENTERTAINMENT
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10012

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2011.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-49 - Master's Degree in Design and Management of Tourism Systems.
Interclass: LM-65 - Master's Degree in Performing Arts and Multimedia Production.
Interuniversity Course: No.
Access: Open Admission.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: DEPARTMENT OF COGNITIVE, PSYCHOLOGICAL, EDUCATIONAL AND CULTURAL SCIENCES.
Website: https://turismo-e-spettacolo.cdl.unime.it
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Optional qualifications (choose from the following):
[L1+TSS]
Bachelor's Degree.
[L2+TSS]
First-Level Degree.
[A2+TSS]
Second-Level Academic Diploma.
[TS+TSS]
Foreign Qualification.
[A1+TSS]
First-Level Academic Diploma.
[LS+TSS]
Specialist Degree.
Roles: Student mobility manager: FRANCESCO PAOLO CAMPIONE.
President: LOREDANA TROVATO.
Coordinator: LOREDANA TROVATO.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
This degree provides access to Specialization Courses, Research Doctorates and Second-Level University Master's Degrees.
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Graduates of LM-49 will be able to find employment opportunities with high-level responsibilities in the design, organization and management of tourism promotion agencies, both public and private; in the coordination and management of companies related to hotel accommodation, in the development of projects for the enhancement of specific material and cultural products of the territory; in the organization of the structural and human resources necessary to welcome and promote cultural and congress tourism; in consulting for bodies responsible for the enhancement of cultural, demo-anthropological, historical and artistic assets; in the administration of archaeological parks and monumental areas, in visitor reception facilities; in private companies of a hotel nature; at tour operators; in cooperative organizations of a tourist nature; in the organization of cultural events; in the field of cultural and tourist communication, including multimedia.
For graduates in LM-65, the field of historical-theoretical research in the various areas of live performance becomes an elective; the criticism of live performance; the design, creation and implementation of works and events in the field of live performance production in its various genres: musical, cinematic, theatrical, photographic and in all those genres that require multimedia productions of varying complexity; the direction and management of theaters, festivals, institutions and performance structures; the design and coordination of training and theatrical animation activities; teaching in primary and secondary schools and in Higher Artistic, Musical and Dance Institutes, where disciplines related to live performance are included. It is clear that the professions of actor and playwright are, despite the precariousness that characterizes them, a fundamental part of the professional expectations of those who dedicate themselves to the study of the performing arts.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam will consist of a discussion of an extensive written paper resulting from an original research project, which may also include audiovisual or digital or performative contributions, on a topic agreed upon with a teacher or a group of teachers within the curricular disciplines. The explicit demonstration of the concrete skills, exploitable in the labor market, acquired during the study path and during the internships at public or private bodies specializing in the tourism and/or entertainment sector will be required.
In particular, the final exam for the class in Design and Management of Tourism Systems LM-49 may also consist of the formulation of an original project, supplemented with executive content, for the operational management of structures for the promotion and organization of tourism and consultancy for cultural enterprises.
In particular, the final exam for the class in Performing Arts and Multimedia Production LM-65 may also consist of the production of an online, offline or multimedia product concerning the area of Performance or the spectacularization of events and cultural and audiovisual heritage, arts. Both individual work and team work with defined individual contributions are allowed.
Knowledge required for access.
Students who wish to access this class must demonstrate that they possess a solid preparation in relation to the disciplines of tourism and/or those of entertainment. The most suitable, but not exclusive, three-year degree courses are L-3 (DISCIPLINES OF VISUAL ARTS, MUSIC, PERFORMANCE AND FASHION), L-15 (TOURISM SCIENCES) and L-20 (COMMUNICATION SCIENCES).
In particular, among the general requirements, there are a good knowledge of the historical, semiotic, ethno-anthropological and philosophical foundations of the artistic disciplines, therefore of the history of art, performance, photography, new media; a clear knowledge of classical and modern dramaturgy; excellent theoretical and practical competence in information technology and multimedia technologies; knowledge of languages and knowledge of the economic, administrative and legal aspects of the specific sector. It would be useful to have completed specific internships and traineeships (in theater, cinema, television, photography, tourism promotion agencies, in the hotel sector, and in food and wine skills).
In any case, possession of at least 60 CFU including: language sectors (L-LIN/02-14), disciplines of arts and entertainment (L-ART/01-08), historical disciplines (M-STO/01-08), semiotic-linguistic knowledge L-LIN/01 and M-FIL/05), sociological (SPS/07-10), anthropological (M-DEA/01), geographical (M-GGR/01-2) and technical-methodological, both of the scientific sectors (INF/01, ING/INF/01,03,04 and 05), and of the humanities (M-PED/01-03), as well as, of course, all those included in this program. The verification of these requirements will be obtained through regularly certified documentation of the university career. Other possible methods of verification will be specifically defined by the didactic regulations of the study course.
In any case, the verification of personal preparation is also planned through methods that will be specified in the didactic regulations of the study course.
Specific training objectives.
Graduates in the degree courses of the LM-49 class must have:
• advanced skills in operating within tourism systems, in homogeneous or integrated contexts, including territorial areas belonging also to different regions, characterized by the integrated offer of cultural, environmental and tourist attractions or by the widespread presence of tourist companies;
• advanced skills in the management of tourist companies in order to integrate accommodation companies with cultural and environmental services;
• advanced skills in the promotion, marketing and management of tourist products, also with the aid of new multimedia technologies;
• linguistic and specialist skills to relate to the processes of globalization of tourist activities or cultural events at international level;
• advanced skills in designing and implementing intersectoral and infrastructural interventions necessary for the qualification of the tourist offer, in particular in the areas engaged in the enhancement of cultural and environmental assets with the creation and promotion of new tourist products;
• technical skills necessary for the programming of cultural intervention projects drawn up by public administrations;
• good knowledge, in written and oral form, of at least two languages (including at least one of the European Union) in addition to Italian. Graduates in the degree courses of the LM-65 class must:
• possess specialist scientific, theoretical and technical, methodological and operational skills relating to artistic culture in the fields of visual arts, performance and visual communication and be able to apply them critically, also from a gender perspective, in the design and creation of works;
• have advanced skills in the sectors of ideation, production of spectacular events, as well as in the management of theatrical, film, television and radio structures;
• be able to use the main computer and telematics communication tools in the specific areas of competence;
• be able to use fluently, in written and oral form, at least one language of the European Union in addition to Italian, with reference also to the disciplinary lexicons.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in TOURISM AND ENTERTAINMENT.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding In general, graduates must demonstrate that they fully understand the link between the management of tourist development and the enhancement of the artistic forms of cultural entertainment. Since the organization of events functional to tourist development must take place on the ground of the highest level of qualitative selection of the artistic product, compatible with the target of cultural tourism at each time precisely identified, the graduate of the class must also be able to associate the quality of the artistic product with its value in terms of economic-tourist attractiveness. Finally, the understanding of the historical and artistic nature of initiatives and cultural events functional to the enhancement of heritage must take place through an in-depth knowledge of contemporary media technologies.
More specifically for the LM-49 class:
1) graduates must have a thorough knowledge of the historical, linguistic, anthropological and geographical-territorial foundations of the context in which they will operate as promoters of cultural tourism. This objective will be achieved through attendance of theoretical lectures and in-depth seminars and will be verified through assessments during the course and final written and oral exams.
2) they must acquire in-depth theoretical and practical knowledge in the legal, economic and administrative field for the acquisition and management of financial, instrumental and human resources, also demonstrating entrepreneurial skills to ensure institutional continuity for tourist development. This objective will be achieved through attendance of theoretical lectures and in-depth seminars and internships at public and private bodies. Verification will take place through final written and oral exams and evaluations of the internship experience.
3) they must speak and read fluently at least two EU languages, which will be taught through an organization of lectures combined with laboratory activities and which will be assessed through oral exams for speaking and written exams for grammar.
4) they must be able to design communication platforms technologically suitable to illustrate, distribute and disseminate information functional to the full enjoyment of cultural, artistic or natural assets. This objective will be achieved through laboratory activities and assessed during the course.
For the LM-65 class:
1) graduates must demonstrate a thorough understanding of the process of transformation and integration taking place between acquired knowledge assets and the technologization of the performing arts. This objective will be achieved through attendance of theoretical lectures and in-depth laboratory activities and will be verified through assessments during the course and final written and oral exams.
2) they must be able to experience concrete skills in experimenting with new technologies of vision, applying them to the various areas of graphic, photographic, video-cinematic, television and theatrical representation, also knowing how to "hybridize" (McLuhan) them with each other. This objective will be achieved through attendance of theoretical lectures and laboratory activities and will be verified through assessments during the course and final written and oral exams.
3) the introduction and advanced practice of the technologies of performance must be based on a thorough theoretical competence, based on the semiotics and sociology of the arts, on the philosophy of language and on the pragmatics of literary, linguistic and artistic communication in the various genres of representation. For this objective, only theoretical lectures and in-depth seminars are planned. The assessment methods will be exclusively oral.
4) graduates must also have a thorough knowledge of the literary and artistic aspects of the languages studied, which will be taught mainly through lectures and assessed in oral and written exams.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding In general terms, graduates must demonstrate that they can apply the knowledge acquired:
1) knowing how to formulate clear and detailed planning projects of all cultural, managerial and intensive exploitation activities of cultural and artistic resources in the territorial context in which they will operate;
2) knowing how to develop targeted cultural policies in the qualitative selection of conservation processes of cultural heritage and in the hierarchy of events that are truly relevant from the point of view of artistic significance;
3) distributing in the appropriate territorial areas the promotional human and instrumental resources available to territorial bodies and private companies operating in the field of tourism and entertainment and coordinating the interaction between the agents producing events and the political-administrative or entrepreneurial operators;
4) organizing communication plans for the promotional strategies of the various events with the joint contribution of experts in the field (historians, anthropologists, sociologists, economists, linguists, semioticians and art historians) and the artistic and entertainment groups that will carry out these events;
More specifically for the LM-49 class:
1) knowing how to mobilize all agencies, tour operators, groups and trade associations (productive, commercial, technical, promotional) around the moments of greatest concentration of congress and/or cultural activity;
2) providing complete support of background knowledge, techniques and legislative, administrative and technological tools for the efficient programming of the interventions of cultural operators;
3) knowing how to organize groups of operators of integrated tourism communication in the main world circuits of traveler circulation;
4) permanently coordinating the main networks of cultural tourism with the operators of food and wine culture, enogastronomy, recreational activities and side activities that make it possible to make stays pleasant and productive for study and/or work;
5) arousing and provoking the cultural demand that can then generate the artistic and spectacular events of the various seasonal occasions;
While for LM-65:
1) acquiring a vocabulary focused on the use of new media;
2) experimenting with the in-depth use of digital technology supports in all representation activities (digital photography, digital television, digital video-cinema, media dramaturgy, new forms of expression on the WEB) and of e-learning art;
3) organizing forms of inter-cultural and multi-disciplinary integrated artistic expression (ethnomusicological festivals, world theater festivals, cinema of globalization, trans-national forms of television and multimedia shows);
4) facilitating the formation of agencies for the distribution of artistic-representational products in the perspective of the knowledge society;
III - Autonomy of judgment Graduates must demonstrate a scientific and cultural versatility that enables them to identify, from time to time, a level of critical awareness aimed at deepening the heterogeneous problems of a territory (such as the island one) in which the need for constant flexibility and wise adaptation to the crucial emergencies but also, surprisingly, to the opportunities determined by a state of constant economic and organizational difficulty of the entertainment world is increasingly evident.
IV - Communication skills Graduates of both classes must be able to express themselves clearly and exhaustively, through verbal languages (at least two EU languages, in addition to adequate Italian for the contents) and non-verbal languages (graphics, statistical graphics, the ability to draw up tables of various types, presentations, photographic and/or illustrative information, films and other communication artifacts). These skills must be demonstrated in the heterogeneity of the multiple cognitive approaches to the history and practices of entertainment, configuring the training of professionals capable of adapting to the operational contexts as designers and curators of theatrical events, expert editors in the field of live entertainment, theater critics, experts in culture and theater education.
Language/s of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN V - Learning ability In general terms, for the common area, the learning skills require rapid processing of information and a deep interest in the topics covered. For the LM-49 class, learning skills in foreign languages, detailed processing of information, fluent ease of learning information technology and multimedia technologies and the tendency to seek efficient solutions to the complex problems of human resource management through constant practice of linguistic mediation are required.
For the LM-65 class, the current scenario is one of continuous change in which the performing arts know, both in terms of their guarded exegesis and in terms of a more and more fruitful experimentation, a continuous turning under the banner of the drift of genres and the mixing of the hitherto distinct forms of acting, musical and visual representation in general. Therefore, learning skills must be calibrated, both qualitatively and quantitatively, on the measure of interdisciplinary skills and flexibility in interpretation and docimological evaluation. Under the aegis of what is defined in rather stereotyped terms as multimedia, there is a widespread need to overcome the most tired and used forms of artistic expression. Graduates must calibrate their learning ability on this instability and measure the value of their theoretical knowledge in a perspective of more than speculative practical experimentation.
