-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [2473] COGNITIVE SCIENCE AND THEORY OF COMMUNICATION
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10526

INFO ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Degree program: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: English. Type: Master's Degree. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: LM-55 - Master's Degree in Cognitive Sciences. Interclass degree: LM-92 - Master's Degree in Communication Theories. Interuniversity course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: MESSINA. Department: DEPARTMENT OF COGNITIVE, PSYCHOLOGICAL, EDUCATIONAL AND CULTURAL STUDIES. Website: https://cognitive-science-and-theory-of-communication.cdl.unime.it. Teaching method: Distance learning. Knowledge requirements for access: No. Course lecturers: View list. Admission requirements: Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [TS], Foreign degree, [L1], Bachelor's degree, [L2], First-cycle degree. Student mobility manager: SEBASTIANO NUCERA. President: GIANCARLO IANNIZZOTTO. Coordinator: GIANCARLO IANNIZZOTTO. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Professionals in cognitive science. The professional profile for which the course specifically prepares is suitable for employment in private and public organisations where skills are required in the areas of cognitive rehabilitation, software programming and neural networks, software design and human-machine interfaces. The working environments are to be found in:
- public administrations where the skills for which the course qualifies are required;
- public and private training organisations;
- universities and other organisations where research is carried out in the field of cognitive sciences;
- scientific research and care institutions and health facilities active in the field of cognitive rehabilitation;
- companies operating in the IT sector, particularly in software programming, website and social platform design and the development of human-machine interfaces.
Graduates in Cognitive Science will also be able to work as freelancers and consultants in the above areas.
Professionals in Communication Theory. The work areas in which graduates in Communication Sciences will mainly work in both the public and private sectors are:
- Web analysis and advanced design
- Corporate communication
- Multimedia communication
- Public, political and institutional communication
- Editing and copywriting
- Marketing
- Design and management of social platforms
- Public relations
- Human resources
- Cultural services. The working environments of graduates in Communication Theories are to be found in:
- public administrations (press offices and other positions of responsibility);
- public and private radio and television companies;
- companies operating electronic and social media publications;
- companies operating in the field of advertising and marketing
- human resources and public relations departments in public and private companies.
Characteristics of the final examination.
The final examination consists of the public discussion of a written dissertation on a topic related to one or more of the subject areas of the course of study, which the student will agree with the chosen supervisor as a guide for the drafting of the paper. For the presentation of their work to the committee, the student will also make use of multimedia support, if deemed appropriate.
Knowledge requirements for access.
Students interested in enrolling in the interclass LM-55 (Cognitive Sciences) and LM-92 (Communication Theories) degree course must have in-depth knowledge of cognitive processes, of the methodology of empirical sciences, and adequate knowledge of neuroscience and of philosophical, semiotic, communicative, pedagogical knowledge, as well as of anthropological, linguistic, IT and socio-political fields.
For enrolment in the course, a three-year degree in the following classes is required: L-05 (Philosophy), L-19 (Education and training sciences), L-20 (Communication sciences), L-24 (Psychological sciences and techniques), L-15 (Cultural tourism), L-03 D.A.M.S., L-39 (Social service sciences), L-31 (Computer sciences and technologies) and equivalent, or another three-year degree obtained in Italy that ensures the acquisition of at least 30 CFU in the following SSDs: MPED, MFIL, MSTO, MPSI, SPS, or another degree obtained abroad and recognised as suitable. As this is an international course given in English, certification of a level of competence equal to at least B2 is required as an entry qualification.
KNOWLEDGE REQUIRED FOR CLASS LM-55 COGNITIVE SCIENCES:
Students interested in completing a Master's degree in the LM-55 class (Cognitive Science) must have a solid background in the disciplines of philosophy, psychology, linguistics and computer science. In particular, the requirements for students wishing to attend the course include at least elementary knowledge of the functioning of the main cognitive processes, the anatomy of the brain, epistemology, the principles of computer operation, the processes of semiotics and communication, the principles of cultural and social relations, as well as the English language. The method of verification will consist in the possession of at least 60 CFUs included in the order of the present course of studies among the characterising activities of the LM-55 class, the common ones and the related ones, all specifically recognised by the Course Council.
KNOWLEDGE REQUIRED FOR CLASS LM-92 THEORIES OF COMMUNICATION: For students interested in enrolling in the Master's degree in the LM-92 class (Theories of Communication), adequate preparation requires broad basic knowledge in the disciplinary sectors of communication, including those related to new technologies and new media; the possession, in written and oral form, of two foreign languages; the ability to use computer tools for the representation of data and knowledge. The verification method will consist in the possession of at least 60 CFUs included in the order of the present course of studies among the characterising activities of the LM-92 class, the common ones and the related ones, all specifically recognised by the Course Council.
Specific training objectives.
As regards the educational objectives of the Interclass Degree Course in Cognitive Sciences and Communication Theories, graduates in Class LM-55 (Cognitive Sciences) must:
- have advanced preparation in the disciplines that contribute to defining the field of cognitive sciences such as cognitive psychology, cognitive neuroscience, linguistics, artificial intelligence, philosophy and social sciences;
- possess in-depth theoretical and operational knowledge for the interdisciplinary approach to the study of the mind and behaviour of organisms;
- have in-depth theoretical and operational knowledge of communication and decision-making processes;
- have in-depth knowledge of data collection and analysis methodologies;
- be familiar with the simulative, observational and experimental methodologies used in the study of the mind-brain system and of the interfaces between man and machine and between man and other complex systems;
- have the ability to analyse decision-making processes at individual and collective level, in organisational, economic and social contexts;
- have the ability to design models and interventions for the reorganisation of the interfaces between man and complex systems;
- be able to carry out basic and applied research activities independently;
- be able to use fluently, in written and oral form, at least one language of the European Union in addition to Italian, with reference also to the disciplinary lexicons.
Graduates from the LM-55 degree courses will be able to carry out functions of high responsibility in organisations and services aimed at individuals, groups and communities (schools, public administration, public and private companies) and in the design of computer interfaces and systems. They will also be able to work as freelancers and consultants in public and private organisations.
For the purposes indicated, the LM-55 degree course provides:
- activities aimed at acquiring theoretical and methodological knowledge relating to the various disciplinary fields of cognitive sciences;
- activities aimed at developing the skills necessary for the creation of computational models of the functioning of systems at different levels of complexity;
- activities aimed at developing cooperative skills in conducting advanced research in the cognitive sciences;
- seminars, laboratory activities, application experiences in real or simulated situations, related to training activities in the disciplinary fields characterising the class;
- the performance of activities that have the value of internships;
- in relation to specific objectives, external activities and study stays at other Italian and European universities, also within the framework of international agreements.
Graduates in the LM-92 class must:
- possess high-level theoretical and methodological skills enabling them to contribute to the analysis, design and implementation of communication models at different levels;
- be able to design studies and research activities in the field of communication;
- be able to understand the structure and functioning of the various media;
- have mastered the technical knowledge of communication software and the resulting communication skills;
- have a written and oral command of at least one European Union language in addition to Italian.
The occupational outlets and professional activities envisaged by the degree course are to be identified, with functions of high responsibility:
- in the analysis, conception and implementation of integrated communication systems, with particular reference to the new media;
- in the communication and dissemination of knowledge, being able to promote and manage the use of new technologies at various levels, both in public bodies and in private companies, as well as in economic organisations of various types, as promoters and managers of information and communication innovation.
For the purposes indicated, the Master's degree course in Theories of Communication:
- includes, among the curricular entry requirements, at least a knowledge of English and practical computer activities;
- includes activities dedicated to deepening knowledge of the fundamentals of communication and information;
- includes ensuring the multidisciplinarity of theoretical and critical knowledge, in-depth studies aimed at acquiring high-level skills;
- includes laboratory and/or internship activities, also in the form of external activities (training periods at organisations, institutes and companies, as well as study stays at other Italian and foreign universities);
- culminates in a design, research or analysis activity, which demonstrates mastery of the subjects, as well as the ability to operate autonomously in the field of communication.
As regards the description of the training course, it is structured, in accordance with the training objectives envisaged for the respective degree classes and according to the relative areas of learning, as follows:
For the LM-55 class it is divided into the following learning areas:
- characterising area of the philosophical and linguistic disciplines, in the SSDs L-LIN/01, M-FIL/01, M-FIL/02 and M-FIL/05. The knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist in the acquisition of the theoretical tools necessary to carry out autonomously and critically the philosophical and linguistic investigation and research on the human and animal mind, on cognitive processes, on the functional role they play in behaviour and language and on the possibility of simulating them through artificial devices;
- characterising area of psychological disciplines (SSD M-PSI/01). The knowledge, skills and abilities provided in this area relate to the psychological investigation of the nature of the thought processes of human and animal organisms and the dysfunctionalities that impair their success.
- characterising area of psychobiology and cognitive neuroscience (SSD M-PSI/02). The knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist in the experimental investigation of the neurophysiological processes underlying cognitive processes and the functional architecture of the brain, and in the acquisition of mastery of the techniques and methodologies adopted in this field of study.
- characterising area of mathematics, informatics and engineering disciplines (SSD INF/01). The knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist of the acquisition of the fundamentals of computer science and information theory, as well as the main programming methodologies.
- characterising area of economics, statistics and social sciences (SSD SPS/07). The knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist in the acquisition of the basic theoretical notions of sociology as regards its relevance to the cognitive sciences.
- related disciplines (SSD INF/01, ING-INF/05, L-ART/06, M-FIL/03 and M-FIL/05). In accordance with the interdisciplinary nature of the course, the knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist of in-depth thematic studies with a specialised vocation relating to the disciplines already present in the characterising areas, as well as the problems inherent in the design of human-machine interaction and the engineering of usability and the study of theoretical approaches to the theme of mediality developed since the twentieth century.
- free disciplines: these are chosen by the student, provided that they are congruent with the main disciplinary characteristics of the course of study;
- other knowledge useful for entry into the world of work;
- final examination.
For the LM-92 class, the course is divided into the following learning areas:
- characterising area of theories and techniques of information and communication (SSD INF/01, ING-INF/05, L-ART/06, L-LIN/01, M-FIL/02, M-FIL/05, SPS/07). The knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist in the mastery of the fundamental notions of information theory and programming, of the problems inherent in the design of human-machine interaction and usability engineering, of the main media theories developed since the twentieth century, of the main theories of linguistic and cultural communication, in the philosophical investigation of the cognitive processes involved in communication, and in the acquisition of the basic theoretical notions of sociology applied to the investigation of communication processes.
- characterising area of socio-economic, historical-political and cognitive disciplines (SSD M-FIL/01, M-FIL/03, M-FIL/05, M-PSI/01, M-PSI/02). In accordance with the interdisciplinary nature of the course, the knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist in the acquisition of the theoretical foundations of the philosophy and ethics of communication as well as the tools for psychological and neuroscientific investigation of the thought processes involved in communication and their neurophysiological correlates.
- related disciplines (SSD INF/01 and M-FIL/01). The knowledge, skills and abilities transmitted in this area consist of in-depth thematic studies on the theoretical foundations of artificial intelligence and reflection on the cognitive processes involved in communication.
- free disciplines: these are chosen by the student, provided that they are congruent with the main disciplinary characteristics of the course of study;
- other knowledge useful for entry into the world of work;
- final examination.
I - Knowledge and understanding skills: Graduates in Cognitive Science (LM-55) will acquire knowledge and theoretical and applied skills concerning:
- the interdisciplinary approach to the study of the mind and the behaviour of organisms, starting from the knowledge traditionally belonging to the cognitive sciences (philosophy of language and mind, linguistics, computer science, psychology, neuroscience), as well as phenomenology applied to cognitive science and sociology;
- the development of the mind-brain system, both physiological and pathological;
- the study of language and behaviour from a philosophical and scientific perspective;
- multimodal communication between humans and between humans and computers, with emphasis on the integration of verbal and non-verbal information and the design of interfaces using these modalities.
These educational objectives will be achieved through lectures, seminars, laboratory activities and their actual achievement will be verified through the profit exams related to each teaching of the degree course and the approval of the activities carried out during internships and stages, laboratory or scientific (participation in conferences, seminars, etc.).
Graduates in Communication Sciences will deepen the knowledge-semiotics, linguistics, informatics, socio-economics, media science, communication, law, historical-political and philosophical acquired during the three-year degree and will further integrate it thanks to the contribution of psychology, neuroscience, philosophy and ethics of communication, philosophy of language and mind.
At the time of obtaining the degree, the student must therefore possess the following skills:
- advanced theoretical and methodological skills, aimed at the analysis in philosophical, linguistic and social perspective of communicative phenomena as regards their origin, their codification, their concrete realisation and their social relevance;
- ability to identify, from a psychological and neuroscientific perspective, the specific cognitive processes underlying communicative phenomena, and mastery of the experimental methods of data collection and analysis needed to develop this type of competence;
- skills necessary for carrying out editorial work in the various media sectors, in international organisations and in public and private bodies;
- skills for designing and managing the software underlying interactive communication processes.
These educational objectives will be achieved through lectures, seminars, laboratory activities and their actual achievement will be verified through the profit exams related to each teaching of the degree course and the approval of the activities carried out during internships and stages, laboratory or scientific activities (participation in conferences, seminars, etc.).
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding: The cognitive science course of study provides skills aimed at dealing productively, adopting a problem-solving perspective, with the fields of application of the cognitive sciences, in particular:
- the ability to conduct basic and applied research activities independently in the field of cognitive science
- the ability to analyse, describe and classify mental processes in their normal functioning and in their pathological alterations.
- the ability to design models and interventions for the reorganisation of the interfaces between humans and complex systems on the basis of neuro-cognitive data.
The ability to apply the knowledge acquired is verified through the profit exams related to the relevant subjects, the evaluation of the laboratory activities, the final evaluation of the internship activity, as well as the individual work carried out by the student under the guidance of a teacher for the preparation and drafting of the final exam.
At the time of graduation, graduates in the Communication Sciences (LM 92) course must be able to apply the skills acquired in the following areas
- the various fields of communication
- experimental research connected with the understanding and explanation of the cognitive processes underlying communication phenomena;
- editorial activities in the various media sectors, in international organisations and in public and private entities;
- the design and management of software underlying interactive communication processes;
- the specific media environments: print media, radio and television formats, Internet, third-generation telephony, press offices, event management.
III - Autonomy of judgement: Graduates of the interclass course will have to show an ability to integrate the high-level skills provided by the course of study with the concrete conditions they will have to face in the relevant operational contexts. They must also show a particular flexibility of judgement that comes from training in different and sometimes competing disciplines. In particular, they must be able to apply standard models of high generality to specific, often individual cases. They must be able to carry out decision-making tasks taking into account the internal and external elements that make the social framework in which an expert in communication and cognitive science operates complex. This complexity derives above all from the heterogeneity of the issues they will be confronted with, which is why strong capacities of autonomy and responsibility of judgement are required, which are correlated above all by the study of disciplines with a high theoretical and reflexive content, such as philosophical knowledge, communication practices, research methodologies and theories of language. Finally, when engaged in scientific research, graduates of the interclass course must possess the ability to reorganise the knowledge acquired on the 'state of the art' around a given thematic field, so as to be able to propose innovative and original solutions.
In order to acquire this fundamental competence, lectures will be accompanied by seminar exercises, scientific writing workshops and participation in scientific conferences and other initiatives will be encouraged. The actual performance of these activities will be verified by means of examinations and ongoing tests.
IV - Communication skills: Graduates of the interclass course must be able to express themselves verbally in a way that is clear, concise and entirely appropriate to the capabilities of the audience, whether they differ in terms of cultural level, geographical location, age, gender, or any other variable that affects the audience's ability to understand. It is also essential to master the use of multimedia languages and all the tools and aids for the representation of ideas: from desktop presentation to audiovisuals, the use of statistical graphics, numerical tables, illustrative or symbolic graphs, illustrations, photographs, films and anything else required by the technology of multimedia representation of knowledge. Graduates must also be able to design expert systems for the applicative implementation of knowledge and for the statistical processing of research data.
In order to acquire this fundamental competence, lectures will be accompanied by seminar exercises and scientific workshops and active participation in scientific conferences and other initiatives will be encouraged. The actual performance of these activities will be verified by means of profit examinations and ongoing tests.
Language/s of instruction and assessment: ENGLISH
V - Learning ability: In general terms for the common area, graduates in 'Cognitive Sciences and Communication Theories' are expected to develop learning skills of an eminently logical-argumentative, experimental, technological and communicational nature.
In particular, students who opt for the LM-55 degree class are required to develop strong skills in understanding and applying scientific literature in the fields of psychology, psychobiology, neuroscience, artificial intelligence, linguistics, philosophy of language, philosophy of mind and phenomenology applied to cognitive science, as well as in participating in laboratory activities. Students intending to pursue their course in the LM-92 degree class will have to acquire research methodologies appropriate to the prevailing studies in this field (critical analysis of communication methods in the advertising, public and private sectors, methodological tools of scientific research, analysis of social contexts, methods of cognitive ergonomics in the production of communications on multimedia platforms, experimental investigation of cognitive skills).
Rather than limiting themselves to applying the skills, knowledge and abilities learnt in the course of study, graduates of the interclass course will have to be able to address the problematic issues that emerge when confronting the disciplinary contents presented, consulting the scientific literature and applying advanced experimental methods in an original way. These skills will also be supported by the students' attendance of seminars of excellence held by foreign visiting professors or their participation in internships and apprenticeships useful, among other things, to ensure the autonomous training necessary to undertake advanced research paths (doctorate, second-level master's degree and postgraduate courses).
Skills related to the function.
Professionals in cognitive science: Knowledge, skills and competences acquired in the fields of cognitive and linguistic rehabilitation, human-computer interface design, artificial intelligence programming, advanced new media technologies, psychological and neuroscientific research, philosophy of mind and cognitive neuroscience.
Professionals in Communication Theory: The knowledge, skills and competences acquired in the course of studies and usually exercised in the work context are of a transversal nature and can be used in various qualified sectors of the world of work. They include:
- mastery of the concepts used in the philosophical, linguistic and sociological theories of information and communication, analysed in terms of their practical application;
- mastery of the tools, languages and information and media technologies used in the management and dissemination of communication and information through different formats and communication channels;
- mastery of the fundamental concepts of the ethical, psychological and neuropsychological disciplines relevant to the genesis, transmission and sharing of communication processes.
Function in the work context.
Professionals in cognitive science: Graduates in cognitive science will be able to:
- exercise functions of responsibility in organisations and management of services directed at the person, groups and communities in both public and private companies, as well as in schools and public administration;
- be employed in all public and private facilities where a cognitive approach to rehabilitation is required, both in terms of theory and application;
- working on the design of software, models, automated systems, platforms and computer interfaces;
- directing and participating in teams at all organisations involved in research in the area of cognitive science and with specific practical applications.
Professionals in Communication Theory: Graduates in Communication Theories will be able to carry out functions of high responsibility in the field of social, journalistic and multimedia communication, the theoretical design of integrated communication environments, with particular reference to the design and implementation of media and multimedia products. They will be able to carry out tasks of responsibility in public and private enterprises operating in the field of the implementation of structured interventions for the dissemination of communication techniques on telematic networks (Internet) and media (radio, television, newspapers, traditional and electronic publishing). They will also be able to carry out tasks of responsibility in the design and programming of thematic content for the services relating to the Web, both in private and public sectors (including, above all, the activities of e-government - or telematic management of the administration - for large state institutions such as ministries, courts, universities, local authorities) in order to enhance the transparency of information processes through the use of professionals in communication theory. In line with the aims of the interclass course, graduates of this pathway will be able to work in corporate structures that are involved in producing user-friendly computer interfaces and platforms.

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [4057] CLASSICAL TRADITION AND ARCHAEOLOGY OF THE MEDITERRANEAN
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10237

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2023.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-2 - Master's Degree in Archaeology.
Interclass Degree: LM-15 - Master's Degree in Philology, Literatures and History of Antiquity.
Inter-University Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: ANCIENT AND MODERN CIVILIZATIONS.
Website: https://tradizione-classica-archeologia-del-mediterraneo.cdl.unime.it/it.
Admission Requirements: Optional qualifications (to be chosen from the following):
[L1+TSS] Bachelor's Degree,
[L2+TSS] First-Level Degree,
[TS] Foreign Qualification,
[TS+TSS] Foreign Qualification,
[TSS+LS] High School Diploma,
[LSS] Specialist Degree.
Roles: Student mobility manager: MARIANGELA PUGLISI.
President: MARCO ONORATO.
Coordinator: MARCO ONORATO.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Archaeologist: Institutions and public bodies dedicated to research, protection, conservation and enhancement of archaeological heritage (Superintendencies and Museums).
Institutions and public, private and third-sector bodies that deal with the study, research and cultural dissemination related to archaeological heritage (Local authorities, Foundations).
To access competitions for the highest levels of management and high responsibility, it is necessary to continue with a post-graduate course consisting of the Specialization School and/or the Doctorate in Archaeology.
Philologist: Cultural institutions; public and private research institutions.
Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance exams for teacher training courses.
Museum curator: Archaeological museums, public and private, in Italy and Europe.
Archaeological sites and parks in Italy and Europe.
Companies that carry out design, maintenance, conservation and promotion activities for cultural and tourist services and events.
Editor of literary, historical and archaeological texts: Newspapers, publishing sector, press offices.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree consists of a public discussion of the thesis, relating to an original work carried out by the student. The thesis should preferably be of an applied, design or experimental nature, from which the Commission can evaluate the cultural and scientific maturity as well as the quality of the work carried out.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Master's Degree Course in Classical Tradition and Archaeology of the Mediterranean, applicants must hold a three-year degree, obtained throughout the national territory, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable by ministerial provisions, provided that they are able to use at least one language of the European Union in addition to Italian and have acquired at least 48 CFU in the following SSDs: L-ANT/01 Prehistory and Protohistory; L-ANT/02 Greek History; L-ANT/03 Roman History; L-ANT/04 Numismatics; L-ANT/05 Papyrology; L-ANT/06 Etruscan and Italic Antiquities; L-ANT/07 Classical Archaeology; L-ANT/08 Christian and Medieval Archaeology; L-ANT/09 Ancient Topography; L-ANT/10 Methodologies of Archaeological Research; L-FIL-LET/02 Greek Language and Literature; L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature; L-FIL-LET/05 Classical Philology; L-FIL-LET/06 Ancient Christian Literature; L-FIL-LET/07 Byzantine Civilization; L-FIL-LET/08 Medieval Latin Literature; L-FIL-LET/10 Italian Literature; L-FIL-LET/13 Philology of Italian Literature; L-LIN/01 Glottology and Linguistics; L-OR/01 History of the Ancient Near East; M-STO/09 Paleography.
For those who wish to enroll in the LM-2 class ("Archaeology of the Mediterranean"), of the 48 required credits, at least 30 must fall within the following SSDs: L-ANT/01 Prehistory and Protohistory; L-ANT/02 Greek History; L-ANT/03 Roman History; L-ANT/04 Numismatics; L-ANT/06 Etruscan and Italic Antiquities; L-ANT/07 Classical Archaeology; L-ANT/08 Christian and Medieval Archaeology; L-ANT/09 Ancient Topography; L-ANT/10 Methodologies of Archaeological Research.
For those who wish to enroll in the LM-15 class ("Classical Tradition"), of the 48 required credits, at least 30 must fall within the following SSDs: L-ANT/02 Greek History; L-ANT/03 Roman History; L-ANT/05 Papyrology; L-FIL-LET/02 Greek Language and Literature; L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature; L-FIL-LET/05 Classical Philology; L-FIL-LET/06 Ancient Christian Literature; L-FIL-LET/07 Byzantine Civilization; L-FIL-LET/08 Medieval Latin Literature; L-FIL-LET/13 Philology of Italian Literature.
The verification of the student's preparation (as per Art. 6, paragraph 2 of D.M. 270/04) is carried out, before enrollment, by a special Commission appointed within the Course Council.
The verification is considered passed for those who have obtained a grade of more than 95/110 in the three-year degree.
In the event that the verification reveals deficiencies in preparation, the Course Council, upon proposal of the Commission, identifies supplementary courses within the Master's degree, depending on the result of the verification of personal preparation, which must in any case lead to the completion of the Master's degree with 120 CFU, without additional training activities.
Specific training objectives: The Master's Degree Course in "Classical Tradition and Archaeology of the Mediterranean" aims to offer students advanced skills and critical processing abilities in the field of historical, philological-literary and archaeological disciplines. The training course is designed to provide students with solid theoretical and methodological foundations, essential for the observation, analysis and reconstruction of ancient societies, in their multiple historical, political, literary, social, economic-productive, monumental and artistic aspects. This will be achieved through the enhancement of the methodologies and techniques of research specific to the literary, historical, philological and archaeological sciences, relating to the ancient and medieval world, with constant attention to direct contact with sources. In-depth knowledge of the literary civilizations of Greece and Rome, ancient history and classical and medieval archaeology will be further enhanced in relation to new perspectives of investigation, such as, for example, the sociological one, the one relating to the history of ideas and the one of technological innovation. Students will be given the opportunity to further enhance the use of all research tools. In order to achieve these objectives, the course provides for a common training program, which covers the entire first year and part of the second, through courses that focus on the areas of classical languages and literatures, ancient history, and archaeology. In the second year, these are supplemented by disciplines that concern the tools and methods of historical, philological and archaeological research. To enrich and refine their cultural and professional profile, students can take advantage of elective credits and internships.
During the training course, students will have the opportunity to:
- carry out a critical reading of texts (literary, epigraphic and papyrological) and archaeological documents (contexts, monuments, works of art, coins and products of material culture) relating to Greek-Roman and medieval culture;
- place these testimonies in their cultural and historical context;
- follow the lines of development of tradition and criticism on these themes;
- acquire the methodologies related to these activities.
Field research activities (archaeological excavations and surveys, archival research and examination of manuscripts kept in public and private libraries) and laboratory and/or internship activities at superintendencies, museums, archives, libraries and publishing houses are also planned.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in CLASSICAL TRADITION AND ARCHAEOLOGY OF THE MEDITERRANEAN.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding: Graduates refine and deepen the linguistic, literary, historical and archaeological knowledge relating to the ancient and medieval world already acquired during the three-year degree, in order to know, understand and interpret correctly the literary, historical and archaeological phenomena relating to those cultures. As regards the field of literary disciplines, one moves from simple texts to more complex texts with the aid of critical editions; from simple metric structures (e.g. hexameter, trimeter iambic) to complex structures such as lyrical meter. As regards historical phenomena, from a general knowledge of events and their interpretation, one moves on to monographic studies that allow the acquisition of more refined methodologies of investigation on the political, institutional, social and economic problems of the Greek and Roman world. All this with the contribution of technical disciplines such as philology and rhetoric, papyrology, epigraphy. In the field of archaeological disciplines, graduates know in detail the development of ancient and medieval archaeology, with particular reference to Sicily and southern Italy. They also acquire the theoretical basis for understanding the meaning that artistic, craft and numismatic documents have in relation to the society that produced them, in terms of their iconography, style and manufacturing techniques. They learn to master the investigation methodologies that are the basis of modern archaeological research: techniques and methods of conducting archaeological excavations, surface surveys, systems for studying and classifying movable artifacts, technical and constructive analysis of monuments and architectural complexes, reconstruction of ancient urban and rural landscapes.
Graduates are thus able to face with conscious maturity problems and themes relating to the ancient world and its influence on medieval and modern culture, open (in line with a more adequate cultural commitment) to inter- and multidisciplinary contexts, also thanks to the carrying out of training internships.
The acquisition of this knowledge and ability takes place through classroom teaching. The acquired skills and personal preparation are verified through oral profit exams, with the contribution of a variety of assessment methods (e.g. reports on cultural activities, written elaborations of individual research with related discussion).
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding: Graduates are able to read critically and interpret the texts and documents through which the culture of the Greek-Roman and medieval world has been transmitted; to correctly identify their context of production; to be able to grasp the constants and changes of literary, cultural, artistic, social, political and economic phenomena, in an inter- and multidisciplinary perspective that allows them to activate a mature and conscious transmission of the heritage elaborated by those cultures and to be able to identify new lines of investigation. Graduates are also able to use the basic theoretical tools of the archaeological disciplines: identify, describe, chronologically and stylistically classify and classify materials and structures relating to the wide chronological range between the Greek and Middle Ages, carry out and conduct field research (archaeological excavations, surface surveys with related documentation), assess the need to resort to scientific investigation techniques (archaeometric, anthropological, paleozoological, paleoecological analyses) and to proceed with the reconstruction of ancient landscapes. The specific laboratory, internship and training activities organized by the course of study constitute the necessary teaching tools for achieving the expected results.
The ability to apply knowledge and understanding is exercised and acquired during seminars, carried out by individual students or small groups, on specific topics, during which the student is directly encouraged to make a personal contribution. The ability to apply knowledge and understanding is verified through oral profit exams.
III - Autonomy of judgment: The graduate is able to carry out an autonomous re-elaboration of the acquired knowledge and the personal interpretation of cultural and historical phenomena, based on a solid awareness of the acquisitions made by modern criticism. Graduates master the numerous themes relating to their field of study; they demonstrate awareness of the problems connected to them; they elaborate autonomous judgments on texts and documents, aware of the context in which these were born and spread. Graduates are also able to elaborate personal reflections on the various aspects relating to professional ethics and the social and ethical responsibilities that the correct practical application of the acquired knowledge entails.
Autonomy of judgment develops through the interaction between the activities provided for in the training program, to which seminar and laboratory experiences are added. The degree of development is verified on the occasion of the training activities and assessment tests (exams, preparation and discussion of the final thesis).
IV - Communication skills: Graduates possess a strong ability to communicate problems, interpretations and methodologies adopted, with clarity, linguistic propriety and effectiveness (modulated according to the different contexts in which communication takes place); this both in written and oral communication. They also have the ability to interact constructively with each other and to be able to work in a group.
Communication skills are developed through the interaction between the activities provided for in the training program, to which seminar and laboratory experiences are added. The degree of development is verified on the occasion of the training activities and assessment tests (exams, preparation and discussion of the final thesis).
Language/s of instruction and assessment: ITALIAN.
V - Learning ability: During the course of study, graduates develop and refine learning skills and methodologies useful for establishing a practice of continuous and permanent self-learning that allows them to renew and deepen their knowledge without difficulty, even after the Master's degree program, also with a view to subsequent studies that they can face with full autonomy.
Learning skills are developed through the interaction between the activities provided for in the training program, to which seminar and laboratory experiences are added. The degree of development is verified on the occasion of the training activities and assessment tests (exams, preparation and discussion of the final thesis).
Skills related to the function:
Archaeologist: skills useful for understanding and solving problems related to research practice (archaeological excavation, drawing, surveying, cataloging, bibliographic and iconographic research); skills useful for the management and conservation of archaeological heritage; appropriate technological skills for the current world of work; ability to disseminate their knowledge.
Philologist: Mastery of the Greek and Latin language and literature; ability to read texts of ancient (papyrus and epigraphs) and medieval (codices) tradition; ability to disseminate their knowledge.
Museum curator: cultural, methodological and technical skills suitable for the study, classification, protection and enhancement of historical, archaeological and artistic assets; creative and design skills aimed at the organization and communication of exhibitions, scientific dissemination and sector communication.
Editor of literary, historical and archaeological texts: mastery of the cultural, linguistic-literary, social and material evolution of the ancient world.
Function in the working context: Archaeologist: Archaeologist, art historian, technical-scientific operator in the field of cultural heritage. He/she deals with: identifying, recognizing and analyzing cultural heritage; carrying out topographical research, archaeological excavations, cataloging; protecting and enhancing contexts, sites, monuments and archaeological assets; collaborating in the care of museums, collections and exhibitions of archaeological assets; promoting the dissemination of cultural heritage.
Philologist: Philologist and linguist: deals with the study of texts; addresses the issues related to tradition, editorial issues, context of production and influence; promotes the dissemination of cultural heritage.
Museum curator: deals with protecting, studying, preserving and enhancing the historical, archaeological and artistic heritage kept in museums and public or private institutions, archaeological sites and parks, also taking care of the organization and promotion of exhibitions, scientific dissemination and sector communication.
Editor of literary, historical and archaeological texts: develops and manages editorial projects, prepares and revises technical and popular texts, deals with the literary and historical-archaeological sector in publishing houses.

-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [9219] BIOLOGY AND ECOLOGY OF THE COASTAL MARINE ENVIRONMENT
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10029

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2020.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian and English.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulations: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-6 - Master's Degree in Biology.
Inter-university Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: DEPARTMENT OF CHEMICAL, BIOLOGICAL, PHARMACEUTICAL AND ENVIRONMENTAL SCIENCES.
Website: https://biologia-ecologia-dell-ambiente-marino-costiero.cdl.unime.it
Admission Requirements: Optional qualifications (choose from the following):
[L2+TSS] First-level Degree, High School Diploma [DU+TSS] University Diploma, High School Diploma [L1+TSS] Degree, High School Diploma [TS] Foreign Qualification [TS+TSS] Foreign Qualification, High School Diploma [TSS+LS] High School Diploma, Specialist Degree.
Roles: President: ALESSIA CATERINA GIANNETTO. Coordinator: ALESSIA CATERINA GIANNETTO.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Biologist and Marine Biologist. The training acquired will enable graduates to carry out professional activities recognized by current regulations as the skills of the professional figure of the biologist (Section A of the professional register) and in all the specific fields of application as indicated in paragraph 1 of Article 31 of Decree 328 of June 5, 2001 (supplement to the Official Gazette 190 of June 17, 2001) and in Law 396 of May 24, 1967, on the organization of the profession of biologist, after passing the state exam and registration with the National Order of Biologists. Graduates will be able to work in public (municipalities, provinces and regions) and private administrations, in other public bodies and private companies, in the management of marine parks and protected areas, in public and private research laboratories dealing with marine organisms, marine pollution, fisheries and aquaculture management, environmental impact assessment, etc.
The course prepares for the professions of:
Marine Bioecologist - Expert in the monitoring and protection of the coastal marine environment and its resources, within the context of current Community and national legislation.
Biologist responsible for the management of marine parks and protected areas - Expert in the management of marine protected areas, with expertise in the protection and control of marine animal and plant species, brackish water environments and transition environments, capable of consistently applying current Community and national regulations on the matter.
Biologist responsible for the management of aquaculture and mariculture plants - Designs and manages farms of marine species in in-shore, off-shore and lagoon environments. Deals with reproductive cycles, feed and the maintenance of optimal water quality and the final product, within the context of current Community and national legislation on aquaculture, responsible for aquaculture and mariculture plants, specialist technician in aquaculture, operator in aquaculture, fish farmer, fish breeder.
Characteristics of the final exam.
To be admitted to the final exam, the student must have obtained the credits provided for in the Study Regulations, with the exception of those assigned to the final exam (20 CFU).
The final exam consists of a discussion, in a public session, of a thesis with an exclusively experimental character, which makes an original contribution, developed under the guidance of a supervisor appointed by the Degree Course at the student's request. For those who have attended the Marine Production curriculum, the thesis must be written and presented in English.
The experimental activity contributes to the training of the marine biologist, completing the knowledge acquired during the course of study with the aim of developing autonomy in research, critical ability in the analysis and evaluation of experimental data, as well as skills in the experimental practices related to biological research applied to the sea, its resources and its conservation.
During the discussion of the thesis, the student must also demonstrate that he has thoroughly assimilated the topics developed during the course of study in order to be able to play a leading role in the activities for which he is called upon.
Knowledge required for access.
Those who have obtained a first-level degree in Biological Sciences, Class L-13 (DM 270/04) or Class 12 (DM 509/99), at any Italian university can access the Master's Degree course in Biology and Ecology of the Coastal Marine Environment.
Those who have another qualification obtained in Italy or abroad, recognized as suitable, as part of an educational path that provides for the acquisition of a sufficient number of CFU obtained in scientific-disciplinary sectors deemed essential for learning the knowledge of the second-level course, can also access. In particular, the student, at the time of the interview, must demonstrate that he has knowledge in basic disciplines such as mathematics, computer science, physics, chemistry and biological disciplines that provide knowledge on the organization of the animal and plant world and on the principles of ecology.
The minimum requirements are as follows:
-MAT (from MAT/01 to MAT/09) - INF/01: 8 CFU
-FIS (from FIS/01 to FIS/08): 6 CFU
-CHIM (CHIM/01, CHIM/02, CHIM/03, CHIM/06): 10 CFU
-BIO (from BIO/01 to BIO/19): 36 CFU. The verification of the student's preparation (as per Article 6, paragraph 2 of DM 270/04) is carried out, before enrollment, by a special Commission appointed by the Course Council.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course in BIOLOGY AND ECOLOGY OF THE COASTAL MARINE ENVIRONMENT aims to train professionals in the field of biology and ecology of the coastal marine environment with specific skills and design capabilities in the monitoring, management and protection, from a biological and ecological point of view, of coastal marine environments, including brackish and transition environments, as well as in the management of aquaculture plants, aquariums, marine parks and protected areas.
To achieve these objectives, the degree course provides, in addition to classroom lectures, specialized seminars, laboratory and field activities, the latter including the sampling procedures used for research in the sea.
Graduates of the course must therefore have acquired: a) a solid cultural preparation in basic biology and a high scientific and operational preparation in the various sectors of biology applied to the sea, with a high scientific and operational preparation in the disciplines that characterize the class and in particular those with more strictly marine biology and ecology content; b) operational, application and management skills in the areas of interest of the biology and ecology of marine environments with particular regard to coastal and transition environments (lagoons, coastal lakes, etc.), aquaculture plants, marine parks and protected areas, as well as marine biotechnologies in general; c) a good mastery of the scientific method of investigation and the main methodologies used for the study, monitoring and protection of coastal environments and for environmental impact assessment; d) a good basic knowledge of the experimental methodologies and technologies that can be used in marine biology and ecology and in environmental impact assessment; e) knowledge of the mathematical and computer tools used for support; f) a good knowledge of the English language in written and spoken form, with particular reference to disciplinary lexicons.
To achieve these objectives, the degree course provides, in addition to classroom lectures, specialized seminars, laboratory and field activities, the latter including the sampling procedures used for research in the sea.
Graduates of the course must, finally, be able to work with a high degree of autonomy, even assuming managerial roles and responsibility for projects, structures and personnel.
The first year of the course involves the acquisition of general knowledge of marine ecology on aquatic organisms. The second year has been deliberately divided into two curricula: Ecological, aimed at the protection and conservation of the marine environment, and Marine Production, in English, aimed at the exploitation of marine resources and providing scientific support for aquaculture production.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in BIOLOGY AND ECOLOGY OF THE COASTAL MARINE ENVIRONMENT. Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding. During the first year, common path, the student acquires specialized skills: a solid cultural preparation in basic biology and a high scientific and operational preparation in the various sectors of biology applied to the sea and in the disciplines that characterize the class and in particular in those with more strictly marine biology and ecology content; operational, application and management skills in the areas of interest of the biology and ecology of marine environments with particular regard to coastal and transition environments (lagoons, coastal lakes, etc.).
The second year is divided into two curricula: "Marine Production" and "Ecological". The Marine Production curriculum, delivered entirely in English, allows the student to acquire skills useful for working in aquaculture plants and marine production in a broad sense, including algae. The Ecological curriculum, on the other hand, allows for professional placement in marine parks and protected areas. In general, the course guarantees the acquisition of a good mastery of the scientific method of investigation and the main methodologies used for the study, monitoring and protection of coastal environments and for environmental impact assessment, as well as in marine biotechnologies in general; a good basic knowledge of the experimental methodologies and technologies that can be used in marine biology and ecology and in environmental impact assessment; knowledge of the mathematical and computer tools used for support; knowledge of environmental law; a good knowledge of the Italian and English disciplinary lexicons.
A significant amount of space is reserved for elective training activities consistent with the general educational path, as well as for language skills, provided in the first year and preparatory for the Marine Production curriculum, which must obligatorily lead graduates to achieve level B2 in English. It should be noted that language skills are provided in the first year and are preparatory for attending the lessons in the second year of the Marine Production curriculum, which are entirely in English.
Both curricula provide for CFU for further application knowledge and thesis work and final exam.
Knowledge is acquired through lectures, monographic seminars, field and laboratory activities.
The achievement of the objectives will be assessed through oral exams, practical tests, ex ante, in itinere and ex post evaluation, through the evaluation of written papers by students for the evaluation of the independence acquired in laboratory activities and finally through the evaluation of the final thesis.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. The graduate in Biology and Ecology of the Coastal Marine Environment will be able to apply the basic analytical, microbiological, diagnostic and toxicological methodologies in the research and study of the marine environment with two specific professional areas: exploitation of resources (Marine Production Curriculum) and conservation and protection of the aquatic environment (Ecological Curriculum). The ability to apply the acquired knowledge will be supported, in addition to frontal lessons, by the considerable amount of practical laboratory and field activities, as well as by internships in affiliated companies or research institutions, both Italian and foreign.
The achievement of the training objectives will be demonstrated by the final exam with the drafting of a written paper and its public discussion.
III - Autonomy of judgment. The Master's graduate has acquired autonomy of judgment which allows him to interpret the experimental data of research designed and conducted with scientific rigor.
He is able to identify new development perspectives/strategies, to evaluate, interpret and rework literature data, to apply the principles of professional ethics.
He has the ability to formulate judgments on the environmental quality and the productive potential of marine environments as well as to evaluate environmental impacts, on experimental data detected in the field and/or obtained through laboratory investigations and on the basis of current international and national legislation.
Autonomy of judgment is developed through comparison with teachers, critical discussion of scientific articles, exchange of project ideas with teachers, experts and colleagues during laboratory activities, also carried out for the preparation of the final thesis.
This autonomy is verified by evaluating individual work skills, even within a group, during internship and laboratory activities.
IV - Communication skills. Graduates in Biology and Ecology of the Coastal Marine Environment are able to:
1) communicate clearly to others what they have learned during the course of study, using appropriate disciplinary vocabulary.
2) elaborate, represent and disseminate experimental data, ideas, information using computer systems.
3) work in a group.
4) develop/present research projects, lead research groups and illustrate the results of the research.
5) support, on solid cultural bases, discussions with specialist and non-specialist interlocutors, on topics, problems and ideas relating to their theoretical and practical knowledge.
These skills are developed through the presentation in the classroom of data obtained during activities carried out in the laboratory and in the field, through the critical discussion of bibliographic data and the preparation of reports during in-progress tests.
The English disciplinary vocabulary is deepened in the second year in the Marine Production curriculum, which is delivered entirely in English. In addition, the English language is used by students through the understanding and reworking of scientific articles proposed by the teachers or independently found.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN, ENGLISH.
V - Learning ability. The graduate acquires adequate skills for the continuous development and deepening of the knowledge and skills acquired during the course of study. He is able to consult databases, specialist texts and bibliographic material also using computer tools. He is able to learn innovative technologies, to apply study methods, to work for objectives both independently and in groups, also through computer tools, power point presentations, case solving and video conferences.
The achievement of this objective is achieved not only through the work carried out for the preparation of the experimental thesis, under the guidance of the tutor/teacher, but also through the continuous updating of the knowledge acquired in the individual course lessons, through the collection, analysis and reworking of recent bibliographic data. The ability to learn is assessed through in-progress tests and oral exams.
Skills related to the function.
Biologist and Marine Biologist. The course provides training that can be used in the first years of employment in the world of work in order to be able to work as: Expert in the monitoring and protection of the coastal marine environment and its resources, within the context of current Community and national legislation. Expert in the management of marine protected areas, with expertise in the protection and control of marine animal and plant species, brackish water environments and transition environments, capable of consistently applying current Community and national regulations on the matter. Aquaculture expert, designs and manages farms of marine species in in-shore, off-shore and lagoon environments, deals with reproductive cycles, feed and the maintenance of optimal water quality and the final product, within the context of current Community and national legislation on aquaculture, responsible for aquaculture and mariculture plants, specialist technician in aquaculture, operator in aquaculture, fish farmer, fish breeder.
Function in the work context.
Biologist and Marine Biologist. The Master's graduate in Biology and Ecology of the Coastal Marine Environment must possess operational, application and management skills in the areas of interest of the biology and ecology of marine environments in general and Mediterranean in particular, as well as brackish, coastal and transition environments, and aquaculture plants.
In the design of the Master's Degree Course in Biology and Ecology of the Coastal Marine Environment, the English curriculum "Marine Production" was included to meet the increasingly pressing demands of external stakeholders, including European ones. In fact, a professional figure was identified that would reflect the advancement of scientific knowledge in the sciences of the sea, in ecological sustainability and in environmental quality.
The included disciplines are essential to deepen the biological and ecological knowledge of the sea area most subject to anthropogenic impact and aim to develop a good mastery of the scientific method of investigation with the main methodologies aimed at the study, monitoring of coastal environments and the assessment of environmental impact.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Mathematician. The employment opportunities and professional activities of Master's graduates in Mathematics are both in research, possibly attending second-level Master's degrees or doctoral programs, and in the world of work, performing highly responsible functions in the construction and development of mathematical and computational models of various kinds, in different application areas, in services and in the public administration, as well as in the fields of mathematical and scientific communication.
Mathematicians can find work as experts in:
- companies and businesses to support the simulation of industrial productions (e.g., in microelectronics) or in the optimization of production processes;
- healthcare companies (e.g., in diagnostic support through "tomographic image analysis") or in quality control processes;
- public administrations to support decision-making processes;
- financial and insurance institutions;
- information technology, scientific communication, publishing.
Graduates who have the credits required by current regulations may participate in the entrance tests for teacher training courses for primary and secondary schools.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam consists of a discussion, in a public session, of a thesis of a research or experimental nature, developed under the guidance of a supervisor appointed by the course council. The candidate must demonstrate that they have developed the topic in an original, in-depth and autonomous manner, as a final stage of the chosen training course.
Knowledge required for admission.
Admission to the Master's Degree Course is subject to the possession of a Bachelor's degree, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable in accordance with current laws and in the forms provided for in Article 22, paragraph 4, of the University's didactic regulations.
Students who:
1) hold a Bachelor's degree in class L-35 according to D.M. 270/2004, or a qualification equivalent to it, according to current regulations;
or
2) hold a degree, at least a first-level degree, in a class other than that indicated in point 1) and can document a study curriculum, accompanied by the course programs, from which it appears that they have acquired at least:
12 CFU in the SSD MAT/02;
18 CFU in the SSD MAT/03;
18 CFU in the SSD MAT/05;
6 CFU in the SSD MAT/07;
6 CFU in the SSD MAT/08;
6 CFU in the SSD INF/01 or ING-INF/05;
6 CFU in any of the SSD FIS/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08;
knowledge of the English language, written and spoken, at least at level B1.
The assessment of the possession of the requirements set out in point 2), necessary for admission, is carried out by the Didactic Commission of the Master's Degree Course in Mathematics. The documentation to be submitted to the Didactic Commission must be accompanied by the course programs of all the subjects taken in the aforementioned scientific-disciplinary sectors. Students who do not have the qualification referred to in point 1) may be enrolled, subject to obtaining the required qualification by the date set annually by the University. The conditions required in point 2) remain for these students.
In any case, a verification of the personal preparation of the applicant is foreseen for admission to the Master's Degree Course, with methods that will be appropriately defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course. In the event that a lack of necessary knowledge is found for admission, the acquisition of the same will be required in order to allow enrollment.
The Didactic Commission also expresses its opinion on the total or partial recognition of the credits already acquired by the student in other Master's degree courses.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course in Mathematics aims to train graduates who:
- know and understand advanced concepts of Mathematics;
- possess high computational skills;
- demonstrate skills in mathematical reasoning, providing rigorous proofs;
- are able to understand and propose mathematical models suitable for describing phenomena in various disciplines;
- possess high skills for the communication of mathematical problems and their solutions to a specialized audience.
Students are encouraged to apply complex and rigorous skills acquired to concrete mathematical models. They will play a primary role in the transmission of mathematical knowledge in the school environment, both primary and secondary, a role that, if underestimated, may lead over time to the decline of scientific thinking.
Graduates in the Master's degree course:
- are able to master a mathematical language suitable for proposing mathematical models of real situations to society;
- are able to disseminate results of great mathematical interest, making them accessible to understanding;
- are able to lead working groups on the development of engineering, computer science and applied science projects, demonstrating communication skills in the mathematical support of the project.
These skills can be achieved at the end of the training course, as a result of the contents of the courses offered. Some courses will provide for the presentation of in-depth topics through seminars or written reports, requiring students to develop expository skills, both written and oral.
The use of texts in English and the presence of a language center at the university will allow students to achieve the objective of communicating at an adequate level.
In order to achieve the objectives set out, the training course provides all the knowledge necessary to achieve them and provides for the completion and acquisition of more specific skills in the field of mathematical scientific-disciplinary sectors, useful both for the continuation of studies (doctorate, second-level master's degrees, specialization schools) and for entry into the world of work. The training courses include in-depth courses dedicated to the study of advanced topics in the area of interest. The completion of the common offer is achieved with related and integrative subjects and optional subjects, which must be consistent with the chosen training course. The preparation acquired in related and integrative subjects and in the optional activities will give the possibility of interacting with graduates in other sectors, as well as with experts in fields not necessarily academic. Some disciplines include laboratory activities, in particular dedicated to the knowledge of computer applications, programming languages and calculation. In addition, external activities may be planned, such as training internships in companies and laboratories, and study stays at other Italian and European universities, also within the framework of international agreements.
The Master's Degree Course in Mathematics is structured in two distinct curricula that emphasize the two fundamental aspects of mathematics. Specifically:
THEORETICAL CURRICULUM: emphasizes the theoretical aspect and methodological rigor. It aims at acquiring specific, high-level mathematical techniques, not necessarily accessible to non-experts, and aims at training scholars who are capable of a high level of abstraction in proposing mathematical concepts and problems.
APPLIED CURRICULUM: emphasizes the use of analytical, numerical, modeling methodologies to address the numerous and important applications of mathematics to the fields of physics, economics, statistics, discrete computation, biology, social sciences and the study of engineering problems.
The diversification into two curricula expresses the desire to offer the Master's graduate, in addition to the advantage of a more marked basic preparation, specific knowledge on advanced and innovative sectors of the area, useful both for his entry into the world of work and for further continuation towards research activities. The training courses offered highlight the two fundamental aspects of mathematics, linked to each other, the highly theoretical one and the one aimed at acquiring specific techniques, of high mathematical level to address the numerous and important applications of mathematics.
In addition to the linguistic knowledge acquired during the Bachelor's degree course, further linguistic skills are provided to help the student have a fluent knowledge of the English language.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in MATHEMATICS Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding Master's graduates:
- are able to understand the rigorous methods and languages of theoretical mathematics in its various articulations;
- possess mathematical skills to understand the modern formulation of mathematical programs of Italian and foreign public and research bodies;
- have the tools to understand the recent results of mathematical research;
- are able to model mathematical situations and phenomena in the real world;
- possess a good knowledge of the methodologies of scientific research in the mathematical field, both theoretical and applied.
The achievement of these skills is achieved through lectures and in-depth activities on topics related to and directly related to the courses. Exercise courses, tutoring activities will be prepared to fill and improve any inadequacies in the acquired preparation. The theoretical path emphasizes the abstract aspect and methodological rigor. The applied path emphasizes the use of analytical, numerical, modeling methodologies. In some laboratories, the acquired skills will be systematically verified. In parallel with learning, participation in conferences, summer schools on mathematical topics or applications of mathematics, will refine the rigorous exposure, also in non-Italian language, as well as stimulate curiosity towards other aspects of mathematics, not necessarily in the university field.
The course of study evaluates the acquisition of the aforementioned skills through the following teaching tools: in-course tests, seminars held by the student, final exams which in some cases provide for a written test and laboratory activities.

- are able to achieve mastery of research programs used in private and public bodies.
The achievement of the indicated skills is achieved through the institutional courses, in which the autonomous solution of problems related to these courses is required. The development of the thesis is in itself a first introduction to research activity. The theoretical path emphasizes the abstract aspect and methodological rigor. In parallel with learning, participation in conferences, summer schools on mathematical topics or applications of mathematics, will refine the rigorous exposure, also in non-Italian language, as well as stimulate curiosity towards other aspects of mathematics, not necessarily in the university field.

III - Autonomy of judgment Master's graduates:
- have the ability to judge, evaluate, independently process scientific knowledge circulating in society;
- are able to independently make decisions about didactic, scientific, research, theoretical and experimental projects;
- have the ability to discern mathematical results and are able to propose them to society in current and interesting elaborations;

Some courses provide for the development of reports, in-depth studies, individually or in groups, in order to give space to autonomous ideas and, at the same time, to encourage the student to make their skills interact with those of other colleagues, gradually with more determination. Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN
V - Learning ability Master's graduates, having acquired specific mathematical preparation, possess excellent ability to learn the most modern scientific knowledge, not only in the mathematical field, but also in other fields such as physics, engineering, statistics and computer science.
These skills are provided by the basic training course, complete from the point of view of the contents, and by the request for internship or training activities in public and private environments, by the request for computer skills, essential for facing the world of work.
The course of study evaluates the acquisition of the aforementioned skills through the following teaching tools: in-course tests, seminars held by the student, final exams which in some cases provide for a written test and laboratory activities. The aforementioned skills are also verified through the evaluation of the activities required for the preparation of the final exam.
Skills related to the function.
Mathematician. Graduates in the Master's degree course acquire skills of abstraction and generalization, combined with mastery of advanced mathematical techniques. The transversality of mathematics will allow them to propose mathematical models suitable for describing real situations and providing solutions.
The use of mathematical models and their simulation also through the use of computational tools will allow them to perform important functions in engineering and applied science projects, where direct experimentation is expensive or difficult.
Function in a work context.
Mathematician. The professional figure of a Mathematician, with a solid knowledge of advanced concepts of Mathematics and computational tools, will be able to build, analyze and apply mathematical models in various fields, both industrial and managerial. The Master's graduate will be able to assume coordination and responsibility roles both in the public administration and in the private sector. Due to his skills, the mathematician will be able to perform high-level functions in multidisciplinary teams.
