-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [9224] CHEMISTRY
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10033

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2020.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-54 - Class of Master's Degrees in Chemical Sciences.
Inter-university Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: DEPARTMENT OF CHEMICAL, BIOLOGICAL, PHARMACEUTICAL AND ENVIRONMENTAL SCIENCES.
Website: https://lm-chimica.cdl.unime.it.
Admission Requirements: Optional qualifications (choose from the following):
[L2+TSS] First-level degree, Upper Secondary School Certificate [TSS+L1] Upper Secondary School Certificate, Degree [TS] Foreign qualification [TS-TSS] Foreign qualification, Upper Secondary School Certificate [TSS+LS] Upper Secondary School Certificate, Specialist Degree.
Roles: President: CARMELA ARENA, Coordinator: CARMELA ARENA.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
A professional figure capable of operating at a managerial level in laboratories, facilities, and public and private companies. The employment opportunities for a Chemistry graduate are:
- Public and private research institutions.
- Analytical, control and quality certification laboratories.
- Public and/or private bodies and companies.
- National or international professional companies and consulting firms.
- Industries and work environments that require high qualifications. Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas will be able to participate in the entrance exams for teacher training courses, as required by current legislation.
The graduate can take the state exam to become a licensed chemist and register with the Order of Chemists and Physicists.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree consists of a public discussion of the thesis, relating to an original work carried out by the student. The thesis should preferably be of an applied, design or experimental nature, and consists of an original written paper (in Italian or English) from which the cultural and scientific maturity of the graduate, the ability to understand the theoretical and experimental basis of the subject matter, the ability to use the equipment and the ability to process the experimental data obtained, emerge.
Knowledge required for admission.
To be admitted to the Master's Degree Course in Chemistry, the following are required:
- possession of a three-year degree in Chemistry or Industrial Chemistry of classes 21 ex D.M. 509/99 and L-27 ex D.M. 270/24 obtained throughout the national territory or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, which guarantees direct access to the Master's Degree course, or
- possession of a degree obtained in other scientific or technological classes with the acquisition of: at least 12 CFU in total in the FIS/01-08 and MAT/01-09 sectors; at least 50 CFU in total in the characterizing sectors of the course (CHIM/01; CHIM/02; CHIM/03; CHIM/04; CHIM/06), with adequate laboratory exercises.
The verification of the student's preparation (as per Art. 6, paragraph 2 of D.M. 270/04) is carried out, after enrollment, by a Commission appointed within the Course Council and will take place according to the provisions of Art. 5 of the Course Regulations.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course in Chemistry aims to train a professional who, possessing the basic knowledge acquired during the three-year degree course, has further deepened his/her knowledge in the areas that characterize the discipline. The Master's graduate will develop a flexible but rigorous mindset, useful and necessary to be able to work in jobs that require adequate familiarity with the scientific method, the ability to design and apply innovative methods and techniques, as well as the critical use of complex equipment. The acquired skills will allow the Master's graduate to adapt to the evolution of the discipline and to deal with competence and autonomy with both professional and academic and industrial research activities.
The didactic organization provides for a common path with the aim of completing the basic training acquired with the Bachelor's Degree and introducing the most advanced specialized knowledge in the areas that characterize the discipline. The course is then divided into curricula that allow the acquisition of personal specialization objectives in different areas such as analytical-environmental, industrial and molecular materials.
All curricula are completed by a course in English, by activities chosen independently by the student, by a practical training and by the development of an experimental thesis. The number of credits assigned to the latter ensures the development of an experimental thesis of considerable scope, which allows the student to apply the cultural knowledge acquired to the solution of experimental problems with good levels of originality.
The training activities include: lectures, laboratories, seminars, conferences, internships, courses, also held at suitable public and/or private institutions in Italy and abroad, recognized by the Course Council, which ensure computer, linguistic and cultural skills consistent with the themes of the Course itself.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in CHEMISTRY. Dublin descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding. Graduates acquire knowledge and understanding through the deepening of the disciplines that characterize the course with the aim of completing the basic training acquired with the Bachelor's Degree and introducing the most advanced specialized knowledge in the different disciplinary areas: Analytical Chemistry, Physical Chemistry, Inorganic Chemistry, Organic Chemistry and Industrial Chemistry. Within the chosen curriculum, they acquire further specific skills in the analytical-environmental field, in the synthesis and characterization of molecular materials, in the study and optimization of industrial processes. Graduates are also able to use scientific English both in written and spoken form.
To develop this knowledge and skills, lectures, exercises in the classroom and laboratory exercises will be used, as well as seminars on cutting-edge topics in the chosen field of study and through the use of advanced textbooks. The achievement of the results is verified through written and/or oral exams and through the oral presentation of the experimental work of the thesis at the final exam.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. The Master's graduate in Chemistry is able to:
- apply his/her knowledge and skills to the solution of complex problems in a multidisciplinary context related to the chemical sciences;
- use the knowledge acquired in the workplace, interacting with other professionals;
- critically learn advanced topics relating to the issues connected with the chemical sciences, in particular focusing on the field of analytical-environmental chemistry, molecular materials and industrial chemistry. The ability to apply knowledge and understanding is acquired through the different types of teaching activities: lectures, exercises in the classroom, laboratory exercises. This ability will be enhanced during the practical training and the thesis. The number of credits assigned to the latter ensures the development of an experimental thesis of considerable scope, which allows the student to apply the cultural knowledge acquired to the solution of experimental problems and to refine his/her ability to plan and carry out an experiment under the guidance of a lecturer from the University.
III - Autonomy of judgment. The Master's graduate has acquired the ability:
- to critical thinking and is able to carry out scientific research independently in the chosen field, identifying the most appropriate methods to address problems in the field of analytical and environmental chemistry, materials science and industrial chemistry;
- to design and conduct experiments, interpret the results and devise the subsequent development of the experimentation in order to acquire knowledge and/or optimize methods or processes in a context of scientific and applied research;
- to work in a group;
- to adapt to different work environments and topics;
- to find and evaluate sources of information, databases, literature, etc.
The acquisition of autonomy of judgment is guaranteed within the specific training activities. In particular, the exercises and laboratory activities offer opportunities to develop the ability to work in groups as well as decision-making and judgment skills, while the privileged teaching tool is the significant practical training and thesis on an original research topic.
These skills will be verified in the discussion of the laboratory reports, during the practical training and the presentation of the thesis.
IV - Communication skills. The Master's graduate is able to:
- communicate in writing and orally, in Italian and English, also using multimedia systems;
- communicate, in a clear and unambiguous way, information, scientific data and conclusions to specialist and non-specialist interlocutors, also through the preparation of written reports, presenting the experimental data in the form of diagrams, tables and graphs;
- work in a group, even interdisciplinary, operate with a high degree of autonomy and quickly integrate into work environments. These skills are developed through the various training activities and their acquisition is evaluated primarily during the tests, which mainly consist of oral exams, written tests and laboratory reports, but also in the activities of participation in working groups set up within experimental courses and in the practical training and thesis activities. These skills are further refined in the preparation of the thesis and the final dissertation, also through the use of multimedia systems.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN. V - Learning ability. The graduate has the ability to update his/her scientific and professional training in all areas of chemistry and its applications. He/she is able to effectively integrate the knowledge acquired by identifying the textbooks, scientific literature and other materials useful for further study. He/she also has the ability to read and learn in English.
The ability to learn is monitored during the course of study through examination tests that focus on the knowledge to be acquired through independent study.
Skills related to the function.
A professional figure capable of operating at a managerial level in laboratories, facilities, and public and private companies. The graduate has acquired in-depth knowledge in the field of chemical sciences. He/she is able to synthesize and characterize new products and new materials, using innovative procedures and state-of-the-art equipment. He/she knows how to evaluate the best methodology for solving analytical and environmental problems, for the analysis of complex matrices, for the determination of molecular structures, applying modern instrumental techniques. He/she is able to develop and optimize industrial chemical production processes with particular attention to environmental sustainability. He/she is able to interpret and rationalize scientific data in an organic way.
Function in a work context.
A professional figure capable of operating at a managerial level in laboratories, facilities, and public and private companies. The graduate carries out research, control and analysis activities on natural or synthetic substances, identifies their composition and chemical and energy variations, identifies and applies investigation methods, formulates theories and laws based on observations; improves substances and synthesizes new ones, produces innovative materials. He/she uses and transfers knowledge in industry, medicine, pharmacology and other sectors of production. He/she carries out expert opinions, consultancy and advice on safety, quality, chemical analysis, industrial plants. He/she may also be involved in teaching and scientific dissemination activities.

COURSE: [9232] PHYSICS
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS

INFO ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years.
Program: 2020.
Status: Open.
Credits: 120 CFU.
Language: Italian, English.
Type: Master's Degree.
Regulations: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: LM-17 - Master's Degree in Physics.
Inter-university Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: MESSINA.
Department: DEPARTMENT OF MATHEMATICAL AND COMPUTER SCIENCES, PHYSICAL SCIENCES AND EARTH SCIENCES.
Website: https://physics.cdl.unime.it.
Admission Requirements:

Coordinator: GIUSEPPE MANDAGLIO.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Physicists, Researchers and technicians with degrees in physics, Physics and nuclear technicians, Physics teachers.
Some examples of career opportunities are:
- research and development sectors in technologically advanced industries, with particular regard to electronics, optics and optoelectronics;
- scientific research at universities and public and private research institutions;
- the transfer of knowledge and technological know-how developed in the field of basic research to the economic and production system;
- radiation protection, medical diagnostics and therapy as well as the acquisition and processing of environmental data;
- environmental monitoring in agencies responsible for this purpose;
- the use of information technologies in technical-commercial sectors of the tertiary sector;
- the creation and use of models of complex reality in areas other than scientific ones (banks, financial companies, consulting firms);
- teaching and dissemination of scientific culture at a high level.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam consists of the presentation and discussion before a special commission of a thesis developed under the guidance of a supervisor and critically reviewed by a co-supervisor. The topic of the thesis will deal with a research topic in the field of physics containing original contributions from the candidate, developed within the activities of the Department.
The score of the degree exam takes into account the entire training course, in terms of expected results and objectives achieved, as well as the quality and presentation of the work carried out by the candidate. In the evaluation of the thesis work, participation in international mobility activities and in curricular internship activities at companies, public or private bodies is also considered favorably.
Knowledge required for access.
To be admitted to the Master's Degree Course in Physics, the following are required:
- possession of a three-year degree in the class L-30 'Science and Technology Physics' or in class 25 according to DM 509/99 or other qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, which guarantees direct access to the Master's Degree course;
or
- possession of another three-year degree with the achievement of a minimum number of credits in the following scientific disciplines: minimum 28 CFU in mathematical and computer disciplines (SSD: from MAT/01 to MAT/09, INF/01, ING-INF/05);
minimum 40 CFU in physics disciplines in the experimental field (SSD FIS/01, FIS/07); minimum 35 CFU in physics disciplines in other areas (SSD from FIS/02 to FIS/06, FIS/08).
The verification of the student's preparation (ex art. 6, paragraph 2 of DM 270/04) is carried out, after enrollment, by a special Commission appointed within the Course Council.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course in Physics aims to train graduates with solid knowledge in the characterizing areas in order to achieve that flexible but rigorous mindset that allows direct access to work activities that require in-depth knowledge of the main physical theories and the scientific method and a predisposition to rapid learning of innovative methodologies and technologies, even in an interdisciplinary field.
The Master's degree graduate in Physics must be able to use his level of specialization in the application phase of his skills and in the ability to update his knowledge. The Master's Degree in Physics is also positioned as an intermediate stage in a training aimed at training for scientific research activities that take place in doctoral courses, providing the student with the skills to face basic and applied scientific research paths as well as the ability to design and develop physical methodologies appropriate for the different problems.
The training offer is structured in four semesters in which advanced and specialized knowledge is acquired in the field of Theoretical Physics, Modern Physics and Experimental Physics.
The progression of the didactic commitment required of students has been calibrated, in the different semesters, to facilitate the transition from three-year degrees. This reduces the possibility that, due to the prolongation of the time required to obtain the three-year degree, the student risks losing pace with the commitments required in the master's degree course.
Also, those related sectors of particular importance for the completion of specific training and the integration of transversal skills will be taken care of.
The Master's Degree course in Physics is organized into curricula that refer to the areas of Physics of Matter, Applied Physics and Nuclear Physics, which are those that prove to be the most qualified, from a research point of view, at the Messina headquarters.
- The curriculum referring to the Physics of Matter, taught in English, is called 'Condensed Matter Physics' and aims to prepare students not only for direct entry into the productive world of high technologies but also for access to the research sector in the academic field.
- The curriculum referring to Applied Physics, aimed at achieving solid preparation on the main technologies and methodologies of Physics applied in the field of Biophysics and Medical Physics, has specific contents aimed at access to the School of Specialization in Health Physics, also present in the University of Messina.
- The curriculum referring to Nuclear Physics is aimed at acquiring solid preparation on the main technologies and methodologies of Nuclear and Subnuclear Physics.
All knowledge is appropriately differentiated based on the different curricula, where the specific theoretical and experimental developments of the sector are addressed, including experimental and numerical calculation activities.
The last stage of the training course is dedicated to the preparation of a research thesis consistent with the chosen training path. During the thesis activity, developed in an original way by the student under the guidance of a supervisor, the student learns to consult and use scientific literature, to collect and critically analyze experimental data, to develop models for the calculation of physical quantities, to write and discuss scientific reports of high quality and complexity. The thesis work involves the student's personal contribution to an original research work which will allow him to develop a capacity for modeling and innovation, to acquire a propensity to examine every concept or situation in depth and a mental flexibility that constitute distinctive elements of the professional profile of a physicist, well known and appreciated even in non-academic fields.
Given the training objectives of the Three-Year Degree in Physics, the different curricula share some further objectives. In accordance with the qualifying training objectives of Class LM17 and according to the classification adopted in the European Qualifications Framework (Dublin Descriptors), the specific skills of the Master's Degree graduate in Physics are listed below.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding skills The training course allows to consolidate the knowledge of modern Physics and its applications in Physics of Matter, Applied Physics and Nuclear Physics, deepening knowledge of the most current topics in the chosen sector.
The degree course provides characterizing teachings for over 60 CFU which include advanced experimental, laboratory and computational techniques, and advanced theoretical subjects applied in different contexts.
A sufficient number of CFU are also planned between related/integrative courses and free-choice courses to acquire transversal skills and consolidate the interdisciplinary role played by Physics.
During the most specific curricular activities (laboratory and degree thesis), the student will be involved in the research topics active in the Department.
Individual exams and tests in progress, in written and/or oral form, will be the methods of verification used to assess the achievement of the expected results. Finally, the presence of courses taught in English provides an additional added value to the knowledge and understanding skills, especially for professional purposes in the academic and research sectors.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding At the end of the training course, the student acquires the ability to:
- Face problems in the fundamental areas of physics, and analyze the limits and validity of the approximations made in solving the problems.
- Apply knowledge in different contexts with full awareness of the interdisciplinary value of the theories and methods studied.
- Use advanced mathematical and computational tools with confidence for the modeling and investigation of systems or problems investigated.
- Set up experimental apparatus to carry out measurements to be used in scientific research, also extending to the use of specialized software.
- Work within research groups, even interdisciplinary, at an international level and at the same time be able to carry out projects independently to progress in frontier research in their sector.
- Identify and consult scientific literature profitably.
These skills are acquired through laboratory, theoretical and computational exercises as well as through the development of the thesis, under the guidance of the supervisor, with participation in the activities of a research group.
Individual exams, in written and/or oral form, presentation of case studies and practical tests will be the methods of verification used to assess the achievement of the expected results. III - Autonomy of judgment Master's degree graduates must:
- possess the appropriate skills that allow them to work with a high degree of autonomy, accepting the responsibilities of both the planning of projects and the management of structures;
- have reached an adequate level of ethical awareness in research and in the field of professional activities.
Training activities dedicated to achieving the expected results:
1) the student is encouraged in the formulation of autonomous judgments through the study and individual deepening also through the consultation of articles on the main scientific journals;
2) the activities of evaluation of the teaching.
Methods of verifying the result:
1) the tests and intermediate verification tests allow to express a correct evaluation of the developed critical attitudes, especially in relation to those issues that, although not explicitly addressed during the courses, can find a solution in terms of a mature critical awareness;
2) the choice and development of the topics relating to the final thesis allow to express a complete evaluation on the degree of autonomy of judgment achieved.
IV - Communication skills Master's degree graduates must:
- be able to work in a group, even interdisciplinary;
- know how to present the results of the research to make them understandable to a varied audience;
- master at least one foreign language with its technical terms.
Training activities dedicated to achieving the expected results:
1) free choice activities and acquisition of specific linguistic skills;
2) training activities carried out through tutoring and seminar activities.
Methods of verifying the result:
1) evaluation of the expository capacity both during the tests and during the presentation of the final paper;
2) evaluation of reports and papers, including the final thesis, which involve the elaboration and presentation of experimental and/or theoretical data, the use of advanced texts (also in English) together with the use of transversal knowledge acquired within the framework of the student's free choice activities.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN, ENGLISH V - Learning ability Master's degree graduates must:
- be able to carry out research using various sources, including specialized ones, recognizing the degree of reliability of the same;
- have the ability to independently understand topics that relate to areas different from their own;
- possess qualified skills to update on new developments and methods of physics and be able to express opinions on their possible application domains.
Training activities and support dedicated to achieving the expected results:
1) access to databases and electronic journals during the courses and during the final thesis;
2) courses for the development of more in-depth linguistic knowledge;
3) cycles of specialist seminars on current research topics.
Methods of verifying the result:
1) evaluation of the study method through written and oral exams;
2) evaluation of the learning of topics proposed for independent study in connection with specific evaluation tests in progress;
3) verification of the bibliographic congruence during the preparation of the final thesis.
Skills related to the function.
Physicists, Researchers and technicians with degrees in physics, Physics and nuclear technicians, Physics teachers.
During the course of study, the Master's degree graduate in Physics acquires the skills necessary to best perform their functions in the working context, mainly through:
- a detailed knowledge of current physics problems, specific to the chosen curriculum,
- the ability to analyze and schematize problems with models capable of a certain predictability,
- a versatility in the use of laboratory and/or computing tools, even relatively complex.
- the predisposition to teamwork and communication in English. Function in the working context.
Physicists, Researchers and technicians with degrees in physics, Physics and nuclear technicians, Physics teachers.
- Design, organization and management of research and/or laboratory activities, at an international level, with responsibility for the processes developed and the related results.
- Coordination and management of environments where complex equipment and machinery are present, in the sectors of industry, environment, health, cultural heritage and public administration.
- Organization, management and analysis of data within a wide range of basic scientific processes;
- Teaching, training, promotion and dissemination of scientific and technological culture.

UNIVERSITY: University of Messina
COURSE: [9236] BIOLOGY OF HEALTH, APPLIED TECHNOLOGIES AND NUTRITION
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unime.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10513

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Degree program: 2020. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Master's. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: LM-6 - Class of Master's Degrees in Biology. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: MESSINA. Department: DEPARTMENT OF CHEMICAL, BIOLOGICAL, PHARMACEUTICAL AND ENVIRONMENTAL SCIENCES. Website: https://biologia-salute-tecnologie-applicate-e-nutrizione.cdl.unime.it. Admission requirements. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+TSS] Bachelor's Degree, [L1+TSS] Bachelor's Degree, [TS] Foreign qualification, [TS+TSS] Foreign qualification, [TSS+LS] High school diploma, Bachelor's Degree. Roles: PRESIDENT ANGELA MARINO, COORDINATOR ANGELA MARINO. Program, texts and objectives. Professional status conferred by the degree. BIOLOGIST. The Biology degree program prepares graduates who are able to carry out professional activities recognized by current regulations as the skills of the professional figure of the biologist (Section A of the professional register) and in all the specific fields of application as reported in paragraph 1 of Article 31 of the DPR 328 of June 5, 2001, (supplement to the Official Gazette 190 of June 17, 2001) and in Law 396 of May 24, 1967 on the organization of the profession of biologist.
The main employment opportunities provided by the Master's Degree Course in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition are those set out below, which, however, do not exhaust the picture of the potential labor market, and refer to:
-Professional activity in public and private laboratories for biological, pharmacological, toxicological, biochemical, microbiological, genetic and cytological analysis.
-Activities aimed at scientific and technological innovation, as well as management and design of molecular technologies; to bioinformatics methodologies; to the integrated knowledge of plant organisms, microorganisms, the environment; to the study and understanding of biological phenomena at the molecular and cellular level.
-Autonomous professional activities in the fields of nutrition and food safety; to biological and biochemical applications in the industrial field. Master's graduates who meet the requirements set out in current regulations may also participate in the entrance tests for training courses for teaching staff for secondary schools of the first and second cycle. The Master's graduate may also:
- access, after passing a competition, to doctoral research courses
- access, after passing a competition, to specialist schools. Characteristics of the final exam.
The final exam involves a period of experimental activity relating to topics consistent with the educational path of the Master's degree course, to be carried out in a university laboratory or in an external public or private body in agreement with the University. The final exam concludes with a discussion in public session of the experimental thesis, which makes an original contribution, and drawn up under the guidance of a supervising lecturer. The final report contains the results obtained during the experimental activity. This activity contributes to the training of the biologist, completing the knowledge acquired during the course of study with the aim of developing critical skills in the analysis and evaluation of experimental data, as well as the ability in the experimental practices relating to biological research applied to the bio-health, nutritional, technological-molecular and microbiological field.
Knowledge required for access.
Given that the Master's Degree Course in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition is not a limited-number course, in order to access the Degree Course, the student must demonstrate that he/she has acquired the knowledge typical of the three-year degree in class L-13 (or class 12 ex D.M. 509) or, if coming from other degree classes, that he/she has the curricular requirements determined by having acquired a total of at least 60 CFU in the scientific-disciplinary sectors (SSD) BIO/01, BIO/02; BIO/04; BIO/05; BIO/06; BIO/07; BIO/09; BIO/10; BIO/11; BIO/18; BIO/19; and in the sectors - FIS/01-08, MAT/01-09: at least CFU at least 12; - CHIM/01-03 and CHIM/06: CFU at least 12. Access to the Degree Course takes place through an interview. The adequacy of the candidates' preparation is verified by a special commission appointed annually, made up of lecturers of the Degree Course. The verification is considered passed for students with a three-year degree in class L-13 (or class 12 ex D.M. 509) according to the methods set out in the University's Teaching Regulations.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Course in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition aims to prepare graduates who, in addition to having a solid cultural background in basic biology in all its aspects, and in the various sectors of applied biology, also have the knowledge relating to the health field, to the biology of food and nutrition, to molecular technologies and to applied microbiology.
The course provides for a common path in the first year, in which students are given the fundamental knowledge and skills common to the areas of learning considered. In the second year, a division into curricula is planned, in which specific professional knowledge relating to specific sectors and linked to the reference areas will be developed. The specific training objectives of the course consist in offering the student the opportunity to acquire skills in the areas:
-Biological-health (diagnostic and analytical, laboratory skills typical of the bio-medical and health sector)
-Technological molecular and Microbiological (application of genetic and molecular methodologies; use of microbial agents in the technological application field, etc.)
-Biological-nutritional (quality of food, physiology of nutrition, functional foods and biochemical processes)
The training path provides the conceptual and technical tools to allow graduates to acquire advanced knowledge and the theoretical-practical preparation necessary to operate at all levels in the application and scientific activities relating to biomedical, cellular, pharmacological, environmental and applied microbiology technologies.
The Master's graduate in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition acquires skills that provide access to different employment opportunities, including: application of genetic and molecular methodologies; identification of microbial agents; biological, pharmacological, cytotoxic and microbiological analyses; quality control in products of biological origin; environmental impact assessment.
The teaching will be structured in lectures and practical exercises, seminars and laboratory activities. In addition, part of the student's commitment, distributed over the two years of the course, is focused on carrying out the internship and the final exam, which involves original research on the topics of biology, in a basic or applied field. During the thesis work, the graduate student joins a research group, actively participating in the experimental work in a laboratory; 4 CFU of linguistic skills (English) are also planned as further linguistic knowledge.
The Master's Course in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition, based on the knowledge acquired, can hold positions of responsibility both in the public and private sectors; have the cultural basis to access (after passing a competition) to Biosanitary Specialization Schools recognized by the Ministry of Health (e.g. Clinical Pathology, Clinical Pharmacology, Clinical Biochemistry, Microbiology), necessary for a managerial career in the laboratory and biosanitary field, both public and private, to Doctoral Research Courses in the biomedical field (e.g. Applied Biology and Experimental Medicine, Experimental and Molecular Pathology, Molecular Medicine) and to Second Level Masters.
For the training purposes that characterize the course of study, reference is made to the principles of European harmonization which encourage the correspondence of the skills of graduates in the Degree Course, thus responding to the specific requirements identified by the system of the Dublin Descriptors according to the Tuning Table prepared at national level (CBUI College) for the LM-6 class.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding. Master's graduates in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition will acquire fundamental knowledge of the physiology of the organs and the nervous system, cutting-edge knowledge in the physiopathological and pathological, microbiological and pharmacological disciplines that broaden the learning of information relating to the human field. They will deepen specific skills within the curricula relating to biomedical molecular, microbiological and human nutrition technologies. They will know the functional anatomy useful for understanding the physiology of nutrition, the basic elements relating to the biochemical and nutritional role of macro and micronutrients, the changes related to the processes of transformation and preservation of food and the molecular and cellular bases of the main metabolic disorders. Knowledge and understanding skills will be achieved through the common activities provided in the various teaching courses, such as lectures, laboratories, participation in seminars, methodological discussion of scientific articles, which allow the individual execution of experimental protocols as well as personal study. In addition, they will be able to:
- select available information and evaluate its reliability for the purpose of continuous updating of knowledge;
- prepare and present written and/or oral reports during the courses (in-course tests) in Italian or possibly also in English;
The achievement of the learning outcomes is verified mainly through oral and/or written exams, in-course tests, reports on laboratory activities.
The methodological choices for the delivery of teaching, the comparison with the teachers during the lectures and laboratory work, as well as the internship activity, will provide the student with the possibility of increasing their knowledge and developing their ability to understand.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. Master's graduates in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition will have the ability to face and solve problems in the field of biomedical research, technological-microbiological and nutritional research thanks to the mastery of the use of a multidisciplinary approach acquired during the training course.
The graduates will acquire laboratory skills typical of the bio-medical, health and pharmacological sector through the training activities of the Biological-health area; skills on genetic and molecular methodologies, on environmental metabolomics, the use of microbial agents in the technological application and nanotechnological field through the training activities of the Technological molecular and Microbiological area; skills on the quality of food, on the physiology of nutrition on functional foods and biochemical processes through the training activities of the Biological-nutritional area. The results in terms of ability to apply knowledge and understanding will be achieved mainly through the laboratory activities and practical exercises during which the student will carry out the activity individually or according to group dynamics. In particular, group work will stimulate the ability to work synergistically and the sense of personal and collective responsibility.
The student's commitment provides for each teaching course CFU dedicated to laboratory exercises as well as the carrying out of the internship and the final exam, which consists of original research on the topics of biology, in a basic or applied field.
The application of knowledge and understanding will also be supported by:
-use of tools necessary for accessing and using scientific literature in English and databases i.e. genomic, molecular and structural, etc.;
-active participation, as a speaker, in seminar activities, journal clubs in order to develop autonomy of study and management of the development of technologies and their applications in the biomedical, diagnostic, microbiological, physiological, pharmacological and nutritional fields;
The Master's graduate will also develop the professional skills required for entering the world of work. In particular, he/she will be able to apply laboratory techniques, microbiological and, thanks to the acquisition of the fundamental notions for critical evaluation, will be able to interpret, as far as he/she is competent, the results of the main diagnostic investigations; he/she will acquire the tools for the analysis of biochemical and metabolic data in vivo; he/she will have knowledge of the fundamental principles that regulate drug development and will be able to plan projects for the development of innovative drugs. He/she will have basic knowledge of statistical and bioinformatics techniques applied to biology. He/she will have knowledge of the control and quality certification of food and advanced molecular methodologies.
These skills will be acquired through the theoretical-practical common training activities in the first year, and specifically in the curricular paths planned in the second year, as well as through activities related to the preparation of the degree thesis. The student will be provided with the tools to face the various stages of the experimentation and to discuss and independently rework the results obtained. The objective of bringing the student closer to the world of work will also be pursued by encouraging periods of activity for the preparation of the experimental thesis at external bodies and structures representative of the areas of interest for the employment of new graduates.
The results obtained will be verified with the profit exams, with the interview for verifying the activity carried out during the internship and with the final exam.
III - Autonomy of judgment. Autonomy of judgment will be acquired through the responsibility of projects, in particular with the design of the experimental master's thesis; with a careful evaluation of scientific data in the literature; with the knowledge of safety regulations in the laboratory (safety data sheets, regulations and legislation); The achievement of the specific training objectives for each teaching unit will be verified, as well as through the planned tests and exams, reports and final exam, also through the student's participation in group activities, such as journal clubs, scientific reports and thematic debates on current issues.
IV - Communication skills. The Master's graduate will have acquired mastery in the approach and in-depth study through bibliographic research and on databases of topics in the biomedical, microbiological, pharmacological, technological applied, physiological and nutritional field. He/she will develop the ability to read, understand and share in a group the contents of scientific works relating to topics relevant to the aforementioned disciplines. He/she will learn to present scientific content in a clear and unambiguous way, to report on the data acquired during the carrying out of laboratory experiments, to plan experimental activities, to propose any updates on the initially presented proposals, to interact with the other members of the working group with a sense of responsibility and determination in achieving the set objectives.
The achievement of these objectives will be achieved and verified through:
1) Active participation (as a speaker) in seminar activities, journal clubs, etc.;
2) Preparation, drafting and presentation of written and/or oral reports during the courses in Italian or possibly also in English;
3) Presentation of partial data acquired during the preparation of the final experimental report;
4) Exams;
5) Final exam, where the student is required to acquire expository and communication skills and adequate language skills. The drafting of the final report (master's thesis) in a European language other than Italian may be allowed, upon request by the student.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN V - Learning ability. The verification of the achievement of learning skills is the subject of the various tests provided for in the training course as well as in the context of the internship and the final exam.
In particular, the graduate in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition will be able to:
1) work independently and update adequately in relation to the development of innovative scientific technologies in Biology;
2) develop experimental strategies and research projects relating to his/her field;
3) critically evaluate the results of the experimental activities carried out;
4) critically evaluate the scientific results produced by research groups published in scientific journals or presented at national and international symposia, etc.
Skills related to the function.
BIOLOGIST. The Biology degree program prepares graduates who are able to carry out professional activities recognized by current regulations as the skills of the professional figure of the biologist (Section A of the professional register) and in all the specific fields of application as reported in paragraph 1 of Article 31 of the DPR 328 of June 5, 2001, (supplement to the Official Gazette 190 of June 17, 2001) and in Law 396 of May 24, 1967 on the organization of the profession of biologist.
The main technical skills acquired relate to the ability to carry out independently analyses: quantitative and qualitative analysis of biological macromolecules, cultivation and manipulation of eukaryotic and prokaryotic cells, bioinformatics analyses, morphological and functional analyses in biological systems under physiological and pathological conditions. Integrated skills in the biomedical, technological-molecular, biodiversity and nutritional sectors;
The Master's graduate in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition will be able to apply the acquired skills in a flexible way in the various working contexts. Function in the working context.
BIOLOGIST. The Biology degree program prepares graduates who are able to carry out professional activities recognized by current regulations as the skills of the professional figure of the biologist (Section A of the professional register) and in all the specific fields of application as reported in paragraph 1 of Article 31 of the DPR 328 of June 5, 2001, (supplement to the Official Gazette 190 of June 17, 2001) and in Law 396 of May 24, 1967 on the organization of the profession of biologist.
The Master's Degree Course in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition prepares graduates who are able to carry out professional activities recognized by current regulations as the skills of the professional figure of the biologist (Section A of the professional register) and in all the specific fields of application as reported in paragraph 1 of Article 31 of the DPR 328 of June 5, 2001, (supplement to the Official Gazette 190 of June 17, 2001) and in Law 396 of May 24, 1967 on the organization of the profession of biologist. Enrollment in the Register for the profession of biologist, section A, is subject to passing the relevant state examination. The Master's graduate in Biology of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition will be able to apply theoretical multidisciplinary skills in research activities, both basic and applied, and in production or service activities. In particular, in the biomedical and neurobiological, technological-molecular, microbiological and nutritional sectors. He/she will be able to apply the knowledge in professional activities that involve the use of advanced methodologies in the fields of Health, Applied Technologies and Nutrition. He/she will have prospects of employment with responsibilities in the following sectors: basic and applied research in biology; professional and/or consulting activities in public and private bodies; activities in diagnostics in the genetic, microbiological, histopathological, biochemical, cellular, biomolecular and nutritional fields in laboratories and Hygiene and Public Health Units, Prevention Bodies, in the Pharmaceutical Industry and in University research; activities in specialized scientific communication and in the dissemination of scientific and technological innovation.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Data Architect: The Data Architect can work within companies operating in various sectors, service companies, consulting firms, research centers, public and private administrations, or as a freelancer.
Data Scientist: The Data Scientist can work within companies operating in various sectors, service companies, consulting firms, research centers, public and private administrations, or as a freelancer.
Market Analyst: The Market Analyst can work within companies operating in various sectors, service companies, consulting firms, research centers, or as a freelancer.
Data Engineer: The Data Engineer can work within companies operating in various sectors, service companies, consulting firms, research centers, public and private administrations, or as a freelancer.
Database Manager: The Database Manager can work within companies operating in various sectors, service companies, consulting firms, public and private administrations, or in research centers.
Data Steward: The Data Steward can work within companies operating in various sectors, service companies, consulting firms, public and private administrations, or in research centers.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The purpose of the thesis is to develop an original study that culminates in a theoretical and/or practical/design result connected to specific problems related to the processing and analysis of data, even in different application contexts, or a reasoned review of the state of the art in a specific area of Data Science.
The study must be agreed upon and reviewed by a supervising lecturer, and possibly a co-supervisor with expertise in the topics covered.
The final exam for obtaining the degree consists of a public discussion of a written final paper and an oral presentation, with multimedia tools, of one's work before an examining board, which can verify the achievement of the qualifying objectives of this Degree Course, through the cultural maturity and the candidate's ability to elaborate, as well as the quality of the work carried out.
Knowledge required for admission.
To attend the Course successfully, the student must have a good background in computer science and statistical-mathematical disciplines. In particular, he/she must have acquired good knowledge of continuous and discrete mathematics, the ability to analyze and describe an algorithm specifying the data structures and controls related to it, the ability to use a programming language, and knowledge of the principles of operation of computer networks.
In particular, the following are required for admission to the Course:
- possession of a three-year degree in class L-8 or L-31 (DM 270/04) obtained throughout the national territory or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, which guarantees direct access to the Master's Degree Course;
or
- possession of a three-year degree or university diploma or equivalent qualification of three years' duration, obtained throughout the national territory, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, and a minimum number of credits equal to 24 in the SSD INF/01 and/or ING-INF/05 and 12 credits in the statistical-mathematical sectors (MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/08, MAT/09, SECS-S/06, SECS-S/01 or SECS-S/02).
Knowledge of the English language is a necessary condition for profitable attendance of the course. In particular, it is required to have knowledge of English at a level equal to B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course in Data Science is aimed at all graduates who are interested in extending the knowledge and skills acquired in their university education towards the statistical techniques and information technologies useful for selecting, processing, and analyzing large amounts of data, in order to extract added value for decision-making processes in various application areas.
The LM-Data Course has the following training objectives:
a) develop an adequate set of skills and approaches oriented to Problem Solving through the techniques and methodologies learned to formulate, even in an innovative way, qualitative and quantitative answers to complex problems involving the processing and analysis of data;
b) provide multidisciplinary training that includes both specific technical-scientific knowledge and knowledge of the business, legal and/or social context in the field of data processing and analysis;
c) deepen the principles of the Data Economy, Data-driven Business and Business Intelligence to analyze complex macro and micro-economic phenomena that influence political, economic and social decisions and business strategies;
d) provide basic knowledge to combine mathematical-statistical and computer technologies and methodologies with specific methods and techniques to adapt them to the reference application context;
e) develop the ability to effectively support change and technological and organizational innovation in companies and public and private bodies, in particular in the aspects involving the use and processing of large amounts of data, taking into account constraints related to security and privacy;
f) train a new generation of scientific and cultural profiles that respond to the emerging needs of Digital Transformation of all organizations, public and private, that aspire to high competitiveness in their reference sector and in a globalized context.
The training course is characterized by a strong multidisciplinary vocation to provide the transversal skills required for a Data Science professional, which include the ability to communicate, problem-solving, technological innovation, as well as the ability to understand and anticipate ethical and privacy issues, to govern the data lifecycle, to manage quality, in order to prevent the misuse of data or analytical results.
The achievement of the training objectives of the Master's Degree Course in Data Science is based on the following thematic areas:
1. Mathematical-statistical area: aimed at defining a basic scientific and cultural background, transversal to the different training paths, as it is necessary to train the professional figures capable of analyzing, interpreting and modeling statistical data.
2. Computer science area: oriented to the knowledge of tools for modeling, design, organization, programming and administration of data and information systems, with reference to taxonomies and ontologies for the representation of data, the processes that include the entire data lifecycle, complex algorithms for the management and processing of the same, security in the processing of data and processes.
3. Information technology area: for the design, implementation and administration of information platforms oriented to the management, representation and processing of data, through Big Data Analytics and Machine Learning techniques, in a Cloud/Edge environment, oriented to High Performance Computing systems.
4. Legal-business area, for the planning of complex information processes with a high impact on business systems, in compliance with the rules on data processing and privacy.
The training offer is structured over two years, the courses are taught in English, and provides for the acquisition of 120 CFU, according to current national regulations, for the purpose of obtaining the Master's degree in Data Science.
The Course of Studies is divided into training paths, which aim to address a greater deepening of specific skills in the field of Data Science.
In particular, the training offer of the first year is common to all training paths and is organized in such a way as to provide the basic tools for the visualization and systematic analysis of data, which include statistical analysis techniques, modeling and formalization of complex algorithms. The issues related to data processing will also be considered from both legal and IT security perspectives.
The courses of the second year will be aimed at a specific professionalization of the student, with characterizing subjects and disciplines that allow the student to develop skills that can be used in different application contexts. The Data Science student will be able to face and analyze the problems related to an application context using the appropriate investigation and data characterization methods and processes. He/She will therefore be able, at the end of the study path, to develop solutions for the management of complex information systems, adopting and/or adapting the technologies available to the specific domain needs.
In particular, the following study paths have been identified:
- ICT (Information and Communication Technology) area: the path investigates the latest information technology and technologies such as Software Defined Network, Cloud, Edge Computing and the Internet of Things;
- economic area: the path deepens the principles of the Data Economy, Data-driven Business and Business Intelligence;
- forensic area: the path investigates the application of Data Science to improve the effectiveness and efficiency of anti-fraud, intelligence and anti-crime systems, forensic, fakeness, data governance and digital sovereignty.
The use of specific teaching and verification methodologies is planned for the development of transversal skills necessary for students to face working and professional contexts after the study path. In particular, the Data Science student will develop the ability to work in a group, to operate with defined degrees of autonomy and to quickly integrate into working environments.
External activities are planned, such as training internships at companies, public bodies, laboratories and study stays at other Italian, European and extra-European universities, useful for achieving the training objectives and for subsequent insertion into the labor market.
Dublin Descriptors: I - Knowledge and understanding At the end of the Master's Degree Course in Data Science, the student will have acquired, through the study in the characterizing and related courses and the resulting verifications:
a) a preparation that includes both specific technical knowledge and knowledge of the business, legal and/or social context;
b) knowledge of mathematical-statistical and computer technologies and methodologies with specific methods and techniques of the area in which they will operate;
c) understanding of the scientific methodology for the processing of large data sets.
The methods and tools used to achieve and verify the expected results include the profit exams, which can be oral and/or written and/or practical, and take place at the end of each training activity, according to methods established by the individual teachers. The courses include both a theoretical part and an exercise and/or laboratory part in which, through practical activities, it is possible to constantly verify the level of learning of the students and any critical issues found.
II - Ability to apply knowledge and understanding The student who graduates in Data Science is able to apply the acquired knowledge to:
a) use the techniques and methodologies learned to formulate, even in an innovative way, qualitative and quantitative answers to complex problems or that require an interdisciplinary approach, taking into account the ethical/legal issues in data processing;
b) effectively support change and technological and organizational innovation in companies and public and private bodies, in particular in the aspects involving the use and processing of large amounts of data, numerical or textual.
This allows the graduate to quickly integrate into the working environment, making him/her capable of collaborating within working groups.
The methods and tools used to achieve and verify the expected results include the exercise and laboratory activities provided in most of the courses offered. It will therefore be possible to constantly verify the ability to apply theoretical knowledge to real cases. In addition, students are given problem-solving exercises to verify and stimulate their ability to deal with complex problems and to work independently. The profit exams may also include the realization and presentation of projects in which students put into practice the knowledge and skills acquired during the course.
III - Autonomy of judgment Graduates in Data Science will be able to:
- build and develop logical arguments with a clear identification of assumptions and conclusions;
- recognize methodological and technological solutions suitable to support concrete problems arising from multidisciplinary application contexts;
- face the experience of autonomous and group work, making original and autonomous contributions.
The methods and tools used to achieve and verify the expected results include:
- the laboratory activities, the test exams, the intermediate verifications and the realization of specific projects that allow to express a correct evaluation of the critical attitudes developed, especially in relation to topics not explicitly addressed during the lessons;
- the carrying out of the degree thesis which allows to evaluate how the student faces in a critical way the development of the topics related to the final thesis.
IV - Communication skills Part of the training course is aimed at the ability to communicate and to interact profitably in order to expose the various discussion topics. To this end, participation in meetings with companies in the sector is encouraged, which helps students to face future job interviews. The specific knowledge acquired during the degree course also allows for high-level professional interactions.
Graduates in Data Science will be able to:
- present data analysis problems and solutions to a specialized or general audience;
- interact professionally with experts from other sectors, for example in project groups. They must also have acquired skills in the use of technological tools useful for communication, also through the creation of multimedia or documentary products, independently prepared.
The methods and tools used to achieve and verify the expected results include the evaluation of the expository ability, during the discussion of specific projects, the test exams and the presentation of the final paper.
Language/s of instruction and assessment.
ENGLISH V - Learning ability One of the problems addressed during the training course is that related to the understanding of text and language.
During the degree course, the ability to learn and to correctly expose what has been studied is increased.
At the end of the training course, graduates in Data Science will be able to integrate and adapt quickly to the demands of the working environment, open to new problems.
These skills are provided by the entire training course, complete from the point of view of the contents, and by the internship/stage activities.
The methods and tools used to achieve and verify the expected results include:
- the evaluation of the study method through written and oral exams;
- the evaluation of the learning of the proposed topics in the realization of specific projects;
- the verification of the bibliographic congruence during the preparation of the degree thesis.
Skills related to the function.
Data Architect: Advanced technical skills in languages for querying databases, ability to analyze and synthesize, creative visualization and problem-solving skills.
Data Scientist: Highly specialized figure, who has in-depth knowledge of mathematical-statistical models and algorithms for data analysis (especially related to Machine Learning). Knows more than one programming language and tools for analysis and representation of complex analyses. Must have skills in business intelligence, semantics, ontologies for the management of information, methods and technologies for the management of innovative data-driven projects.
Market Analyst: The Market Analyst must have skills in economic/business/legal theory, econometrics, statistics and computer science to process big data for a comprehensive analysis of various phenomena.
Data Engineer: The Data Engineer must have skills in the design of Databases, a solid mastery of coding languages and tools for IT security. They must also possess solid problem-solving and mathematical skills. The soft skills include excellent written and verbal communication skills, as well as the ability to work both independently and in a team.
Database Manager: Knowledge of database software to organize both structured and partially or unstructured data, technical knowledge of computer languages and operating systems, IT security techniques and data encryption for the secure management of data and the implementation of privacy-oriented policies.
Data Steward: Knowledge of theories, principles and practices on databases, data modeling, technical knowledge of programming languages, operating systems, regulations and policies and their application to data.
Function in a work context.
Data Architect: The Data Architect designs the information flows within business processes, identifying the reference requirements for the design of company information systems. He/She uses the technical skills on computer languages oriented to data to plan architectural strategies for each thematic area of the business model.
Data Scientist: The work of the Data Scientist consists in extracting valuable information for the various business needs: business, marketing and sales strategies, definition of new products and services, etc. This professional develops and implements mathematical-statistical models to be applied on Big Data in order to try to answer complex questions related to the specific application and analysis domain.
Market Analyst: The Market Analyst is able to carry out the analysis of economic/business problems using methods for managing large amounts of data. He/She may analyze complex macro and micro-economic phenomena that influence political, economic and social decisions and business strategies using specific information techniques in compliance with the rules on the processing of personal data.
Data Engineer: The Data Engineer identifies the best technological and IT solutions to aggregate, analyze and manipulate large amounts of Big Data. This is aimed at improving the accessibility of data and the design of reports, dashboards and tools for end users, ensuring the availability, quality and usability of data for those who use it, also taking into account issues related to IT security.
Database Manager: The Database Manager is responsible for the organization and management of databases, examines business requests for the use of data, evaluates data sources to improve their structured or unstructured organization, and helps to design and install the storage hardware. He/She must keep up with technological developments and business needs, in order to actively contribute to its growth and efficiency.
Data Steward: The Data Steward takes care of the management of content and related metadata in terms of quality and consistency so that anyone who uses it is sure to use data that is consistent with its meaning, complete and reliable in its quality. This is done in compliance with the regulations in force, as well as the rules defined within the company for access to data. The Data Steward is responsible for the Quality of Data and its usability at the company level, with the task of making them available to anyone who needs to develop queries, analyses, transactional applications.
